### S19. `Strategy3`'s mirror equivalence to Strategy 1 is structural, not tick-exact — and unmeasured

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`Strategy3`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/Strategy3.java) + [`RuleEngine.decideBuyEntry`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/rules/RuleEngine.java), added 2026-09-04 on the user's request: *"strategy 3 … does buy instead of sell … rule sets are same as that of strategy 1 just inverse the signal … if there is a PE sell signal we will make it CE buy"*. Documented in [STRATEGIES.md](STRATEGIES.md#strategy-3--the-inverted-baseline-buy-side). |
| Why | The request pairs a signal on one leg (PE cross-down SELL) with a trade on the *other* leg (CE BUY). The architecture scans and trades only the config's own `trading_side`, so the build realises the pairing by mirroring the detection onto the traded leg's own series: CE cross-**up** + `isSmaNNUpTrending` → BUY. That fires on the same market move as the PE sell, but not necessarily on the same bar — the two legs have different premiums, different SMA levels, and independently-stamped trend flags (with `maxDeviations = 0`, one flat CE bar kills `UpTrending` for the CE's whole session while the PE's `DownTrending` survives). The alternative — exact-tick coupling, where the CE config literally consumes the PE config's signal — was **not** built: it would cross config cache boundaries, contradict the `(trading_side ↔ option type)` ledger invariant restored by [S2](#s2-strategy1-scanned-every-configs-legs-not-its-own--resolved-2026-08-25), and need a strike-mapping convention (same strike? mirrored moneyness?) that has no `TradeConfig` field yet. |
| Impact | **Unquantified.** Two measurements wanted: (a) replay a window with a PE config on strategy 1 and its CE sibling on strategy 3, and join the two signal streams by session — how often does the CE buy have a matching PE sell within 0 / 1 / 3 bars, and how often does either fire alone; (b) the strategy's own ledger P&L over the same window, since long-option economics (theta against the position, SL/target geometry inverted) differ from the short side even when timing matches perfectly. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing until measured. If (a) shows the mirror diverging materially, the exact-tick design above is the fallback to propose — as its own entry, with the strike-mapping question put to the user per CLAUDE.md #9. |
| Effort | **S** for measurement (a) — signal-stream join over one replayed month; **M** for (b) with a written verdict. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-04 at build time; the strategy ships with this question attached._ |

---

### S20. `Strategy4` buys the falling option — the fade is specified but unmeasured

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`Strategy4`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/Strategy4.java) + the `mapAction` seam in [`AbstractSmaCrossStrategy`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/AbstractSmaCrossStrategy.java), added 2026-09-04 on the user's request: *"execute strategy 1, whenever we get sell signal instead of sell we will do buy … do not change the rule set of sell … rest remains same — profit, sl, stop loss move etc"*. Built exactly as specified: detection is byte-identical to Strategy 1 (a `1,4`-tagged config detects on the same ticks under both), only the emitted action inverts. Documented in [STRATEGIES.md](STRATEGIES.md#strategy-4--strategy-1s-detection-inverted-at-execution). |
| Why | The trade this opens is a long in an option whose premium just crossed **down** through its SMA with the whole-day down-trend flag set — long against the detected momentum, with theta also against the position (a long needs the premium to *rise* to profit, and the same absolute `target` is now above entry instead of below). It may be a deliberate mean-reversion fade and it may work; nothing about it is measured. Note the bracket numbers were tuned on the short side: the S4b measurement showed the ladder/ceiling geometry interacting with *falling*-premium trades, and none of that transfers by symmetry. Also worth knowing when reading a `1,4` ledger: the two strategies open opposite directions off one bar, so their rows pair up with identical `entry_time` and mirrored `entry_direction` — expected, not a duplication bug. |
| Impact | **Unquantified.** The cheap first measurement: tag one window's configs `1,4` (with a `transaction_type = BUY` clone for the 4 side — one config row cannot carry both directions, so it is a cloned config pair, same legs and numbers), replay, and compare the two strategies' ledgers row by row — same entries by construction, opposite P&L before brackets, diverging after brackets since SL/target/trailing trigger at different times per side. A three-way window against strategy 3 (mirror-detection long) on the same dates would also answer which inversion the user actually wants to keep. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing to fix — the build matches the specification. This entry exists so the fade ships measured rather than assumed; per working rule 3, any behaviour change that measurement suggests goes back to the user first. |
| Effort | **S** for the paired replay; the config cloning is the only fiddly part. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-04 at build time, alongside [S19](#s19-strategy3s-mirror-equivalence-to-strategy-1-is-structural-not-tick-exact--and-unmeasured)._ |

### S21. Entry detection reads one strike's premium, which steps as spot walks the strike grid

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`SMAIndicatorImpl`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/indicator/SMAIndicatorImpl.java) (SMA over candle **lows**, per option token) feeding the cross predicates in [`strategy/rules/`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/rules/) via the per-leg series `AnalysisScheduler` writes into `SharedData.strikeMarketDataByInstrumentAndInterval`. Filed 2026-09-04 while adding the dashboard's averaged ATM±1 / ATM±2 panes ([CHART_DASHBOARD.md](CHART_DASHBOARD.md#averaged-strike-ladders-atmn-panes)) — the panes exist because the user wanted the ladder average visible next to the single strike. |
| Why | Every entry decision is a cross of one contract's premium against its own SMA. A single strike's premium carries a component that is not about direction at all: as spot walks across the strike grid the same contract slides between ITM, ATM and OTM, and its delta — and so its sensitivity to the next point of spot — changes underneath a series the indicator treats as homogeneous. The mean of a ladder straddling the money does not have that component, which is the whole reason the new panes were asked for. **Nothing here says the strategy is wrong.** The single-strike series is also the thing actually traded, and an average of five contracts is not an instrument — a signal off it would still have to be executed on one leg. The open question is narrower: *does the averaged series cross at materially different bars than the traded leg does?* If it does not, the noise is cosmetic and this entry closes. |
| Impact | **Unquantified — and deliberately not guessed.** The measurement does not need a code change to start: for one replayed window, pull the traded leg's 5-minute series and the ATM±1 / ATM±2 averages for the same expiry from `historical_option_candles`, compute `SMA(low)` on each at the config's period, and count bars where the cross direction disagrees. That is a read-only script over data already imported — the chart panes render exactly these three series, so a first look is a page load, not a run. Only if the disagreement rate is non-trivial does the expensive version follow: a paired backtest, one arm gating on the averaged series, on the same window, per working rule 3. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing to fix and **nothing to change without the user** — this is a live entry rule. If the disagreement turns out to matter, the shape would be a config-driven choice of gating series (single strike, as today, vs an averaged ladder of configurable width), never a hardcoded switch: per Rule 9 the width would be a `TradeConfig` column, and today there is no such field, so it has to be asked for rather than assumed. Note the averaged series would also need the all-legs rule the chart uses — averaging whichever legs happen to be present steps the level and manufactures crossings, which would be the exact opposite of the point. |
| Effort | **S** for the disagreement count (read-only, existing data). **M+** for anything beyond it — a new config column, a second gating path, and a before/after pair. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-04 at build time of the averaged chart panes. Not blocking anything; the cheap measurement is worth doing before the expensive question is even asked._ |

### S22. Pressure's "VWAP" term is a session typical-price mean — and was briefly implemented as something else

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`SessionAnchoredPrice`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/indicator/series/SessionAnchoredPrice.java), selected by `app.pressure.anchor-mode` in [`SpotFeatureCache`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/pressure/SpotFeatureCache.java). Consumed as one of the four confirming terms in [`PressureScore`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/pressure/PressureScore.java). Filed 2026-09-05; **rewritten the same day** after the strategy's author corrected the premise. |
| What happened | The written spec says *"Session VWAP from 09:15, typical price (H+L+C)/3, expanding within the day"*. That was read literally. NIFTY is an index and has no traded volume — 19,572 of the 19,602 `historical_spot_candles` rows for 2024 carry `volume = 0` — so rather than ask, the first implementation **invented a substitute**: it weighted each bar by CE+PE volume summed across the front weekly expiry. The author then clarified (2026-09-05): *"The word VWAP in Pressure = not real VWAP. I should have called it session typical-price mean… No volume in the formula."* The reference book of 1,560 tickets was produced with an unweighted expanding mean of HLC typical price, exactly what this codebase had written only as a degenerate fallback. |
| Why it mattered | This is not a naming quibble. `close vs anchor` is one of four confirming terms against a 3-of-4 threshold, so a bar where the weighted and unweighted series sit on opposite sides of `close` **flips the signal outright**. Every figure from the affected build was therefore measuring a different strategy, and nothing in the logs, the summary table or the trade CSV would have looked wrong. The general lesson is the one working rule 2 already states in a different form: an input that cannot be computed from the available data is a **question for the author**, not an invitation to model a substitute. |
| Resolution | `TYPICAL_MEAN` is now the default and reproduces the reference formula. Pinned by [`SessionAnchoredPriceTest`](../src/test/java/com/moneymaker/indicator/series/SessionAnchoredPriceTest.java), whose first assertion exists specifically to fail if a weight is reintroduced under the banner of "fixing the VWAP". The class was renamed from `SessionVwap` so the code stops asserting something untrue. |
| Still open — the real-volume experiment | The author proposed the honest version of the original idea: a genuine volume-weighted price built from the **option tape** (ATM ±2 strikes, both rights) rather than from a spot volume column that does not exist. That is available today as `app.pressure.anchor-mode=OPTION_TAPE_VWAP`, but the current weighting is the whole front-weekly chain, not an ATM ±2 band, so the mode as shipped is **not** the proposed experiment. |
| Impact | **Unquantified, deliberately.** Two separate unknowns and they must not be conflated: **(a)** how much the mis-implemented weighting moved the January slice — measurable now by re-running the same window under both modes and diffing the entry set; **(b)** whether an ATM ±2 option-tape VWAP is *better* than the typical-price mean, which is a strategy question, not a bug. (b) requires the reference book to be re-marked against it before any comparison means anything — the author has offered to do that if asked. |
| Fix sketch | For (b), narrow the weight query from the whole chain to ATM ±2 strikes both rights, which needs the ATM per bar and therefore the spot close — an input `volumeWeights` does not currently take. Keep it opt-in; do not make it the default without a paired before/after per working rule 3, and not without the re-marked reference. |
| Effort | **S** for (a). **M** for (b): a reshaped query plus a paired run plus a re-marked reference book. |
| Priority | _Resolved for correctness (the default now matches the reference). Open only as the (b) experiment, which is the author's call to commission._ |

### S23. Pressure's trail tests the bar's wick, while its spec says test the close

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`PositionService.thresholdBreach`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/position/service/PositionService.java) — floors are tested against `adversePnl` (the bar's adverse extreme) and fill at the floor price. Applies to Strategy 5's `25:25` give-back trail; see [PRESSURE_STRATEGY.md](PRESSURE_STRATEGY.md#the-2525-trail). Filed 2026-09-05. |
| Why | The Pressure spec says "Use bar high/low for stop/target (intrabar), **close for trail test**". This codebase does not distinguish: since the S4 resting-order decision (2026-08-31) every floor — fixed stop or trailing rung — is treated as an SL order resting at the broker, so it fills the moment a bar touches it. Keeping that model for Strategy 5 was a deliberate user decision (2026-09-04) rather than an oversight: it is consistent with every other strategy here, needs no change to shared exit code, and is the more conservative of the two. But it **is** a divergence, and it runs one way — a wick through +25 that closes at +40 exits here and would not exit under the spec, so this book will show more trail exits and shorter holds than the reference. |
| Impact | **Unquantified.** Measurable directly and cheaply once a Pressure run exists: count `TRAIL_SL` exits whose bar *close* was still above the floor — those are exactly the trades the two models disagree on. Their realised P&L versus what the position would have done had it survived to the next bar is the size of the divergence. Doing better than that requires a second exit path and a paired run. |
| Fix sketch | If the count turns out to matter, the shape would be a **config-driven trail test mode** (`ADVERSE_EXTREME`, as today, vs `CLOSE`) rather than a switch baked into `PositionService`, so strategies 1-4 keep the resting-order model untouched and only Strategy 5 opts out. Per Rule 9 that is a `TradeConfig` column, which does not exist today — so it has to be asked for, not assumed. **Do not change the shared floor test in place**; it is the exit model for every strategy in the system. |
| Effort | **S** to count. **M** for the config-driven mode plus a before/after pair. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-05 at build time, as an accepted-and-recorded deviation rather than a defect. Revisit when the first full Pressure run is reconciled against the reference figures._ |

### S24. Pressure's entry fill is the signal candle's close, not the last close at or before the signal

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`Strategy5.execute`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/Strategy5.java) stamps `TradeSignal.price` from the resolved leg's last cached candle; `OrderService.openOrder` writes it to `trade_order.entry_price`. Filed 2026-09-05. |
| Why | The spec says "Entry premium = last option close at or before signal timestamp on that contract. If no bar, first bar after." The framework convention is the signal candle's own close, which is the same thing whenever the leg's series is current — and is **not** the same thing when the leg's series lags the spot bar that fired the signal. The S8 freshness stamp makes the lagging case *skip* the trade rather than fill it at a stale price, so the divergence surfaces as a missing trade rather than a mispriced one — the safer direction, but still a difference in trade count. Note also that the decision bar is spot while the fill is an option: `signalTime` is deliberately the spot bar's timestamp, so `entry_time` and the leg's own bar can differ by up to one bar in a degraded fetch. |
| Impact | **Unquantified.** Count Pressure ticks where a side fired but `resolveTradableKey` returned null — those are the trades the spec would have taken and this does not. The log line already exists (`fired but no tradable leg cached`, DEBUG). If the count is near zero the divergence is theoretical and this entry closes. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing to change unless the count is material, and not without the user. The candidate would be to fall back to the leg's last available close when the freshness stamp fails — which trades a missing trade for a stale-priced one, a strictly worse failure mode unless the staleness is bounded. That bound would have to be specified first. |
| Effort | **S** to count from existing logs. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-05 at build time. Lowest of the three Pressure entries; likely theoretical, but unmeasured._ |

### S33. Strategy 8's chandelier is a tighter-of-two floor, and `2 x ATR` outruns the premium band on a third of entries

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`PositionService.thresholdBreach` / `applyTrail`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/position/service/PositionService.java), `strategy_defaults.trail_atr_multiple` (changeset 048). Filed 2026-09-06 from the [S31](#s31-first-engine-replay-of-strategy8-2024-depth-2-ladder-2715-pts--770-trades--but-the-leg-set-is-not-the-replays) ledger. |
| Why | [STRATEGIES.md](STRATEGIES.md) describes the exit as "a chandelier trail (peak - 2 x ATR-14)", which reads as *the* exit. In the engine it is one of **two** floors: `thresholdBreach` takes whichever of the fixed stop (`LEAST(entry x sl_pct, max_sl_points)`) and the chandelier (`peak_profit - trail_atr_distance_at_entry`) sits higher, and labels the exit `STOP_LOSS` or `TRAIL_SL` accordingly. Which one governs is decided by the leg's own volatility, not by design. |
| Impact | **Measured on the 770-trade ledger.** `trail_atr_distance_at_entry` (2 x ATR-14 of the 15-minute series) ranges **18.11 to 301.35 points** while the fixed stop is capped at 60 and the premium band is 80-250. On **259 of 770** entries (34%) the chandelier is already tighter at entry and governs from the first monitored bar - 175 of those exit `TRAIL_SL`, and the group averages **+4.86/trade (+1,260)**. On the other 511 the fixed stop governs first and the chandelier only overtakes it once peak profit has climbed; 140 still exit `TRAIL_SL`, and the group averages **+2.85/trade (+1,456)**. At the top of the range a 301-point trail distance on a leg whose premium is at most 250 and whose fixed stop is at most 60 cannot be reached before the fixed stop fires - the trail is structurally unreachable there, not merely loose. |
| Open question | **"Does the trail need fixing?" — answered 2026-09-06: no.** Counterfactual over the 315 `TRAIL_SL` exits, re-priced with *no trail* (the fixed stop if the leg's later high reached it, otherwise the 15:00-bar close the engine itself uses for `FORCE_CLOSE`): actual **−3,448.1** vs no-trail **−4,899.4** — the trail is worth **+1,451.2 pts on its own exits** (avg −10.95 vs −15.55). 178 of the 315 would have gone on to hit the fixed stop, 98 were winners the trail cut short, 39 exited better than the close would have. Caveat: this holds the rest of the session fixed and ignores that a trail exit frees the slot for a later entry — which in this engine works *for* the trail, not against it. The tighter-of-two floor is doing what it was built to do, and the 301-point cases are simply legs where the fixed stop is the stop, which is the documented intent. What remains is an **optimisation** question, not a repair: whether a multiple other than 2.00 does better. The measurement is the sweep (1.0 / 1.5 / 2.0 / 3.0) over the same window and config set, reading pts/trade and the `TRAIL_SL` / `STOP_LOSS` split — four ~5-minute runs, no code change, since the value is already a `strategy_defaults` column. Not urgent. |
| Caveat | Do not read the two groups as a like-for-like comparison of the floors: membership is decided by ATR, so the split also sorts by leg volatility, and the +4.86 vs +2.85 gap may be the volatility rather than the floor. Separating them needs the multiple sweep above, where membership changes but the legs do not. |
| Fix sketch | **None required.** If the optional sweep ever shows a better constant, that is a live trading change (CLAUDE.md Rule 0(c)): confirm with the user, change the seed, re-run the same window before and after, record both numbers here. There is no admin endpoint for `trail_atr_multiple` (only `bracket-mode`), so a sweep edits `strategy_defaults` directly; `OrderService` reads the row per order, no restart needed. |
| Effort | **S** - four backtest runs on the existing config set. |
| Priority | _Open as an optimisation only — filed 2026-09-06; the fix-required question was closed the same day._ |

### S34. Lab Foundation 1m is dormant on 30 of 60 sessions: the protected high has been frozen at 23,923.5 since 7 Sep

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`research/strategy_lab/engine.py`](../research/strategy_lab/engine.py) (`qualifies()`, `rebuild()`, the CHoCH / flip test); every lab strategy built on it (ST1–ST4, and ST5–ST6 through the FZ gate). Filed 2026-09-27 at the FZ docs step (FZ evaluation M42 / M21; the FZ build does not change `engine.py`). **Scope widened 2026-09-30:** the same freeze silences every later engine-SETUP strategy on the 1-minute tape from 7 Sep — ST9–ST12 (managed exits, reverse), ST15, ST17–ST18 and the 1-minute learners ST19 / ST21 / ST23 (their journals end at 4 Sep 15:20) — and on 5-minute the protected high freezes too, with SETUPs only on 1, 3–4, 7 and 22–23 Sep; the c2c regime (ST25–28) reads the same trend. The candle files are complete through 25 Sep at both timeframes (375 / 75 candles every session), so the silence is the rule, not the data. The rainbow pair (ST29–30) does not use the protected level for entries. |
| Why | A swing is admitted as the protected level only if it sits on the far side of the AVWAP anchored at the last flip (`qualifies()`: in a downtrend a swing high above that AVWAP). After the 4 Sep 15:20 flip (down, anchor swing high 24,069.4) that AVWAP stayed above almost every later swing high while the closes fell about 900 points, so the protected high stepped 24,070.7 → 24,058.1 → 24,054.1 → 23,923.5 and has been frozen since 7 Sep 14:05. Nothing touches 23,923.5 again (last close 23,186 on 25 Sep, low close 23,070), so there is no CHoCH and no SETUP for the rest of the tape. Separately, every CHoCH on this tape flips the trend in touch mode (307 of 307 on 1m, 123 of 123 on 5m), because touch mode tests the AVWAP on the same wick that breaks the level; the non-flip path exists in code but never runs, so same-direction CHoCHs never repeat. That is also why FZ's re-entry rule R5 (a same-direction SETUP) is rare. |
| Impact | **Counts measured, trade impact Unquantified** (All-data windows, 2026-09-27 build). Sessions with at least one SETUP: ST1 30 of 60 (dormant 3–7 Jul, 27 Jul–11 Aug, 19 Aug, 7–25 Sep: 0 of the last 14), ST3 27 of 60 (last SETUP 4 Sep), ST2 31 of 57 (3 of the last 14), ST4 27 of 57 (last SETUP 4 Sep). The Design period's 55 1m SETUPs sit in 8 of its 22 sessions (26 Aug–4 Sep). Any per-session number (trades per session, time in market, Sharpe over calendar sessions) credits this silence to whatever runs on top, which is why the FZ reports carry active sessions and list the dormant stretches. What would measure the trade impact: a protected-level variant run as its own strategy code on the same windows, compared on SETUPs per session and on the book. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing changes without the user (Rule 0(c)), and not inside the FZ build. Candidates, each a new rule value in a strategy file: (a) a protected level that has gone untouched for N sessions is released and re-qualified against the current AVWAP; (b) qualify swings against the latest CHoCH's AVWAP pair rather than the flip anchor; (c) a close-mode flip test (ST3 / ST4 already use close-mode CHoCH and are dormant on the same September stretch, so this alone does not fix it). |
| Effort | **M**: an engine rule behind a strategy-file key, a new strategy file, a before / after run. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-27. Affects every lab Foundation strategy; the FZ numbers already report around it (active-session denominators)._ |

### S35. FZ `leave_far_side` defines the instrument: is a leave into another remembered band a TAKE?

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`research/strategy_lab/fz.py`](../research/strategy_lab/fz.py) (gate, TAKE branch 1); `leave_far_side` in `research/strategy_lab/strategies/strategy_5.json` / `strategy_6.json` (seeded `block_list`, note "user decision pending"). Filed 2026-09-27 at the FZ docs step (FZ evaluation A1 / D02 / C02). |
| Why | The 20-point bands tile the tape (94.7% of 1m bars and 83.5% of 5m bars close inside one), so two closes beyond band X usually land inside band Y. TAKE branch 1 (LEAVE) then depends on what a leave means: `any` takes every leave in the SETUP's direction, a momentum entry into the next band; `block_list` refuses it when the entered band's own read is RECYCLE / THIN / HUNT / REJECT (the SETUP falls to WATCH with `block_reason = leave_into_recycle`); `no_band` takes only a leave into ground with no remembered band. The evaluation proposed no default because this is the definition of the strategy, not a threshold; `block_list` was seeded so the build could run, and is labelled pending. |
| Impact | **Counts measured, trade impact Unquantified.** Branch-1 TAKEs each reading gives on the seeded run's ledger (any / block_list, seeded / no_band; counted from the entered read, not re-run): 1m All data 65 / 10 / 4, Design 11 / 1 / 0, Unseen 54 / 9 / 4; 5m All data 23 / 14 / 3, Design 4 / 1 / 0, Unseen 19 / 13 / 3. Of the 10 seeded 1m branch-1 TAKEs, 4 enter no band and 6 enter a band (entered visit_n median 8.5). 55 1m SETUPs are WATCH with `leave_into_recycle`. The counts are not the other readings' results: a different TAKE changes the positions, the `in_position` blocks and every watch after it. To measure: run each reading as its own code (or a filtered run with the value changed) on the same windows and read the bridge, the session-matched random control and the kept-vs-refused permutation side by side ([S39](#s39-fz-v1-first-lab-results-strategies-56-the-gated-futures-book-is-no-better-than-random-selection-and-the-setups-it-refuses-are-not-the-worse-ones) lists this as a candidate run). |
| Fix sketch | The user's decision (Rule 0(c)); nothing changes until it is made. Whichever reading is chosen, record it in both strategy files with `source: decision:<n>` and drop the pending note; if `any` is chosen, the Rules text must say TAKE-on-LEAVE is a momentum entry into the next band. |
| Effort | **S** per reading (a filtered `python lab.py ST5 ST6` takes about 5 minutes); the decision is the user's. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-27. Decide before any FZ result is read as a verdict on the idea._ |

### S38. FZ v1 (lab Strategies 5–6): the fade block also refuses breakouts, the open guard refuses almost every opening SETUP, and the pre-registered visit_n is off

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`research/strategy_lab/fz.py`](../research/strategy_lab/fz.py) (`fade`, `open_pierce`, `gate`, the visit rule); seeds in `research/strategy_lab/strategies/strategy_5.json` / `strategy_6.json` (`fade_scope`, `fade_block_bars`, `open_bars`, `open_sit_closes`). Filed 2026-09-27 while building the FZ core (phase 1 of the FZ build plan; ids S34–S37 are reserved for that plan's docs step). |
| Why | **(a) The fade block also refuses breakouts.** The build plan scopes `hunt_fade` to "the ref / left band": a HUNT (one close out and back in on 1m) followed within `fade_block_bars` by a two-close LEAVE of the same band the same way BLOCKs a SETUP in the leave direction, so the second, successful push is treated as a fade of the first. Decision 6 was written for flush-then-reclaim; nothing says whether it should also cover a later leave of that band. **(b) The open guard is near-total.** `open_pierce` asks whether the bar's range crosses an edge of *any* band last visited in an earlier session with fewer than `open_sit_closes` closes inside today; with bands covering most of the tape an opening bar almost always crosses one, so the guard reduces to "no SETUP in the first `open_bars`". **(c) Pre-registered visit_n is off.** The evaluation pre-registered a visit_n median of ~30 at 1m SETUPs; its simulator ended a visit on the first close inside a neighbouring band, while the plan (D01 tie-break) keeps the visit live, so visits are fewer and longer. **(d) A LEAVE into open ground that fails TAKE has no watch.** The plan opens a WATCH only on a containing band (else BLOCK `new`), while evaluation B10 / A11 say a LEAVE refused for volume or the gap-open rule "gates WATCH". |
| Impact | **Counts measured, trade impact Unquantified** (all-data windows, seeded thresholds, 2026-09-27 dry run; 94.7% of 1m bars and 83.5% of 5m bars close inside a band). (a) 1m: 24 `hunt_fade` BLOCKs of 207 SETUPs; 15 are LEAVE reads in the leave direction, and in 15 the HUNT is on the left band only. 5m: 5 of 73, 3 LEAVE reads, 1 on the left band only. (b) 1m: 10 of the 11 SETUPs in the open window are BLOCKed `open_pierce` (the 11th is a WATCH); 5m: 1 of 1. (c) visit_n median at SETUP 19 on 1m (26.5 over the 52 inside-read SETUPs), 7 on 5m. (d) 0 occurrences on either file. To measure the trade impact: run the FZ bridge and session-matched random control twice on the same windows, once as seeded and once with each candidate below, and compare kept-vs-blocked expectancy. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing changes without the user (Rule 0(c)). (a) Candidate: a `fade_scope` value that reads only the ref band's own HUNT, so a leave of the hunted band in the pierce direction reaches TAKE branch 1. (b) Candidate: test only the band containing the open (or the nearest few) and make that choice a strategy-file key. (c) ~~Correct the README expectation when the docs step lands~~ — done 2026-09-27 (the README's FZ expectations table shows the pre-registered ~30 beside the measured 19); no rule change. (d) Candidate: WATCH the left band with R1–R3 already met, R4 re-evaluated per far-side bar, and the gap rule held until `open_quiet_bars`. |
| Effort | **S** per variant run once `lab.py` dispatches FZ rows; **M** for the paired report. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-27 during the FZ core build. Decide (a) and (b) with the user before the first FZ result is published._ |

### S39. FZ v1 first lab results (Strategies 5–6): the gated futures book is no better than random selection, and the SETUPs it refuses are not the worse ones

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`research/strategy_lab/fz.py`](../research/strategy_lab/fz.py) (gate), [`fz_report.py`](../research/strategy_lab/fz_report.py) (bridge, session-matched random control, permutation test), `lab.py` (`run_fz`, `fz_payload`); numbers in `web/ST5|ST6/<run>/-/summary.json['fz']` and `results/fz_ledger.csv`. Filed 2026-09-27 at the FZ lab-integration step (phase 2 of the FZ build plan), from the first `python lab.py ST5 ST6` run with the seeded thresholds. |
| Why | The evaluation (M43) says FZ must not be judged by net against Foundation: costs are about the whole per-trade loss, so any gate that drops trades raises net. The question is whether FZ's selection beats keeping the same number of Foundation positions per session at random, and whether the Foundation trades it keeps differ from the ones it refuses. On the first run the answer is no on both timeframes, and the refused SETUPs include Foundation's largest winners. |
| Impact | **Measured, futures, seeded thresholds; the Unseen window is not out of sample for FZ; trade impact of any change Unquantified.** Numbers from the 2026-09-27 phase-5 rebuild ([S40](#s40-fz-v1-review-three-rule-readings-the-build-chose-without-the-user-and-a-conformance-fix-that-moved-two-1m-positions): two 1m defend TAKEs are no longer turned into REENTERs, and the permutation's kept set is now the SETUPs FZ traded; the first run keyed it on the gate as of the SETUP bar, which put most REENTER SETUPs in the refused set). **1m (ST5) All data:** 51 positions (29 TAKE + 22 REENTER), net −81,023 vs Foundation's 207 / −216,468 in the same run. Bridge: avoided price move −37,161, avoided charges + slippage +173,698, REENTER vs Foundation's exit on the same SETUP −1,091 (22). Session-matched random control: FZ at the **6.2th percentile** (draws p5 −82,712 / p50 −44,994 / p95 +17,551). Kept (the SETUPs FZ traded) vs refused Foundation trades: mean −1,567 vs −875 per trade, permutation p 0.44. Unseen: 41 positions, 6.5th percentile, p 0.54. Design: 10 positions (sample flag "indicative"), 37.0th percentile, p 0.62. **5m (ST6) All data:** 26 positions (14 + 12), net −52,261 vs 73 / −73,753; control 49.8th percentile; kept vs refused mean −2,354 vs −267, permutation p 0.18 (Unseen −2,822 vs −13, p 0.08: the SETUPs FZ trades are the worse ones there, not the better); Unseen 80.6th; Design 6 positions (PF / t "not reported"), 42.1th, p 0.97. **Where it comes from (1m All data, net INR / futures pts before costs):** REENTER from WATCH_EDGE 20: −32,999 / −164.9; REENTER from a plain WATCH 2: −4,569 / −36.2; TAKE on a LEAVE 10: −22,872 / −180.9; TAKE defend 19: −20,583 / +9.3. 5m: REENTER from WATCH_EDGE 12: −27,292 / −214.4; TAKE on a LEAVE 14: −24,969 / −143.9. **Foundation points of the SETUPs FZ hard-BLOCKed:** 1m +460.7 over 42 (the `clock` BLOCK of 4 Sep 15:22 alone is +854, Foundation's short still open at the data end); 5m +380.1 over 15 (two trades: +396 `clock` 28 Jul 15:20, +322 `hunt_fade` 21 Jul 09:30). The TAKE rows' own Foundation points: 1m −171.6 over 29, 5m −143.9 over 14. Options via futures (W-ATR2) sit at the 11.0th (1m) and 79.3th (5m) percentile of their own control. End-of-data convention (a position open on a window's last bar reads as ending there, so a SETUP on that bar could open a second one): 0 SETUPs on any window's last bar, 0 FZ positions open at any window end. First run, for the record: 1m 27 + 24 positions, net −83,817, 4.0th percentile, p 0.58; 5m p 0.63. |
| Open question | Is the negative result a property of the gate or of one or two branches? The measurement is a paired run per candidate (same windows, bridge + control + permutation read side by side): `edge_watch = containing_band` (drops most WATCH_EDGE re-entries), `leave_far_side = no_band` / `any`, and the S38 (a) / (b) fade and open-guard candidates. The outlier dependence (one clock BLOCK carries most of the 1m BLOCK points) needs the same tables with the open-at-end trade excluded (`books.*.net_ex_open` already reports it). |
| Fix sketch | Nothing changes without the user (Rule 0(c)); the seeded values stay. Each candidate above is a new strategy-file value run as its own code or a filtered lab run, with both results recorded here. |
| Effort | **S** per candidate run (a filtered `python lab.py ST5 ST6` takes about 5 minutes); **M** for the side-by-side write-up. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-27. Show the control percentile beside net wherever an FZ result is published, so cost avoidance is not read as edge (done 2026-09-27: the KPI strip's Net P&L tile and the Zone gate tab both carry it)._ |

### S40. FZ v1 review: three rule readings the build chose without the user, and a conformance fix that moved two 1m positions

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`research/strategy_lab/fz.py`](../research/strategy_lab/fz.py): `take_branch` (the gap-LEAVE rule), `watch_step` (R1 / R3), the bar loop's return into the band just left, `gate` (a TAKE turned REENTER). Filed 2026-09-27 from the three-lens review of the FZ build (phase 5 of the FZ build plan); numbers from the rebuild that followed. |
| Why | **(a) Gap LEAVE quiet clause.** Evaluation A11 / M25 (the source of `open_quiet_bars`) says a LEAVE whose outside closes include the session's first bar gates WATCH "until `open_quiet_bars` have elapsed and R2 is re-met by closes after that". The build plan kept only the first half, and `fz.py` refuses a gap LEAVE only while `session_bar < open_quiet_bars`, so a gap leave is TAKEn on the opening closes alone once the quiet bars have passed. **(b) R3 reading.** R3 is "since R1 no close inside the band or across its mid". `fz.py` reads it as an unbroken far-side run: a close back inside (fewer than `cancel_inside_bars` of them, which would cancel an armed watch) breaks the run, and the next far-side close is a fresh R1 that re-arms the watch. The strict reading (after the first R1, one close back inside and the watch can no longer REENTER) is not implemented. The ledger carries `armed_bars` (from the first R1) and `rearmed_bars` (from the latest), the watch log `armed_time` and `last_armed_time`. **(c) A return into the band just left while a neighbour holds the visit.** The build plan says a close back inside the left band starts visit n+1 on it (read RECYCLE / …) and sets `last_leave_failed`; the D01 tie-break says one live visit at a time. The build lets D01 win: the return ends the LEAVE and flags the band (`last_leave_failed` on the card while it is the ref band), but no visit opens on it while the neighbour's visit lives; the bar is counted as `leave_return_no_visit`. **(d) Fixed in this phase (conformance to the plan and to evaluation A12 / M22, no user choice involved):** the gate turned *any* TAKE into a REENTER when a same-direction watch sat on the band, including FIRST_PRINT / defend TAKEs whose fill is inside the band and whose watch had never armed, so those positions got the band-reclaim exit without R1–R5. Now only a branch-1 (LEAVE) TAKE of the watched band converts; a FIRST_PRINT or defend TAKE stays Foundation's TAKE and the watch is left as it is. Whether a same-direction TAKE on the watched band should end the watch is not in the plan and is left open (both watches involved survived their TAKE; neither re-entered: one was replaced, one cancelled by an opposite leave). |
| Impact | **(a)–(c): counts measured, trade impact Unquantified. (d): measured before / after.** (a) Branch-1 positions on a gap LEAVE: 1m 1 (31 Aug 09:26, session bar 11, TAKE; All data and Design), 5m 2 (14 Aug 09:25 session bar 2 and 17 Aug 09:30 session bar 3, both TAKE; All data and Unseen). Which of them the full clause would refuse is not measured. (b) REENTER positions whose watch re-armed before the fill: 1m 1 (3 Sep 13:02, 71 bars from the first arming, 69 from the latest), 5m 0; watches that re-armed at least once (All data): 1m 30 of 134, 5m 0 of 44. (c) Bars where a close came back inside the band just left while a neighbour held the visit (All data): 1m 1,164 of 22,500 shown bars (5.2%), 5m 205 of 4,275 (4.8%); Foundation SETUPs on such bars: 1m 8 (6 WATCH, 1 REENTER, 1 BLOCK `clock`), 5m 1 (WATCH). (d) 1m futures, before → after: 2026-08-13 11:49 up, REENTER −10.60 pts (`band_reclaim` after 1 bar) → TAKE +41.00 (`next_choch`); 2026-08-26 12:54 up, REENTER −1.40 (`band_reclaim`) → TAKE −10.00 (`stop_loss`). All data: positions 27 TAKE + 24 REENTER → 29 + 22, futures pts −415.7 → −372.7, net −83,817 → −81,023, random-control percentile 4.0th → 6.2th. Design: 3 + 7 → 4 + 6, net −13,778 → −14,337, 42.0th → 37.0th. Unseen: 24 + 17 → 25 + 16, net −70,039 → −66,686, 3.5th → 6.5th. 5m: no conversion, unchanged. The same rebuild also changed reporting only (no trade): the permutation's kept set is the SETUPs FZ traded (p before → after: 1m All 0.58 → 0.44, Design 0.36 → 0.62, Unseen 0.77 → 0.54; 5m All 0.63 → 0.18, Design 0.15 → 0.97, Unseen 0.39 → 0.08), gate tables count SETUPs by `outcome_gate` (how they ended), the crosshair's volume ratio is NA when the band's first visit is NA, and `results/summary.json` rows carry `fz_priced`. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing changes for (a)–(c) without the user (Rule 0(c)); each candidate is a strategy-file key and a paired run on the same windows (bridge, random control, permutation side by side), both results recorded here. (a) Count the gap LEAVE's far-side closes with `session_bar >= open_quiet_bars` (reset like the outside run) and take branch 1 only when that count reaches `leave_closes`; touches the 3 positions above. (b) Strict R3: after the first R1, a close back inside ends the watch's REENTER chance (outcome `armed_expired:back_inside` at once); touches the 1m REENTER above. (c) End the neighbour's visit (`ended_by = 'return'`) and open visit n+1 on the left band on that bar; this changes the card on those bars and every read downstream, so the truncation and parity tests must be re-run. (d) needs a decision only on the open question above (a `same_take` cancel reason, or none). |
| Effort | **S** each for (a) and (b); **M** for (c) (card change, tests, paired run). |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-27. (a) and (b) touch 3 and 1 positions on this tape; (c) is the one that can move many reads._ |

### S49. FZ v3 (lab Strategies 13–14, reserved): the band gate must skip losing SETUPs and not profitable ones — the sideways "CHoCH after CHoCH, no break" regime, reaction at visibly high volume, level respect vs break, and a learned rule list

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab/strategies/strategy_13.json` / `strategy_14.json` (numbers reserved with the session that owns 9–12; **not created**); [`fz.py`](../research/strategy_lab/fz.py), [`fz_report.py`](../research/strategy_lab/fz_report.py) and Strategies 5–8 untouched. Study scripts and tables live in the FZ session's scratchpad (`FZ_V3_STUDY_BRIEF.md`, `fz_v3/`). Filed 2026-09-29. |
| Why | User's ask (2026-09-29): the bands "exist to take me to profit, not to loss — avoid only loss trades and not profitable ones, and it is different for different times"; "if CHoCH and CHoCH and no breaks then it is sideways; trades in sideways are hurting"; check how price reacts at visibly high volume — a level "will be respected: if it breaks there will be a large move, else price retests, retests and respects"; derive the rules with "reinforcement learning and generative AI". The frozen v2 gate (S42) is a card diagnostic whose gain over Foundation is cost avoidance (S39); it has never been judged as a loser filter. Five pre-registered hypotheses, one agent each, on the 5-year near-month files: **H1** what the ST7/ST8 (and ST5/ST6) gates skip vs keep by Foundation outcome, hour, read and block reason (precision / recall as a loser filter; where the big winners are skipped); **H2** regime from the engine's own event stream (CHoCHs since the last BOS, bars since CHoCH, alternation count, range / ATR) → Foundation P&L by regime and hour, then a skip filter over a small grid; **H3** volume ≥ v × rolling session median (grid) → forward returns, MFE / MAE, high-low hold rates, SETUP outcomes within M bars by direction agreement; **H4** at every touch of a protected level / room edge / swing: break (close beyond > x ATR within N bars) vs respect, move size after each, retests before a break, SETUP outcomes at respected vs broken levels; **H5** a learned gate on the per-SETUP feature table — a depth-≤3 tree and a greedy rule-list search (≤ 8 conjunctive rules) plus a gradient-boosting ceiling. Sequential RL is not justified (one-step decision per SETUP, a few thousand SETUPs): this is an offline contextual bandit / supervised rule search, and "generative" is the search over the rule space, reported as such. |
| Protocol | IS = sessions 2021-10-01 … 2025-12-31 (all learning and selection, session-blocked CV); OOS = 2026-01-01 … 2026-09-25, evaluated **once** after selection. Every implied gate is reported with kept-vs-skipped Foundation expectancy (never net alone: fewer trades always raises net by ₹1,114 per skipped 1m trade), the session-matched random-control percentile (`fz_report.random_control`) and the kept-vs-refused permutation p. Every feature at a SETUP bar uses bars ≤ that bar; forward returns are labels only. Any surviving rule is a strategy-file key with `source: "learned on IS 2021-10..2025-12, <script>"`, never a number in code. Two adversarial refuters (leakage / definitions; arithmetic / control) per finding. |
| Impact | **Unquantified.** What measures it: the H1 audit numbers (share of Foundation winners the frozen gate skips, by hour), and for each candidate rule the IS and OOS kept / skipped nets, precision / recall on losers and the control percentile. Recorded here when the study returns. **Bridge of the frozen gates under the 2026-window publish (2026-09-29, other session's full build, `results/history` v5 vs v4 for ST5/ST6, v3 vs v2 for ST7/ST8; the version change = fz default keys now written explicitly + lab pricing changes: square-off candle rule, strike lock holding a still-open position's last candle; `history_from = 2026-01-01`, so all-data rows are not like-for-like, design / unseen / 3M are).** Futures net ₹ (trades), old → new: ST5 design −5,866 → −1,493 (6 → 14), unseen −42,080 → −39,759 (43 → 40), 3M −46,422 → −39,400 (52 → 55); ST6 design −15,440 → +13,983 (6 → 6), unseen −16,410 → −8,830 (7 → 10), 3M −28,817 → −2,216 (14 → 18); ST7 design −11,711 → −8,949 (9 → 9), unseen −46,574 → −25,149 (30 → 30), 3M −65,366 → −38,971 (41 → 41); ST8 design +5,510 → −7,651 (11 → 9), unseen −18,965 → −24,067 (11 → 10), 3M −20,510 → −37,273 (26 → 20). Same trade counts with different nets (ST7 all three windows) = the pricing change; changed counts (ST5 design 6 → 14, ST6, ST8) = the lock / square-off change and the memory start moving which SETUPs open, not an FZ rule change (`fz.py` untouched since the freeze). **Unquantified** which of the two explains each count change; the paired local run on the pinned 3-month tape (`pin3m.py`) with the old lab pricing would measure it. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing changes on Strategies 5–8 (frozen, Rule 0(c)). If a rule list survives OOS and the control, build Strategies 13 (1 min) / 14 (5 min) as new files (`entry_rule fz_v3`) carrying the learned keys, after the user chooses which rules go in; ST7/ST8 are never retuned toward these results. If nothing survives, record that here and the sleeve stays a card. |
| Effort | **L** (study, running) + **M** (build, if warranted). |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-29. Study in progress._ |

### S155. 1-minute Supertrend option selling that turns into a straddle, the losing leg cut on a range breakout (the user, 2026-10-28: "take a position on the 1-minute Supertrend; if the other side comes make it a straddle; on a range breakout exit the loss-making one; only sell"). Measured on NIFTY weekly options 2018-01 - 2026-10 (2,150 days): it loses in every year; the straddle beats plain flip-selling (PF 0.86 / 0.83 vs 0.75 / 0.76) but the breakout cuts lose -7.2 points a leg on 9,404 legs while the legs held to 15:20 make +21 - the range breakouts are mostly false. Nothing adopted

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only; no strategy changed |
| Code | `research/strategy_lab_v2/tools/st_straddle.py` |

**Rules, fixed before the run.** Supertrend(10, 3) on 1-minute near-month futures. Flat: a flip 09:20 - 14:30 sells one ATM weekly option at the next minute's open (flip down -> CE, flip up -> PE; ATM from the index; nearest weekly on or after the day); the range starts there. One leg: the opposite flip sells the other option (V1 at the held leg's strike = a true straddle; V2 at that minute's ATM); the range freezes = futures high / low since the held leg's entry. Straddle: a 1-minute close above the range buys back the CE, below buys back the PE; the kept leg is one leg again (range restarts; the next opposite flip re-straddles). Conversions until 15:10, all bought back 15:20, no stop on a single leg. Lab option slippage and dated charges, lot 65. B0 = sell on every flip, buy back and reverse on the next.

| Version | Legs | Avg pts / leg (after slippage) | Charges ₹ / leg | Win % legs / days | PF 2018-22 | PF 2023-26 | PF 2026 | Net 2018-22 ₹ | Net 2023-26 ₹ | Net 2026 ₹ | Worst day ₹ | Max drawdown ₹ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **V1 straddle at the held strike (as asked)** | 12,277 | -0.61 | 60 | 35 / 42 | 0.86 | 0.83 | 0.75 | -5,30,977 | -6,96,984 | -2,49,980 | -20,242 | -12,27,975 |
| V2 second leg at that minute's ATM | 12,241 | -0.26 | 59 | 36 / 45 | 0.91 | 0.84 | 0.76 | -3,27,119 | -5,98,484 | -2,22,843 | -16,863 | -9,25,778 |
| B0 sell on each flip, no straddle | 16,011 | -0.79 | 59 | 35 / 38 | 0.75 | 0.76 | 0.71 | -9,06,478 | -8,57,251 | -2,51,501 | -18,738 | -17,61,813 |

V1 by year (₹): 2018 -1,62,394; 2019 -95,215; 2020 -1,498; 2021 -93,258; 2022 -1,78,611; 2023 -1,40,609; 2024 -1,33,278; 2025 -1,73,116; 2026 -2,49,980. Exits: 9,404 breakout buy-backs, -7.2 points a leg, -49.8 L; 2,873 legs held to 15:20, +21.0 points, +37.6 L. About 6 legs a day (max 13).

**Reading.** Turning the flip into a straddle does help - it trades fewer legs and the second leg collects decay the plain flip book gives away - but the 1-minute range around a flip is narrow, so a 1-minute close outside it is mostly noise: the cut leg is bought back near its high and the market comes back. The legs that survive to the close earn the decay (+21 points), which says the edge here is in holding the straddle, not in cutting it on a 1-minute break. Open (not tested, needs pre-registration): a wider break (5-minute close, or the range plus a buffer), or keeping the straddle and cutting a leg only on a premium stop.

### S154. Sideways from the Choppiness Index, its integral as a grade of the sideways stretch, and whether the grade says anything about the breakout (the user, 2026-10-28, after the range-band charts: sideways = the sum of the last candles' ranges much larger than their high-low span; then "apply integration in this and grade its quality"). Measured on 5-minute futures 2018-01 - 2026-10: the integral grades the sideways stretch (longer, narrower boxes score higher) but not the breakout - on a fixed 30-point target A / B / C follow through equally (37 / 37 / 36% in 2018-22, 40 / 44 / 45% in 2023-26) and no worse than breaks not from sideways (39 / 48%); every grade's break trade loses after costs. Nothing adopted

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only; no strategy changed |
| Code | `research/strategy_lab_v2/tools/chop_integral.py` |

**Rules.** CHOP(12) on 5-minute near-month futures from the previous 12 candles (n-1 lag): path = sum of (high - low), span = highest high - lowest low, CHOP = 100 log10(path / span) / log10(12); sideways when CHOP >= 50 (N and the cutoff were picked by eye on 21-23 Oct 2026 charts for the user, not tuned here; the textbook 61.8 shaded only 12 - 14% of candles). Integral = the area above 50 over the running sideways stretch. Breakout = a close outside the previous-12 high / low box with the candle before sideways, 09:30 - 14:30; trade in at the next open, stop on a close past the box's other side, out after 1 hour or at 15:20. Grade = the integral's 2018-22 terciles (C < 24 <= B < 78 <= A), the same cut points on 2023-26. Half of all 5-minute candles read sideways at 50.

| Breaks | Trades | Avg stretch, candles | Median box, pts | Went 1x box the break's way | Gross pts / trade | PF 2018-22 | PF 2023-26 | PF 2026 | Net 2018-22 ₹ | Net 2023-26 ₹ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| not from sideways (reference) | 8,208 | 0 | 83 | 9% | +0.55 | 0.80 | 0.68 | 0.75 | -10,70,716 | -17,92,104 |
| all from sideways | 10,119 | 9 | 44 | 24% | +0.71 | 0.80 | 0.64 | 0.51 | -11,81,830 | -23,20,077 |
| grade A | 3,754 | 16 | 39 | 29% | +1.76 | 0.85 | 0.67 | 0.53 | -2,76,230 | -8,52,177 |
| grade B | 3,201 | 6 | 44 | 25% | -0.13 | 0.78 | 0.61 | 0.49 | -4,56,618 | -7,83,912 |
| grade C | 3,164 | 2 | 51 | 19% | +0.30 | 0.78 | 0.64 | 0.52 | -4,48,982 | -6,83,987 |

Follow-through measured as 1x the box width ranks A > B > C in both halves (32 / 27 / 20% and 25 / 21 / 18%), but A boxes are narrower, so that target is easier. On a fixed 30 points from the entry before the stop (added after the first run to separate the two): 37 / 37 / 36% (2018-22), 40 / 44 / 45% (2023-26); breaks not from sideways 39 / 48%. By stretch length alone the 1x-box rate rises 20% (1-3 candles) -> 30% (13+), the same width effect.

**Reading.** The integral is a fair grade of how sideways a stretch was - long, tight, choppy stretches score A - and it is a usable sideways detector (it reads the 21-23 Oct 2026 days the way the eye does). It does not grade the breakout: a long tight range is not followed by a bigger move in points, and a breakout trade on its own loses in every grade. Open: use the CHOP sideways flag as a no-trade filter on a book that already makes money (Jilly, the S149 candidate) - not tested, would need its own pre-registration.

### S153. Multi-timeframe Supertrend (the user, 2026-10-28: "if the daily, 15-minute and 5-minute Supertrend are in the same direction, trade the 1-minute in the same direction"). Measured on near-month futures 2018-01 - 2026-10: the gross edge is 0 - 2 points a trade against a 4 - 13 point cost, so all six pre-registered versions lose in both halves and in 2026; the pick (fresh 4-way alignment, out on the 5-minute flip) PF 0.93 / 0.73 / 0.49, and through a 100-point ITM weekly option 0.94 / 0.80 / 0.57. Nothing adopted

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only; no strategy changed |
| Code | `research/strategy_lab_v2/tools/supertrend_mtf.py` |

**Rules, pre-registered.** Supertrend(10, 3) on hl2, Wilder ATR. Daily = yesterday's completed daily bar (today's is not known intraday); 15m / 5m = the last completed candle; 1m = the 1-minute Supertrend. Entries 09:30 - 14:30 at the next minute's open, one position at a time: E1 the 1-minute Supertrend flips to the side the three higher frames agree on; E2 all four agree and did not the minute before. Exits at the next open: X1 / X2 / X3 = the 1m / 5m / 15m Supertrend flips against; always out at 15:20. Pick = the best 2018-22 PF. The three higher frames agree on 37% of 09:30 - 14:30 minutes (long 24%, short 14%).

| Version | Trades | Avg hold, min | Gross pts / trade | Win % | PF 2018-22 | PF 2023-26 | PF 2026 | Net 2018-22 ₹ | Net 2023-26 ₹ | Net 2026 ₹ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E1 1m flip / X1 1m flip | 2,350 | 42 | +0.41 | 30 | 0.70 | 0.59 | 0.28 | -3,36,975 | -4,92,632 | -2,98,027 |
| E1 1m flip / X2 5m flip | 1,083 | 150 | +1.92 | 38 | 0.93 | 0.79 | 0.38 | -74,922 | -2,06,585 | -2,25,918 |
| E1 1m flip / X3 15m flip | 1,032 | 218 | +0.69 | 47 | 0.89 | 0.81 | 0.41 | -1,41,026 | -2,10,187 | -2,43,294 |
| E2 fresh 4-way align / X1 1m flip | 3,147 | 41 | -0.22 | 30 | 0.70 | 0.54 | 0.33 | -4,67,281 | -7,73,234 | -3,54,958 |
| **E2 fresh 4-way align / X2 5m flip (pick)** | 1,480 | 146 | +0.79 | 39 | 0.93 | 0.73 | 0.49 | -99,811 | -3,89,363 | -2,30,086 |
| E2 fresh 4-way align / X3 15m flip | 1,306 | 214 | -0.17 | 46 | 0.92 | 0.75 | 0.50 | -1,27,002 | -3,90,107 | -2,45,701 |

The pick by year (gross / cost points a trade, net ₹): 2018 +2.6 / 3.8, -13,291; 2019 +2.3 / 4.0, -17,706; 2020 +5.2 / 3.9, +15,194; 2021 +6.0 / 4.9, +12,029; 2022 -2.7 / 5.1, -96,037; 2023 +8.1 / 5.4, +30,820; 2024 -7.4 / 6.8, -1,28,776; 2025 +1.9 / 8.2, -61,322; 2026 -13.7 / 13.2, -2,30,086. Longs lose more than shorts overall (-2.95 L of the -4.89 L).

Through a weekly option 100 points in the money (S150's method): the pick 1,448 trades, PF 0.94 / 0.80 / 0.57; E1 / X2 0.95 / 0.91 / 0.49; E2 / X3 0.92 / 0.81 / 0.56 - cheaper than futures but still below 1.

**Reading.** Agreement of the daily, 15m and 5m Supertrend picks the trend side only marginally better than chance on a 1-minute entry (a few points gross in the good years, negative in 2022, 2024, 2026), and the 1-minute flip that times the entry is inside normal noise - the 1m exit versions trade 3x as often and lose most. Not tried (open): Supertrend alignment as a filter on a book that already makes money (Jilly, the S149 candidate), or other (ATR, multiplier) settings - those would need their own pre-registration, not a re-pick on these numbers.

### S152. Strategy 1's trades against the classic daily pivots (the user: "add pivot to Strategy 1 and validate how many fail inside R1 - S1 and how many outside"). Measured on all 8,326 futures trades 2018-01 - 2026-10: 85.8% fail inside S1 - R1 (5,857 trades, PF 0.28) and 83.6% outside (2,469, PF 0.36); every pivot zone and both pivot sides lose with 80 - 88% failures, so a pivot filter cannot turn Strategy 1 around. Nothing adopted

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only; Strategy 1 unchanged |
| Code | `research/strategy_lab_v2/tools/st1_pivots.py` |

**Build.** Pivots from the previous session's near-month futures (the download store): P = (H + L + C) / 3, R1 = 2P - L, S1 = 2P - H, R2 = P + (H - L), S2 = P - (H - L). Each saved Strategy 1 trade (results/ST1/all-data_1m/FUT, 1-minute, flat 15:25) placed by its entry price; failed = net <= 0 after costs.

| Entry zone | Trades | Failed | Fail % | Net ₹ | PF | PF 2018-22 / 2023-26 | Avg net ₹ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| inside S1 - R1 | 5,857 | 5,024 | 85.8 | -60,26,935 | 0.28 | 0.26 / 0.29 | -1,029 |
| outside (beyond R1 or S1) | 2,469 | 2,065 | 83.6 | -21,64,938 | 0.36 | 0.33 / 0.38 | -877 |
| - above R2 | 271 | 226 | 83.4 | -2,12,683 | 0.42 | 0.33 / 0.47 | -785 |
| - R1 - R2 | 731 | 600 | 82.1 | -5,63,387 | 0.41 | 0.38 / 0.42 | -771 |
| - S1 - S2 | 956 | 801 | 83.8 | -8,99,117 | 0.31 | 0.33 / 0.30 | -940 |
| - below S2 | 511 | 438 | 85.7 | -4,89,750 | 0.36 | 0.28 / 0.40 | -958 |

By side: the least bad cells are longs above R2 (168 trades, 81.5% fail, PF 0.55) and shorts below S2 (301, 84.1%, PF 0.49) - breakouts beyond the second pivot in their own direction; the worst are longs below S2 (88.1%, PF 0.17) and shorts above R2 (86.4%, PF 0.23) - counter-trend entries beyond the far pivot. With the pivot (a long above P / a short below P) 4,719 trades PF 0.30, against it 3,607 PF 0.30 - no difference.

**Reading.** 70% of Strategy 1's entries happen inside S1 - R1, where it fails slightly more often (85.8% vs 83.6%) and loses more per trade (₹-1,029 vs ₹-877). The pivots do rank the zones in a sensible order - a breakout past R2 / S2 in its own direction is the least bad, a counter-trend trade beyond the far pivot the worst - but the best zone still fails four trades in five and loses ₹600 - 800 a trade; Strategy 1's problem is the 1-minute entries and costs (S141), not where they sit against the pivots.

**Open (not adopted).** A pivot zone as a filter on a profitable book (e.g. Jilly) is untested.

### S151. CHoCH to CHoCH (Strategies 25 - 28 / 147 - 150) with compounding lots - "capital for 2 lots, trade 1; at 2x the capital trade 2 lots, and so on" (the user). Measured: the books never reach 2x the starting capital in any start year, so the rule never adds a lot; from 2018 every book runs out of the money for one lot within the first year; ST147 from 2025 ends +13% (1 lot throughout)

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only |
| Code | `research/strategy_lab_v2/tools/scale_lots.py` |

**Build.** On the saved trades (1 lot each, its net after costs): starting capital = 2 x the first trade's premium x 65; lots = max(1, floor(equity / starting capital)); trading stops when equity cannot pay one lot of the next put (a long put is paid in full). A second reading sizes each trade off its own premium (equity / (2 x premium x 65)) - identical here.

| Book | Start 2018 | Start 2023 | Start 2025 |
|---|---|---|---|
| ST147 (PCR on, close stops) | ₹21,352 → ₹11,417 (-47%), out of money 2018-11 | ₹24,992 → ₹20,198 (-19%), stopped 2025-05 (premiums rose past the equity) | ₹41,782 → ₹47,162 (+13%), 163 trades |
| ST148 (PCR off, close) | ₹18,193 → ₹12,797 (-30%), stopped 2018-03 | ₹32,838 → ₹17,365 (-47%), stopped 2023-01 | ₹55,250 → ₹20,162 (-64%), stopped 2025-08 |
| ST149 (PCR on, touch) | ₹21,352 → ₹12,238 (-43%), stopped 2018-11 | ₹24,992 → ₹22,979 (-8%), stopped 2025-05 | ₹41,782 → ₹39,029 (-7%) |
| ST150 (PCR off, touch) | ₹18,193 → ₹14,509 (-20%), stopped 2018-02 | ₹32,838 → ₹17,115 (-48%), stopped 2025-01 | ₹55,250 → ₹43,385 (-21%) |

ST25 on its own data window (2026-06-24 - 09-04, 31 trades): ₹46,670 → ₹56,679 (+21%), still 1 lot. Max lots is 1 in every run.

**With top-ups** (the user: "keep adding money if it runs out" - when equity cannot pay one lot, add enough to bring it back to 2 lots' worth at that premium; lots = whole multiples of all the money put in): still 1 lot in every run, so the result is the 1-lot flat net. From 2018: ST147 ₹97,427 put in (4 top-ups) → ₹68,972 (-₹28,455); ST148 ₹1,93,268 (8) → ₹58,598 (-₹1,34,669); ST149 ₹85,278 (3) → ₹50,793 (-₹34,484); ST150 ₹1,06,847 (4) → ₹36,289 (-₹70,558). From 2025: ST147 ₹41,782, no top-up → ₹47,162 (+₹5,380); the others lose ₹2,753 - ₹55,338.

**Reading.** Compounding multiplies an edge; these books have none over the long run (S147: PF 0.72 - 0.84), so the step rule only ever trades 1 lot and the capital shrinks. Premiums also roughly doubled from 2018 to 2026 with the index, so capital sized for 2 lots at the start cannot pay for 1 lot later even at break-even. A sizing rule is worth fitting only on a book with a positive expectancy (e.g. the S150 candidate through the option).

### S150. Why 1-minute CHoCH / BOS loses after a Jilly noise-band break, the fixes that follow from it, and the S149 candidate traded through an ITM option. Measured: the band break is a weak trend-day signal (only 32% of break days trend; 1-minute structure nets +76 points a day on those and -48 / -102 on the flat / failed ones); first-trade-only with a 5-minute-swing stop lifts the 1-minute trades from PF 0.82 / 0.65 to 1.15 / 0.93; the frozen S149 candidate through a weekly option 100 points in the money: PF 1.84 / 1.11 / 1.74 (2026) against 1.82 / 1.03 / 1.36 on futures - the option cuts the cost to about 1 point a trade

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only; no strategy changed |
| Code | `research/strategy_lab_v2/tools/why_1m_fails.py`, `tools/next_tests.py` |

**Why it fails** (the user: "why do 1-minute CHoCH / BOS not work if the market is not sideways - the noise-band cross says it is not sideways - where does it fail on those days"; tools/why_1m_fails.py, 6,853 trades on 1,349 break days, 2018-01 - 2026-10): holding the break to 15:20 splits the days into trend held (> +50 points; 435 days, 32%; holding +131 a day), flat (577, 43%; +2) and failed (337, 25%; -131). 1-minute structure on those: 3.5 / 5.6 / 6.1 trades a day, net +75.7 / -47.7 / -101.7 points a day - it keeps 63% of a real trend day's move but churns on the other two thirds. Exits: 51% at the stop (the last 1-minute swing, a median 14.4 points away - about two 1-minute bars' range of 6.8), averaging -14.5; 36% at the next opposite CHoCH, +1.9; 13% at 15:20, +66.5. 54% of the losers were ahead by more than their round-trip cost first (median best 6.7 points). Re-entries the same day pay a median 5.9 points worse than the previous exit; the first trade of a day averages +5.2 gross points, the later ones +1.1. Costs average 30.9 points a day against +9.6 gross.

**Fixes, pre-registered** (tools/next_tests.py; this bench's structure engine runs on the whole series, so T0 differs a little from the jilly_ext numbers above; pick = the best 2018-22 PF, >= 200 trades):

| Version | Trades | Gross pts / trade | Win % | PF 2018-22 | PF 2023-26 | PF 2026 | Net 2018-22 ₹ | Net 2023-26 ₹ | Net 2026 ₹ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| T0 every 1-minute CHoCH / BOS the break's way | 4,487 | +1.56 | 21 | 0.82 | 0.65 | 0.56 | -3,41,903 | -10,38,524 | -3,77,100 |
| T1 first trade of the day only | 1,122 | +3.94 | 20 | 0.92 | 0.87 | 0.65 | -47,026 | -1,15,581 | -79,947 |
| T2 no new trade after a stop | 1,663 | +2.29 | 20 | 0.82 | 0.75 | 0.52 | -1,33,767 | -3,03,350 | -1,63,286 |
| T3 the stop on the 5-minute swing | 3,332 | +2.57 | 26 | 0.94 | 0.71 | 0.61 | -96,119 | -7,30,219 | -2,75,347 |
| **T4 first trade only + 5-minute swing stop (the pick)** | 1,122 | +6.86 | 36 | 1.15 | 0.93 | 0.89 | +1,28,367 | -78,211 | -31,171 |
| H hold the break to 15:20 (reference) | 1,383 | +8.22 | 51 | 1.13 | 0.98 | 1.10 | +2,33,935 | -36,941 | +42,654 |
| C the S149 frozen candidate (reference) | 644 | +15.07 | 48 | 1.82 | 1.03 | 1.36 | +3,47,237 | +23,053 | +59,007 |

**Through a weekly option 100 points in the money** (the same entry / exit minutes; CE for a long, PE for a short, strike from the index at the signal minute, the nearest weekly on or after the day; the lab's option costs - charges about 1 option point a trade):

| Signals | Trades (no option candle) | PF 2018-22 | PF 2023-26 | PF 2026 | Net 2018-22 ₹ | Net 2023-26 ₹ | Net 2026 ₹ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| H hold the break to 15:20 | 1,362 (21) | 1.11 | 0.99 | 1.28 | +1,33,659 | -10,204 | +75,950 |
| C the S149 frozen candidate | 632 (12) | 1.84 | 1.11 | 1.74 | +2,52,594 | +56,377 | +72,833 |
| T4 | 1,105 (17) | 1.20 | 1.00 | 1.09 | +1,13,525 | +387 | +14,979 |

**Reading.** 1-minute structure does work on real trend days; the noise-band break just does not tell those apart (two thirds of break days are flat or fail), and a 1-minute-swing stop inside normal noise plus chasing re-entries turn those days into many small paid losses. One trade a day with a 5-minute-swing stop removes most of that (PF 0.65 → 0.93 after 2022) but still does not clear the futures cost. Trading the signals through an ITM option keeps less of the move (delta below 1) but costs about a fifth as much; it helps most in 2026 (the futures STT): the candidate goes 1.36 → 1.74. Caveat: the candidate C was put together after four bots had looked at 2023-26 (S149), so its 2023-26 / 2026 numbers are a check, not a clean test.

**Open (not adopted).** C through the ITM option as a lab strategy for forward paper tracking from 2026-10-27; a better trend-day detector at the break (the band says "not sideways" on 2.3x more days than trend) is where the remaining edge is.

### S149. The band -> structure intraday research programme (six research bots on one bench, plus "trade structure after the noise-band break"): a sideways band, a break, CHoCH / BOS entries and exits, continuation indicators, a higher-timeframe exit, moving windows and open interest - about 3,500 versions on NIFTY near-month futures, 1-minute, 2018 - 2026-10. Measured: nothing chosen on 2018-22 stays profitable on 2023-26; the edge is in the band break itself on a trend day, held, and it has shrunk (gross 5 - 7 points a trade in 2018-22, 2 - 4 since) while the cost rose (4 -> 8 -> 15 points); the one family that recurs in every bot is Jilly's noise band broken and confirmed by a BOS, near break-even after 2022. Nothing adopted

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only; no strategy changed |
| Code | `research/strategy_lab_v2/tools/bandlab.py` (the shared bench), `bandlab_entry.py`, `bandlab_exit.py`, `bandlab_filter.py`, `bandlab_window.py`, `bandlab_oi.py`, `jilly_break_struct.py`; the bots' write-ups: `results/bandlab/{inventory,entry,exit,filter,window,oi}.md` and their CSV grids (git-ignored) |

**The ask** (the user, 2026-10-26): "look at all our profitable intraday strategies; a band between which the day is sideways; once it is crossed use CHoCH or BOS to enter and exit, or enter on the band cross and exit on CHoCH / BOS, or a continuity indicator, or switch to a higher timeframe after some profit; work out what is required and create different bots to research it"; then "consider moving windows and OI and its crossovers"; then "once Jilly's noise band is broken it is not a sideways day - then check Strategy 1 / 2 / 3 or plain CHoCH / BOS on 1 / 5 minutes".

**The bench** (tools/bandlab.py): futures 1-minute from the store; 1 lot; 0.5 pt slippage a side + charges by date (a round trip ~4.3 points 2018-22, ~8 in 2025, ~13 - 15 from 2026-04, the STT rise); bands or15 / or30 / previous day H-L / Jilly UB-LB / VWAP +/- 1 or 2 sd / the 5-minute EMA bands / a 60-minute Donchian; structure (core.foundation) on 1 / 5 / 15-minute bars; entries cross, cross-then-structure, structure-outside; exits opposite CHoCH, swing trail, back inside, EMA, escalate to a higher timeframe after a profit; flat 15:20. Every bot: selection on 2018-22 only (min trade counts), 2023-26 and 2026 read without re-picking, gross points against the hurdle.

| Bot | Question | Versions | Its 2018-22 pick → 2023-26 / 2026 | What survives both periods |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 inventory | which lab intraday books are profitable since 2018, what they share | 98 books | - | 7 books PF >= 1.1 (ST124, ST111, ST110, ST123, ST117, ST121, ST122), all weaker after 2022, two losing; winners: a volatility-scaled opening-range band, one close outside it, entry 09:45 - 11:00, held to 15:15 - 15:20 (median 175 minutes), shorts better; 1-minute structure entries after a band signal lose (ST114 - ST118 PF 0.65 - 0.77) |
| 2 bands x entries | which band, which entry | 848 | Jilly, cross then 5m BOS, exit 5m CHoCH: PF 1.95 → 0.91 / 0.79 (gross 23 → 4.5 points) | only Jilly bands (median PF 1.21 / 0.90; every other band 0 - 2 gross points); 12 Jilly versions PF > 1 in both, best "Jilly, any 1m BOS outside the band" 1.30 / 1.14 / 1.14 (2026) but below cost in 2025 and found by looking at 2023-26 |
| 3 exits | structure / swing / inside / EMA / escalation, band stop | 456 | 15m CHoCH on closes (= hold to 15:20): PF 1.08 → 0.85 / 0.64 | none: escalating to 5 / 15m after a profit lifts winners 75 → 80 - 83 points and losers too; EMA exits are the worst; any exit inside ~1.5 hours loses; the band stop barely matters |
| 4 filters | what known at entry separates real breaks | 6 pre-set filters on 8 systems | 5m-EMA band cross + big day range + 15m trend: PF 1.76 → 0.74 / 0.37 | day range, previous day, 5m trend: strong in 2018-22, gone after; 15m trend with the trade mildly right in both; leads seen after the fact: 5m ADX <= 20 (1.16 / 1.07), entries 10:30 - 11:59 (1.27 / 0.99) |
| 5 moving windows | Donchian 30 - 240, Keltner, Bollinger, compression box, rolling VWAP | 920 | Donchian 240, cross then 5m structure, escalate: PF 1.43 → 0.96 / 0.69 (a spike: neighbours 0.93 - 1.06) | none that clears cost: Keltner 20 x 1.5 / 2.5 PF 1.01 - 1.11 / 1.00 - 1.05; every moving family's median below 1 after 2022; the compression box < 1 in all 160 runs |
| 6 open interest | futures OI build-up, option OI walls as a band, window PCR and PE-CE OI-change crossovers | 228 | Jilly cross then 5m structure + PE-CE 15-min OI filter: PF 2.10 → 0.90 / 0.83 | the OI wall is a poor band (price inside it 96% of the time); 5 filters lifted ~100% of systems in 2018-22 and ~56 - 78% after (+0.01 - 0.07 PF); PCR crossing 1 as an entry 1.36 → 0.80; Jilly cross-then-structure with an OI filter 1.03 - 1.18 after 2022, against ~0.93 - 1.04 without |

**After the noise-band break** (tools/jilly_break_struct.py; the first 1-minute close outside UB / LB 09:30 - 14:00 arms the day, then jilly_ext's structure trading; futures; PF 2018-22 / 2023-26 / 2026): plain CHoCH / BOS 1m 0.79 / 0.63 / 0.51 (6,853 trades, +1.9 gross points); plain 5m 1.11 / 0.89 / 0.78 (+5.9); Strategy 1 SETUPs 0.71 / 0.57 / 0.46; Strategy 2 (5m) 1.04 / 0.83 / 0.87; Strategy 3 0.80 / 0.68 / 0.76; reversals too ("both") worse everywhere. With Jilly's full signal as the confirmation instead (= Strategies 114 - 118) the same trades do a little better, and Strategy 2 SETUPs in the signal's direction are the only system positive in both periods (191 trades, 1.20 / 1.13 / 1.94 - Strategy 117).

**Reading.** (1) The tradable part is the band break on a trend day, held - Jilly's volatility-scaled band is the only band that carries it (opening range, VWAP, previous day, EMA bands, Donchian, Keltner, Bollinger, compression boxes and OI walls all earn 0 - 4 gross points a trade). (2) Slicing the day into 1- or 5-minute structure trades, or exiting early on any indicator, spends the edge on costs; 1-minute structure never clears the hurdle. (3) The gross edge of the break itself has roughly halved since 2023 while the futures cost has tripled, so every pick made on 2018-22 fails afterwards. (4) What recurs in four bots: Jilly's band broken, confirmed by a BOS on 1 / 5-minute bars, one trade a day, held long (escalated exit or 15:20), optionally with the 15-minute trend or the futures OI build-up agreeing - near break-even to slightly positive after 2022, never chosen by a pre-set rule.

**Open (not adopted).** (a) Freeze one candidate now - Jilly band + the first 5-minute BOS that way + the escalate exit (1m CHoCH, 15m after 20 points), one a day - and judge it only on data after 2026-10-26 (paper). (b) The cost side is now the binding constraint: the same signals traded through an ITM option (STT on the premium, not the notional) are untested. (c) ADX <= 20 at the break and entries 10:30 - 11:59 need a fresh pre-registration.

### S148. How to arrive at a 1-minute intraday strategy like the EMA bands - measured in three steps: NIFTY futures mean-revert at 1 - 5 minutes and trend only weakly at 15 - 60 minutes (and less since 2023); a futures round trip costs 4 - 5 points to 2024, 8 in 2025 and 15 from April 2026; the EMA-band family on 1-minute bars, intraday, earns at most +5 - 6.5 gross points a trade, so nothing clears the cost after 2022 except one marginal version. The EMA bands work positionally, not intraday

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only |
| Code | `research/strategy_lab_v2/tools/intraday_search.py` (the grid: results/intraday_search_grid.csv) |

**Step 1 - is there anything to follow?** NIFTY near-month futures, 1-minute, same-session returns: corr(last h minutes, next h minutes) 1 min -0.017 / -0.036 (2018-22 / 2023-26), 5 min -0.018 / -0.019, 15 min +0.040 / -0.000, 30 min +0.033 / +0.005, 60 min +0.049 / +0.018; variance ratio 0.94 / 0.92 (5 min) ... 0.88 / 0.86 (60 min) - below 1 at every horizon (intraday NIFTY reverts more than it trends in variance terms). The 30-minute persistence was strongest 09:45 - 10:30 and 12:00 - 13:30 in 2018-22 (+0.06) and is gone there in 2023-26 (-0.01 / -0.02).

**Step 2 - the hurdle** (one lot, 0.5 pt slippage a side + the futures charges by date, jilly.py): 2019 4.0 points, 2022 5.1, 2025 8.0, 2026 15.2 (STT 0.05% of the sell leg from 2026-04-01 alone is about 12 points at 24,000).

**Step 3 - the family.** EMA 20 / 50 / 100 / 200 x band 2 / 3 / 4 mean distances x distance window 20 / 60, read as a breakout (a close through a band enters that way) or a fade (a close back inside enters against it), with or without the S146 slope filter (60 minutes); entries 09:30 - 14:30, out on a close back through the EMA or at the 15:20 open: 96 versions. Breakout readings have a positive gross edge (median +1.8 points a trade), fades a negative one (-1.9). The best gross is +5 - 6.5 points a trade, held 50 - 90 minutes - about the hurdle before 2025, under it after. Chosen on 2018-22 (net PF, >= 300 trades): EMA 100, x4, window 20, breakout, slope filter - PF 1.21 (305 trades, ₹+57,663) → 2023-26 PF 0.74 (177, ₹-68,651). The only version positive in both halves: EMA 200, x4, window 60, breakout with the slope filter - 489 trades, +6.6 gross points, PF 1.02 / 1.07, ₹+6,980 / ₹+22,400.

**Reading - how to arrive at one.** (1) Measure the edge at the holding horizon before writing rules (here: weak trend at 15 - 60 min, reversal at 1 - 5 min). (2) Price the hurdle in points for the instrument and the date. (3) Only rules whose average gross move per trade is a multiple of the hurdle survive: slow EMAs, wide bands, long holds, few trades - which is why Strategy 138 works positionally (its multi-day trends pay; same-day exits lose, S146) and its 1-minute intraday cousins do not. (4) Choose on the early years, read the late ones (the in-sample pick fails here). Untested routes: the same 1-minute signals traded through an ITM option (STT on the premium, not the notional), or holding the futures signal overnight.

### S147. "Nifty CHoCH to CHoCH in Plain English" (the user sent it again - "build this, check if this exists"): it exists as Strategies 25 - 28, tested on 2026-06-23 - 09-14 only; run on the download store's option data from 2018 (Strategies 147 - 150), all four lose (PF 0.72 - 0.84); the 5-point premium trail takes the winners out at ~3 points while the 5% stop costs ~19 points

|---|---|

**Exists.** Strategies 25 - 28 (c2c.py, the "CHoCH to CHoCH" family on the lab page) are this document rule for rule: the 5-minute structure bearish, a fresh swing high within +/- 50 points of the VWAP anchored at the pre-break peak, not the flip candle, the day not down 0.6%, window PCR < 1.3 (ST25 / ST27), the ITM1 put of the nearest monthly at least 15 days out at the next candle's open, one at a time, carried overnight; exits on a bullish flip, the 5% premium stop and the 5-point trail, the 15:15 ladder (profit > 40%, loss > 2%, PCR > 0.95). Not modelled: the leg's delta / gamma / vega / bid-ask guard rails (no Greeks or quotes in the data). Their option prices and OI come from the lab's full-chain files, 2026-06-23 - 09-14 only: 31 - 47 trades there; the "All data" runs skip 500 - 660 signals.

**Build.** Strategies 147 - 150 = ST25 - ST28's settings with the option side from D:/nifty_data (c2c_store.py): expiries from the store's folders (monthly = the last of each month), the put's 5-minute candles from its 1-minute rows, OI = the last 1-minute print at or before the candle the same session; regular strike files only (strikes within 250 points of the index day range - the 300-point PCR window is usually a little short; the .wide files left out for memory). Signals unchanged (the index 5-minute candles, the lab's cache to 2026-10-09).
