Skipped in ST147: 279 signals with the PCR not computable (fewer than 9 strikes with OI in the window - 2018 - 19 mostly), 61 while a put was open. Exits, ST147: 277 trail stops averaging ₹+222, 75 stop losses averaging ₹-1,198, 21 bullish flips ₹-703 each; gross PF 1.01, charges ₹79 a trade. ST148: 763 trail stops ₹+172 each, 193 stop losses ₹-1,236 each; gross PF 0.88. Average holding 72 / 112 minutes.

**Reading.** The retest entry is not the problem so much as the exits: a 5-point trail on an ITM put's premium closes a winner at about 3 points of profit (₹170 - 220 a lot), while the 5% stop on a ₹250 - 400 premium costs 12 - 20 points; the ladder and the bullish flip rarely act. The window ST25 was judged on (35 trades) is the one stretch where it shows a profit; over eight years it does not.

**Open (not adopted).** The exits are where to look (a wider trail, or the structure exit alone), each choice made on 2018 - 22 and read on 2023 - 26.

### S146. Why the EMA distance bands (Strategy 138) fail - and a correction chosen on 2018 - 23 (Strategy 146); plus the combined book with the RSI option book (Strategy 141) instead of / beside Strategy 142. Measured: the band breakouts fail when the EMA has not turned their way, and 2026's bear market with sharp rallies turns that into losing longs with the widest bands on record; taking only breakouts with the EMA's own slope (plus a breakeven stop) was the 2018 - 23 pick and holds on 2024 - 26 (PF 1.14 → 1.19, drawdown ₹1.98 → ₹1.35 lakh, 2026 ₹-10,717 → ₹+83,925), at ₹1 lakh less over the whole span. In the combined book, ST141 instead of ST142 makes less (₹14.0 lakh against ₹15.7 lakh)

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only; Strategy 138 unchanged (re-run identical: 1,554 trades ₹+10,12,860) |
| Code | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/pinerules.py` (opt-in `slope` / `be`, `_trades_emab_x`), `st146.py` |

**Combined book with the RSI option book** (the user: "don't we have RSI in the combined strategy - do it"; one position per side, first entry wins, as S145): with ST142 2,742 trades, PF 1.277, ₹+15,74,671, max DD ₹-2,22,168 (the option book 124 trades ₹+2,47,298); with ST141 (CHoCH + RSI 70) instead 2,682 trades, PF 1.250 (1.29 / 1.21), ₹+14,02,994, DD ₹-2,09,360 (46 trades ₹+2,19,868 - it fires 96 times in 8 years); with both (ST142 first on a tie) 2,670 trades, PF 1.269, ₹+14,88,629, DD ₹-1,87,247 (134 option trades ₹+3,58,283).

**The failure pattern** (1,556 trades with their entry context, points less 1 pt, before charges). (1) The edge is the multi-day trend: trades still open overnight earn PF 4.8, those closed the same day PF 0.24 - an outcome, not an entry filter, but it says the losses are breakouts that reverse within the day. (2) The EMA's own direction at the break separates them: the EMA moving the trade's way by 0.05 - 0.15% over the last hour PF 2.12 (2018-22 2.32, 2023-25 1.56, 2026 3.65); flat 1.32; against the break 1.40 overall but 0.31 in 2026; more than 0.15% (already extended) 0.56 on 53 trades. (3) 2026: NIFTY futures 26,300 (Dec-2025) → 22,330 (Mar) → 24,438 (Jul) → 22,582 (Oct) - a falling market with sharp rallies: longs PF 0.79, shorts 1.73; the bands are wide (the entry is on average 122 points from the EMA exit, against 40 - 110 in earlier years) so each failed break costs more (average loss ₹4,827, the largest). (4) Volatility rank, RSI, the hour, how far beyond the band the close is: no consistent split. Note the diagnosis read all years including 2026; the candidates below came from it, so 2024 - 26 is a check on a choice made on 2018 - 23, not a clean out-of-sample test.

**Correction test** (nine candidates fixed before the run; the pick = the best 2018 - 23 return / max drawdown; costs as Strategy 138):

By year, base → pick (₹): 2018 +45,127 → +44,861 · 2019 +1,00,630 → +77,266 · 2020 +2,14,722 → +1,51,847 · 2021 +2,94,901 → +2,35,448 · 2022 +77,676 → +88,130 · 2023 +74,763 → +87,316 · 2024 +95,731 → -2,036 · 2025 +1,22,938 → +1,42,798 · 2026 -10,717 → +83,925. As a lab strategy (Strategy 146, the bar-by-bar engine): 1,382 trades, ₹+9,12,746, 2026 ₹+84,196; lab pages PF 1.34 all, 1.46 (2018-22), 1.23 (2023-26; ST138 1.18).

**Reading.** The theory holds up: a band break against or without the EMA's own turn is mostly noise, and in a falling, high-volatility market the long breaks are rallies that fade. Requiring the slope removes 12% of trades, keeps the shorts and cuts the 2026 long losses from ₹1.11 lakh to ₹42k; the breakeven stop trims drawdown. The cost is the strong trend years 2020 - 21 (₹1.2 lakh less) and 2024 (flat). Fixed stops and a daily-regime filter both make it worse. "Slope with, at most 0.15%" did a little better on 2024 - 26 but was not the pre-set pick.

**Open (not adopted).** Judge Strategy 146 forward from 2026-10-26; the long side in a falling market is still its weak spot (2026 longs ₹-42k).

### S145. The combined book with the best 15-minute option-selling book added (Strategy 142), one position per side (bullish / bearish), first entry wins; and a confluence score - how many other books hold a position the same way minus the other way at a trade's entry. Measured 2018-01 - 2026-10, 1 lot: the book makes ₹+15.7 lakh at PF 1.28 (1.32 / 1.23). Trades with 2+ more books agreeing than opposing are clearly stronger in both halves (PF 1.83 / 2.01, avg ₹+2,068 against about ₹+580); score +1, 0 and -1 are about the same. The rule chosen on 2018-22 ("only with agreement") fails on 2023-26. Nothing adopted

|---|---|

**Build** (the user's request: "pick the best of these and run it along with the others; one trade per side at a time, first entry wins; then a variant scoring the trade by multiple strategies' signals - more similar signals, strong or weak - figure out how"). The best of Strategies 139 - 145 by its weaker half with 100+ trades in each: Strategy 142 (G_hardstop K5; PF 1.64 / 1.52; the others 0.79 - 1.44 in their weaker half, ST141 on 48 trades a half). "Side" read as the market direction: bullish = long futures or a sold PE, bearish = short futures or a sold CE; at most one open per side, so a bullish and a bearish trade can be open together (196 times). Ties on the same minute: Jilly, IBS, Basis z, CPR + ORB, EMA bands, the option book. Score of a trade = for each OTHER book, +1 if its standalone book holds a position the same way at the trade's entry minute (opened before it, not yet closed - known at that minute), -1 if the other way; 6 books, so -5 ... +5. The option book's ₹ are per lot of 65 with its own costs (S144).

The selection rule set before reading (the best 2018-22 return / drawdown) picks "only with agreement" (13.2 against 9.2 for every signal), which falls to 1.65 on 2023-26 against 2.92 - agreement as a filter does not hold. The score's one consistent finding is the strong end: trades with two or more books more agreeing than opposing (296, 6% of trades) earn PF 1.83 and 2.01 in the two halves and three times the average trade; it was found with both halves on the table. The score is driven by the long-holding books (EMA bands and IBS are in a position most of the time), so "agreement" mostly means agreeing with them.

**Open (not adopted).** Pre-register "score >= +2 -> 2 lots" (or a separate high-confluence book) and judge it on data after 2026-10-23 only. CPR + ORB loses inside the book again (S142 / S143).

### S142. Jilly + the user's four Pine rules (IBS overnight, basis z-score overnight, narrow CPR + ORB, EMA distance bands 5m) run as one futures book - one position at a time, the first signal takes it, the rest ignored while it is open. Measured 2018-01 - 2026-10, 1 lot: the combined book makes ₹+14.6 lakh at PF 1.26 (1.34 / 1.18 by half), against ₹+25.8 lakh if all five could hold their own lot; the EMA bands (positional) hold the position most of the time and block 1,062 of the other books' signals. Research only

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only; nothing live and no lab strategy changed |
| Code | `research/strategy_lab_v2/tools/combo_book.py` |

**Build** (the user's request after the four Pine scripts: "along with Jilly and these 4, validate what will happen if all are run together - whichever gets the first signal takes the trade, no trade if a trade from another exists"). NIFTY near-month futures, 1-minute, the download store (to 2026-10-23), 1 lot of 65, 0.5 pt slippage a side + futures charges by date (jilly.py). A decision at a bar's close fills at the next bar's open. Jilly = Strategy 111's futures trades (10 x ATR stop, 1R, 15:20). IBS = the Pine rules at the 15:19 bar, in at 15:20, out next session 09:16 open; skipped on the contract's expiry day (it does not exist overnight) and before a weekday holiday (the "tomorrow is holiday" box). Basis z = futures vs the index close at 15:19, minus 6.5% x days-to-expiry / 365 (the contract's own expiry), z against the previous 60 sessions, |z| > 2; in at 15:20, out next session 15:20; not on expiry day. CPR + ORB = the Pine rules on the previous two sessions' high / low / last close, the previous 20 CPR widths, the 09:15 open beyond today's CPR, a close beyond the 15-minute range; BUILD (the Pine rule gives no exit): stop at the far side of the opening range (a touch exits at the next open), else out at 15:20. EMA bands = 5-minute bars, EMA 50, 20-bar mean |close - EMA|, x 3; in at the next 5-minute bar's open, out at the next bar's open after a close back through the EMA; positional (held overnight); out at the last close of a contract before the roll (33 times). Ties on the same entry minute go Jilly, IBS, Basis z, CPR + ORB, EMA bands; there are 127, nearly all IBS and Basis z both entering at 15:20 (108 of the 146 Basis z signals). With Basis z ahead of IBS the book takes 112 Basis z trades instead of 33: 2,699 trades, PF 1.262 (1.30 / 1.23), ₹+14,91,384 - about the same.

**Reading.** Every book but CPR + ORB is positive in both halves on its own; CPR + ORB loses from 2023 (PF 0.92) and loses inside the book too. "First signal wins" costs about ₹11 lakh against running each book with its own lot: the EMA bands hold positions for days and take most of the slot (the Jilly trades left after blocking earn PF 1.15 instead of 1.27). The four Pine rules came with their numbers already set (the user's / their sources'); no parameter was chosen here, but how they were first found is not known, so the 2018 - 22 / 2023 - 26 split is a check, not an out-of-sample test. Basis z rests on 146 trades (33 inside the book).

**Open (not adopted).** (a) Dropping CPR + ORB from the book; (b) a priority rule instead of first-come (for example intraday books before the positional EMA bands), or one lot per book; (c) the EMA bands and IBS rules as lab strategies with charts. Each is a change to what trades get taken - to be agreed before it is run.

### S141. Strategy 1 filtered by Jilly's noise band / the opening range (the user: "if we don't trade within the Jilly band or the day range it will likely save false trades"). Measured on Strategy 1's saved futures trades 2018-01 - 2026-10: trades inside the band are worse, and keeping only trades beyond the band the trade's way lifts PF 0.30 → 0.49 in both halves - but it does that by dropping 91% of the trades, and what is left still loses before costs (gross PF 0.65). The opening range does not separate them. Strategy 1 stays a loser either way; nothing adopted

| Code | none (an analysis of `results/ST1/all-data_1m`; the band from `strategies/jilly.py` engine, Strategy 111's settings without the vol filter) |

Direction = the trade's signal (BULLISH / BEARISH). 8,266 of 8,326 futures trades fall on days with a band.

The saved weekly ATM option run covers only 2026-06-30 - 2026-10-09 (566 trades, the options cache): B kept 70 trades at PF 0.80 against 0.78 for all; too few to read.

**Reading.** The user's idea holds in direction: a Strategy 1 trade taken while the future is inside the noise band loses more per rupee (PF 0.28) than one taken outside it (0.42), and the gap is the same in both halves; taking only the trade's own side of the band is better still (0.49). But the average trade still loses about ₹800 - 900, before costs too (gross PF 0.58 - 0.65): the filter saves money by trading a tenth as often, not by finding winners. Inside / outside the opening range makes no useful difference.

**Open (not adopted).** The band as a filter on a different, profitable entry is the useful part (as in Strategy 111 itself); on Strategy 1 it cannot turn the result positive.

### S140. Jilly on the option's own chart (Strategies 120 - 123): why it enters late, and checking every 1 / 5 minutes instead of every 15. Measured: the noise band, not the 15-minute grid, makes the entries late - at the checkpoints before a signal the band is still failing 87% of the time (ST120), and the median entry is at a premium already ~50 - 70% away from its 09:15 open; checking every minute moves the median entry only ~10 minutes earlier (10:15 → 10:05); results are mixed (ST122 out-of-sample PF 1.045 → 1.135, ST123 1.214 → 1.153). Nothing adopted

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only; no strategy changed (`ck_step` is an existing setting) |
| Code | none (`jilly.ck_step` in `strategies/jilly_opt.py`) |

**The entry rule** (the user asked what it is): at checkpoints `ck_first` ... 14:00 every `ck_step` minutes (09:45 every 15 for ST120 - 122, 10:00 for ST123), a leg is bought when its close is above its OR high + `atr_buf` x ATR(9, its 5-minute bars), above UB(key) = max(09:15 open, previous close) x (1 + `band_k` x sigma(key)) and above its VWAP (the sell: the mirror); fill at the next minute's open; the first signal per leg per day.

**Why late** (signal days, the checkpoints before the one that fired; same direction): ST120 - band failing 37%, OR + band 29%, OR + band + VWAP 21%, OR only 11%; ST122 - 37 / 29 / 23 / 9%; ST123 - OR + band 35%, band 31%, all three 18%, OR only 15%. Median premium move from the leg's 09:15 open at the entry: ST120 58%, ST122 48%, ST123 70%. On the future sigma is a fraction of a percent; on an at-the-money premium (20 - 40% intraday moves) band_k x sigma puts the band tens of percent from the open, and max(open, previous close) lifts it further (options often open below yesterday's close).

### S139. Jilly on the option's own chart, both sides (Strategies 120 - 123): what a later opposite signal does to a trade - close it, reverse it, close on the other leg's opposite signal, or (after a stop) take the opposite signal. Measured 2020-02 - 2026-10: the situations are rare (0 - 37 trades of 800 - 1,672 over 6.7 years) and every logic moves PF by 0.02 or less; the in-sample pick beats the as-built book out of sample on one of four books (ST122, +₹11,766). Nothing adopted

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only; Strategies 112 / 119 - 123 re-run identical after the code change (same trades; nets within ₹1 rounding of the saved results) |
| Code | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/jilly_opt.py` (opt-in `opp`, `_run_opp`, `_stops`; `legs()` now keeps every checkpoint signal of the day in `cands`) |

**Build** (the user's request: "in options standalone, if there is a sell in CE and a buy comes in, or close CE - add these kinds of logics and check"). Each leg (CE, PE) still trades its day's first signal; every later checkpoint signal (09:45 ... 14:00, same rules) is now kept. `opp`: "close" - an opposite signal on the same leg (a buy while the leg is sold, a sell while it is bought) closes it at the next open; "reverse" - closes it and trades the leg the other way from there (same stop / target rules), once a day; "xclose" - a signal on the other leg pointing the other market way (CE buy / PE sell = up, CE sell / PE buy = down) closes it; "both" = reverse + xclose; "flip" - after the trade is stopped out, the first later opposite signal on the same leg is traded (once a day); "flip_x" = flip + xclose. The stop / target is checked before the signal on the same minute. Selection rule set before reading: the highest in-sample PF (2020-02 - 2023), read on 2024 - 2026-10.

| | flip_x | 804 | 1.192 | 1.210 | 1.201 | +1,81,799 | -67,942 | |

**Reading.** An opposite signal on the same leg almost never meets an open trade: a buy needs a close above ORH + buffer, a sell below ORL - buffer, and the trade's stop (the OR midpoint, or a multiple of the leg's ATR) sits between the two, so it is hit first - 0 times on ST120 / ST121 / ST123, 13 on ST122 (10 x ATR stop). The case the user describes happens after the stop: "flip" takes it, 4 - 22 times. The other leg's opposite signal closes 6 - 37 trades. With so few events every change is within 0.02 PF and a few thousand rupees a year; out of sample the pre-set pick is better than as built on ST122 only (1.045 → 1.054), equal on ST120 / ST123 and worse on ST121.

**Open (not adopted).** Nothing to take forward on this evidence; the events are too rare to judge. A looser trigger (an opposite close beyond the OR midpoint rather than the full breakout) would act more often - untested.

### S138. Jilly (Strategy 111) with an extra entry filter - RSI 14, SMA 20 direction, VWAP +/- 1 / 2 sigma - on top of its own conditions, chosen on the early years and read on 2023 - 26. Measured: nothing clearly helps. RSI > 50 and SMA 20 direction change almost nothing (the breakout already implies them); "beyond VWAP + 2 sigma" has the best early PF on futures (1.98) and loses out of sample (0.91); the only filter better in both halves of both books, "not RSI(1m) 70 / 30, wait for the next check", adds about 0.03 - 0.05 PF - within noise, and found after seeing both halves. Nothing adopted

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only; Strategy 111 unchanged (ST110 / ST111 / ST112 / ST119 re-run identical after the code change) |
| Code | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/jilly.py` (opt-in `xfilter` / `xmode`, `_xfilters`, `_xok`) |

**Build** (the user's request: "check if RSI helps or SMA 20 direction helps alongside the existing ones, even check 1 sigma or 2 sigma VWAP"). At each of Strategy 111's checks (09:45, 10:00 ... 14:00) a signal that passes the existing rules must also pass the filter; "veto" = a failing first signal ends the day, "wait" = the next check that passes both is taken. Long rules (a short is the mirror): `rsi1_50` / `rsi5_50` RSI 14 > 50 on the future's 1- / 5-minute closes; `rsi1_70` / `rsi5_70` RSI < 70; `sma1` / `sma5` SMA 20 rising (now vs one candle back - the user's "slope"); `vw1_beyond` / `vw2_beyond` close above VWAP + 1 / 2 sigma; `vw1_inside` / `vw2_inside` close below VWAP + 1 / 2 sigma (sigma = the session VWAP's volume-weighted standard deviation of hlc3). BUILD: RSI / SMA continuous across sessions and contract rolls; the 5-minute bar is the one completing at the check (the checks fall on 5-minute boundaries). Strategy 111's exits unchanged. Selection rule set before reading: the highest in-sample PF (futures 2018 - 22; ATM option 2020 - 22).

**Reading.** The pre-set rule picks "beyond VWAP + 2 sigma, veto" on futures (IS 1.98 → OOS 0.91, below the as-built 1.106) and "inside VWAP + 1 sigma, veto" on the option (8 in-sample trades - no evidence; the rule had no minimum trade count, which it should have had). RSI > 50 is nearly always true at a Jilly breakout (the 1-minute version changes 0 - 2 trades). SMA 20 direction removes a few trades and lowers PF slightly in both books. Vetoes cut trades hard and mostly cut profit; "wait" versions stay near the as-built numbers. "rsi1_70 wait" (skip a signal while RSI(1m) is at 70+ / 30-, take the next check) is the one filter above as-built in both halves of both books (futures 1.446 → 1.477 / 1.106 → 1.152, net +₹27,202; option 1.420 → 1.476 / 1.154 → 1.177, net +₹8,960), but it was seen with both halves on the table and the gain is small against the trade-to-trade noise.

**Open (not adopted).** If wanted, pre-register "rsi1_70 wait" as a candidate and judge it on data after 2026-10-23 only (forward paper trading); the in-sample selection for any other filter should require at least half the as-built trade count.

### S137. Lab v2 Strategies 129 / 130 - Strategy 111 with scale-out lots (+20 / +30 premium points) and with a reverse trade when the trade fails back inside UB / LB. Measured 2018 - 2026: both lose against Strategy 111. The +20 / +30 lots lose on their own (PF 0.75 - 0.87) and take the three-lot book from PF 1.26 to 1.00; the "failure" (3 closes back across the line) fires on 80% of trades, cutting them early loses more than holding them, and the reverse trades lose too (futures PF 1.27 → 0.97). Nothing adopted

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only; Strategy 111 and the other Jilly strategies unchanged (re-run before / after the code change: ST110, ST111, ST112, ST119 identical trade counts and net) |
| Code | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/jilly.py` (`scale_pts`, `fail_rev`, `_walk_fail`, `_reverse_of`), `st129.py`, `st130.py` |

**Build** (the user's request: "enter 3 lots, exit one at 20 points, exit 2nd at 30 points", points on the option premium; then "one lot per side, if the sell fails - it goes above green or below red and sustains - take the reverse trade", green = LB, red = UB on the Jilly chart). ST129: Strategy 111's ATM / 1-ITM option bought as 3 lots; lots 1 and 2 are resting limits at entry + 20 / + 30 premium points (filled at the limit, or the minute's open when it opens beyond), any minute from the entry to before the final exit; lot 3 and any lot whose limit is not reached exit on Strategy 111's rules (OR-midpoint stop and 2R on the future, 15:20). Options only. ST130: one lot; a long (taken above UB) fails when the future closes below UB(key) for 3 consecutive 1-minute closes, a short (below LB) when it closes above LB(key) 3 times (BUILD: "sustains" = 3 closes, the n = 3 the user chose for Strategies 88 / 89); checked after the bar's stop / target touch; the trade is closed at the next open and the other side taken there (ATM / 1-ITM from the spot at the failure minute for options), its stop / target by the same rules from the failure bar, flat 15:20; a reverse does not reverse again. Futures and options.

The failure fires on 628 of 789 futures trades (430 of 578 option trades): UB(key) / LB(key) widen through the day with sigma, and a breakout usually closes back inside the line within the first minutes. Those trades do lose as a group when held (PF 0.61), but closing them at the failure loses more (the stop is often further away than the next few minutes' move back), and the reverse trades lose as well (futures PF 0.75; 583 of 628 run to 15:20). Option books: ATM ST130 2023 - 26 is break-even (PF 1.00) where ST111 made PF 1.15.

Lab pages (cache to 2026-10-09): ST129 ATM PF 0.99, 1-ITM 1.00; ST130 futures 0.97, ATM 0.99, 1-ITM 1.00.

**Open (not adopted).** A stricter failure (more closes, or a close beyond the OR midpoint rather than the band line) would fire less often; untested, and any count must be chosen on 2018 - 22 and read on 2023 - 26 only.

### S136. Lab v2 Strategies 125 - 128: gap days (0.2% and more) classified by the day's first CHoCH / BOS on the NIFTY spot as "follow" (the break goes the gap's way) or "reverse", the 150-point ITM option bought that way. Measured 2018-02 - 2026-10: all four lose, before costs as well (gross PF 0.91 - 0.98), in both halves; neither follow nor reverse has an edge; Strategy 124's opening-range read stays the better gap-day entry

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only; no live strategy changed |
| Code | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/gapstruct.py`, `st125.py` - `st128.py` |

**Build** (the user's request after S135: "identify if it's gap days and whether price follows or reverses using CHoCH or BOS and backtest"). Gap = the 09:15 open against the previous session's 15:15 close, a gap day at 0.2% or more (Strategy 124's small-gap boundary; BUILD). The Foundation engine (Strategy 1's rules, touch) on the index's 1- or 5-minute candles, warmed on 2 sessions; the day's FIRST CHoCH or BOS confirmed after the 09:15 candle and closing by 14:00 classifies the day and is traded (one a day): the 150-point ITM weekly (CE up / PE down) bought at the open of the minute after the break candle closes. Exits: "premium" = Strategy 124's (stop 70%, 80% trail of the highest close once above 105%, flat 15:15); "structure" = the next CHoCH against on the same candles, flat 15:15. 0.1% of premium a side + charges by date; 1 lot of 65. The first 1-minute break comes 14 minutes after the open (median; 55% by 09:30); the first 5-minute one 60 minutes after (19% by 09:45).

| Strategy | Trades | Win % | Net PF | Gross PF | PF 2018-22 / 2023-26 | Net ₹ | Max DD ₹ | Follow: trades, PF, net ₹ | Reverse: trades, PF, net ₹ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ST125 1m, premium exits | 1,316 | 37.3 | 0.93 | 0.98 | 0.93 / 0.93 | -1,32,067 | -1,99,199 | 948, 0.93, -95,146 | 368, 0.93, -36,921 |
| ST126 1m, next-CHoCH exit | 1,316 | 38.7 | 0.88 | 0.91 | 0.90 / 0.86 | -3,27,053 | -4,06,170 | 948, 0.93, -1,27,602 | 368, 0.76, -1,99,452 |
| ST127 5m, premium exits | 1,300 | 36.6 | 0.89 | 0.94 | 0.86 / 0.93 | -1,95,095 | -2,35,103 | 899, 0.93, -87,276 | 401, 0.82, -1,07,818 |
| ST128 5m, next-CHoCH exit | 1,300 | 42.8 | 0.90 | 0.93 | 0.91 / 0.89 | -2,57,041 | -3,23,138 | 899, 0.94, -92,130 | 401, 0.81, -1,64,911 |

At +0.2% a side (Strategy 124's stress) PF 0.90 / 0.85 / 0.86 / 0.87. Skipped 18 (1-minute) / 26 (5-minute): no ITM candles or no candle at the entry minute. Lab pages (cache to 2026-10-09): ST125 PF 0.93 t -1.0; ST126 0.87 t -1.75; ST127 0.89 t -1.5; ST128 0.90 t -1.43.

**Splits (none holds in both halves).** The first event is a BOS on about 90% of days (1-minute 1,204 of 1,316). A day opening with a CHoCH reads better (PF 0.96 - 1.07 on 112 - 158 trades) but only on 2018 - 22 (1.14 - 1.34) and loses on 2023 - 26 (0.42 - 0.92), and without its top 10 trades it is 0.16 - 0.51. "Reverse by BOS" is the worst cell everywhere (PF 0.71 - 0.88). By gap size, follow on gaps of 1% and more is 0.89 - 1.16 overall (2023 - 26 1.37 - 2.20, 2018 - 22 0.67 - 0.96; 125 - 126 trades in all); small gaps (0.2 - 0.5%) reversed lose in all four (0.65 - 0.86). By year no variant is positive in more than 3 of 9 years.

**Reading.** The first structure break after a gap does not tell which way the day goes, well enough to pay a bought option: follow and reverse lose alike, before costs. Against Strategy 124 (gross PF 1.38): its entry waits for the 09:15 - 09:45 range and a 10% break beyond it, the gap's way only, and stands down once the gap has filled - the early 1-minute break used here comes before the range is set (87% by 09:45).

**Open (not adopted).** The CHoCH-first and big-gap-follow cells are full-window observations on small counts; to test either, pre-register it on 2018 - 22 and read 2023 - 26. Using structure as a filter on Strategy 124's own entries (rather than as the entry) is untested.

### S135. Lab v2 Strategy 124: OptionsBuyer.in's NIFTY gap-and-go -> Williams chain built from the user's pasted spec and backtested 2018-02 - 2026-10. Measured: net PF 1.31 over 1,253 trades, but 1.58 on 2018 - 22 and 1.08 on 2023 - 26 (1.04 at the spec's +0.2% cost stress); the gap-and-go leg carries it, the Williams back-up is marginal (PF 0.95 without its top 10 trades); 2023 and 2025 lose

| | |
|---|---|
| Impact | Measured (below). Research only; no live strategy changed |
| Code | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/gapgo.py`, `st124.py` |

**Build.** Every number is the spec's: opening range = the highest / lowest 1-minute close 09:15 - 09:45, bands 10% of its width outside it, checks 09:46 - 12:00 in the gap's direction (gap = 09:15 open vs the previous 15:15 close); stands down on `opposite_first` (the other band breaks first) or `filled` (the index touched the previous 15:15 close before the break); the Williams back-up only after those two and only when |gap| < 0.2% or >= 0.5%: levels open +/- 0.5 x the previous day's highest-minus-lowest close, the first close beyond either from the stand-down minute to 14:29. Entry at the next minute's open of the 150-point ITM option (ATM = spot rounded to 50) of the nearest weekly on or after the day; exits on the premium's 1-minute closes, filled at the next open: stop 70% of entry, once a close is above 105% a trail at 80% of the highest close, flat 15:15; 0.1% of premium a side + option charges by date, lot 65. Choices the spec leaves open (marked BUILD in `gapgo.py`): the 09:15 price is the candle's open; "filled" = a 1-minute high / low through the previous close before the break; the trail's highest point is read on closes; on expiry day the same-day contract. 15 signals skipped (8: no candles for the ITM contract in the store; 7: no option candle at the entry minute). 1,281 signal days: 656 gap-and-go, 460 Williams after opposite_first, 165 after filled.

**Results** (index + store options, 2018-02-01 - 2026-10-23, 1 lot):

| Version | Trades | Win % | Net PF | Gross PF | w/o top-10 | 2018-22 / 2023-26 PF | Net ₹ | Max DD ₹ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| chain as specified (0.1% a side) | 1,253 | 41.5 | 1.309 | 1.383 | 1.164 | 1.58 / 1.08 | +4,53,120 | -77,056 |
| - gap-and-go trades | 639 | 43.7 | 1.420 | 1.501 | 1.179 | 1.80 / 1.09 | +3,03,687 | -33,972 |
| - Williams trades | 614 | 39.3 | 1.200 | 1.268 | 0.953 | 1.36 / 1.07 | +1,49,433 | -81,568 |
| - CE / PE | 658 / 595 | 43.5 / 39.3 | 1.290 / 1.328 | | 1.047 / 1.080 | 1.63 / 0.99 ; 1.51 / 1.17 | +2,18,832 / +2,34,288 | |
| chain, costs +0.2% a side (the spec's stress) | 1,253 | 40.9 | 1.258 | 1.329 | 1.117 | 1.51 / 1.04 | +3,87,942 | -89,870 |

Exits (as specified): 631 trail, 447 flat 15:15, 175 stop. Gap-and-go alone (no Williams) is exactly the gap-and-go row: the back-up never displaces a gap-and-go trade.

By year (PF, net ₹): 2018 1.25 +22,730 · 2019 1.64 +60,147 · 2020 1.80 +1,25,605 · 2021 1.44 +80,168 · 2022 1.65 +1,03,678 · **2023 0.89 -20,308** · 2024 1.55 +94,292 · **2025 0.88 -30,862** · 2026 (to 10-23) 1.10 +17,668.

By gap size: under 0.2% 555 trades PF 1.06; 0.2 - 0.5% 272 PF 1.12; 0.5 - 1% 303 PF 1.62; 1% and above 123 PF 2.10. By entry hour: 09 h 1.39, 10 h 1.40, 11 h 0.93, 12 h 1.26, 13 h 0.86, 14 h 1.93.

Lab pages (the lab cache ends 2026-10-09): 2018-22 686 trades +₹3,92,329 PF 1.58 t 3.99; 2023-26 566 trades +₹52,337 PF 1.07 t 0.53.

**Reading.** The edge is concentrated in the early years: from 2023 the chain is near break-even (t 0.53) and the spec's own cost stress leaves PF 1.04. The gap-and-go leg is the better half in both periods; the Williams back-up adds money only through its largest trades. Large gaps (>= 0.5%) carry most of the profit and small ones barely pay; late entries (11 h, 13 h) lose.

**Why 2023 - 26 is weaker (measured, `gapgo_why` on the same trades).** Not the costs: about ₹70 - 75 a trade in both periods, and the gross PF itself falls 1.67 → 1.13. Not the direction: the index is on the trade's side at 15:15 on 56% of trades in both. The moves got smaller: median best move after entry 0.36% → 0.25%, median day range 1.10% → 0.84%; trades on days under 0.8% range lose in both periods (PF 0.38 / 0.24) and were 26% of trades then, 46% now, while days of 1.2% and more (PF 2.25 / 3.09) fell from 43% to 23%. Gaps of 0.5% and more fell from 43% to 23% of trades and pay less (0.5 - 1%: PF 1.98 → 1.16). Exits follow: stops 12% → 16%, held to 15:15 (the winners, about ₹+2,600 / +3,200 each) 41% → 30%. By days to expiry, 2 - 3 days was the best bucket before (PF 2.21) and loses now (0.84); the cause is unquantified (the weekly expiry weekday and the number of weeklies changed in the period - not tested). Day range is not known at entry; an opening-range-width or previous-day-range filter is a candidate only, to be pre-registered on 2018 - 22.

**Open (not adopted - Rule 0 (c)).** "Gap-and-go only", "gaps >= 0.5% only" and "no entries after 11:00" all read better above, but they were seen on the full window, so they are candidates, not results; to test one honestly, pre-register it, choose on 2018 - 22 and read 2023 - 26 only.

### S131. The user's TradingView script "SG2 Options [Rules: Jilly ORB + Noise Band]" reviewed against Strategy 111: the same rules (its header's numbers are ours within rounding), plus two options - a CPR confirmation and skipping the expiry weekday. Measured: neither adds anything (the trades each removes are profitable); the script has a few build differences that can make it signal differently live

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | The Pine v6 indicator pasted by the user 2026-10-26 ("check if this has any advantages"); measured on Strategy 111's stored trades (futures 2018 →, ATM / 1-ITM 2020-02 →, to 2026-10-09). |
| Same as Strategy 111 | The noise band (k 1.25, N 20), the gap-adjusted reference, the sigma(09:45) rank filter (< 0.20 of the prior 250, none below 120 values), OR 09:15 - 09:29, the ORH / ORL + 0.3 ATR9 + band + VWAP checkpoint test at 09:45 ... 14:00, first signal only, ATM from spot at the signal bar (1-ITM alternative), the OR-midpoint stop (10 x ATR fallback) and 2R on the future, flat 15:20. Its header: IS 2020-22 PF 1.430, OOS 2023-26 1.159 (ours 1.42 / 1.13 - 1.15). |
| Its two options, measured | **CPR confirmation** (a long only when the day opens above the prior day's CPR, a short only below it): futures 782 → 431 trades, PF 1.257 → 1.211, ₹+484,165 → ₹+216,104 (the 351 trades it removes: PF 1.314, ₹+268,060); ATM 573 → 316 trades, PF 1.246 → 1.284, ₹+197,876 → ₹+118,149 (the removed 257: PF 1.206, ₹+79,727); 1-ITM PF 1.245 → 1.286, ₹+226,989 → ₹+139,041. **Skip expiry day**: ATM 573 → 456 trades, PF 1.246 → 1.260, ₹+197,876 → ₹+165,672; 1-ITM PF 1.245 → 1.241. Both drop profitable trades: the PF barely moves, the profit falls 15 - 55%. |
| Build differences that matter live | (1) It runs on 5-minute bars and computes `key = minutes + 5`: correct only on a 5-minute chart; on any other timeframe the keys, the band and the checkpoints are wrong. (2) VWAP and ATR are the chart symbol's: on the NIFTY index chart (no volume) VWAP is not usable - it must run on the futures chart. (3) The roll day (UP = DN = O) is approximated as the day after the last chosen weekday of the month - one input (Tuesday or Thursday), while monthly expiry moved from Thursday to Tuesday in 2025 and holidays shift expiries; Strategy 111 compares the contract expiries. (4) The stop and target on its label are measured from the signal bar's close, not the next bar's open fill (a small offset). (5) It draws signals and levels only; exits (stop / target / 15:20) are not tracked, so a live position has to be managed by hand against the label. The 5-minute band and checkpoint test themselves agree with the 1-minute version at the checkpoint times. |
| Read | No advantage in the rules; the script's value is the live alerting. Keep CPR and the expiry skip off. Fix (1) - (3) before relying on it: lock it to a 5-minute futures chart, and take the roll from the contract rather than the weekday. |
| Effort | **S** (measured). |
| Priority | _Closed by measurement; nothing adopted._ Related: S120, S129. |

### S130. Trailing exits for Jilly - 19 trailing rules (ATR chandelier 2 - 6 ATR with and without an arming profit, a profit lock of 50 / 70% after 3 / 5 ATR, break-even after 2 / 4 ATR, a swing / protected-level trail, a VWAP-close exit, no target) against the spec's own exits, on four books, chosen on the early years and tested on the later ones. Measured: no trail beats the spec's exits out of sample on any of the four; tight trails lose; removing the target loses everywhere. Nothing adopted

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | A sweep script on the stored signals (no strategy change): Strategy 111 on the future (choice on 2018 - 2022, test 2023 - 2026-10-23), its ATM option with exits read on the future (choice 2020-02 - 2023, test 2024 →), Strategy 122 and 123 on the option's own chart (choice 2020-02 - 2023, test 2024 →). Each trail starts from the book's initial stop (10 x ATR on the future; the OR midpoint for the future-read option; the strategies' own stops on the option chart), replaces the target, moves only after a bar closes, never loosens; a touch exits at the next minute's open; flat 15:20 (a session ending earlier: its last close). The harness reproduces Strategy 111 exactly (789 trades, PF 1.269, ₹+508,259) - a first version carried five short-session trades to the next morning, found by that check and fixed before any number was read. 2026-10-26, the user: "find the optimal way to trail in Jilly and exit at higher profits". |
| Impact | **Out-of-sample PF, the spec's exits → the rule chosen on the early years: futures 1.106 → 1.063 (VWAP close after 2 ATR; in-sample 1.446 → 1.546); future-read ATM 1.200 → 1.051 (VWAP close after 2 ATR); Strategy 122 1.045 → 1.048 (chandelier 3 ATR; the same); Strategy 123 1.214 → 1.115 (break-even after 2 ATR).** No target (stop or 15:20): out of sample 1.078 / 1.075 / 1.005 / 1.053 - worse than the target in every book. Tight trails lose: futures chandelier 2 ATR 0.89 - 0.97, swing trail after 2 ATR 0.81, lock 70% after 3 ATR 0.92 out of sample. The nearest to the spec: a 50 - 70% lock after 5 ATR (futures OOS 1.080 - 1.082, ATM 1.122 - 1.147, ST123 1.192 - 1.194) and a 4 - 6 ATR chandelier (futures 1.069 - 1.074, ST123 1.133 - 1.160) - close, not better. |
| Read | The strategy earns on trend days by holding to the close or to a far target; any trail tight enough to protect an open profit on a normal day also exits the trend days early, and the trend days pay for everything (S129). The spec's targets already are the "exit at a higher profit": 1R at 10 x ATR on the future (a target about 2 - 3% away) and 2R from the OR midpoint on the option; removing them costs in every book, so the profit-taking is part of the edge. The in-sample winners (VWAP, break-even) won on the early years' smooth trends and did not repeat. |
| Open (the user's call) | None from this sweep. Unquantified: a partial exit (half at the target, half to 15:20). |
| Effort | **S** (measured). |
| Priority | _Closed by measurement; nothing adopted._ Related: S120, S123, S128, S129. |

### S129. Where the Jilly variants fail - a cross-variant analysis of all 13 (Strategies 110 - 123). Every variant is a trend-day strategy: the whole profit comes from the days the index moves more than 150 points open-to-close, and every other kind of day loses; 2023 and 2025 (quiet years) are the universal failures; the gains are concentrated (without their 10 best trades most option-chart versions lose). Two patterns hold in both halves with a reason behind them: no entry the day before expiry, and not buying the CE on its own breakout. Nothing adopted

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | The stored lab runs (2018 - 2026-10-09 futures, options from 2020-02) of ST110 / ST111 (futures and the future-read ATM option), ST112 / ST113 / ST119 - ST123 (the option's own chart), ST114 / ST117 (structure after the confirmation); each trade tagged with its day's index move and range, the opening gap, the futures sigma(09:45) rank, the entry hour, the side, days to expiry, the entry premium, the exit and the weekday. 2026-10-26, the user: "evaluate all Jilly variants, see where it fails, do an analysis". The index-move and range cuts use the whole day (look-ahead): they explain where the money comes from, they are not filters. |
| The mechanism | By the index's open-to-close move, PF (futures spec / future-read ATM spec / option-chart both spec / 100 - 250 defaults): below -150 points 5.71 / 5.59 / 6.20 / 5.46; -150 to -50 0.42 / 0.19 / 0.27 / 0.49; -50 to +50 **0.03 / 0.00 / 0.20 / 0.08**; +50 to +150 0.81 / 0.51 / 0.82 / 0.96; above +150 4.28 / 5.99 / 3.01 / 3.34. The futures version makes ₹+1.50M on the trend days and loses ₹-1.02M on the rest; days with a range below 200 points lose in every variant (PF 0.02 - 0.80) and days above 300 carry it (1.8 - 3.8). The breakout is right when the day trends and is held to 15:20; on a flat or modest day the same entry is a cost. |
| By year | 2023: PF 0.71 - 0.78 in every variant but Strategy 123 (1.01); 2025: 0.73 - 0.98 in all the option versions (futures 1.01 - 1.07); 2021 the best year everywhere (1.33 - 2.05). Worst months: futures 2024-12 (₹-98,229) and 2024-10; option-chart 2024-04 (₹-29k to ₹-47k), 2025-05. |
| Concentration | The 10 best trades are 50% of the futures versions' net profit, 83 - 101% of the future-read options', 71 - 337% of the option-chart versions' (above 100%: without them the version loses). The stop exits cost ₹-0.36M to ₹-1.6M per variant, paid for by the target and 15:20 exits. |
| Volatility, gaps, time, side | The futures sigma filter's bottom band (rank < 0.2) loses (PF 0.96 futures defaults) - the filter is right there; the option-chart versions use their own leg's sigma, and the futures-rank bottom band still loses for most of them (0.85 - 0.87) except the 100 - 250 leg (1.00). Gap-down days are the best (PF 1.2 - 1.9), gap-up days weak for the option-chart defaults (0.80 - 0.90). Entries 09:30 - 10:00 are weak on the ATM option-chart versions (0.81 - 0.96) and 12:31 - 14:00 weak on most (future-read ATM spec 0.56); 10:00 - 12:30 is the best window. Futures: shorts PF 1.35 against longs 1.16; options: buying the PE on its breakout PF 1.15 - 1.40, buying the CE 0.89 - 1.02 (the weakest leg in every option-chart version); selling the CE 1.12 - 1.59. |
| Expiry | Every option-chart version does best ON expiry day (PF 1.14 - 1.27) and worst the day BEFORE it (0.77 - 0.91). |
| Do they fail together | Correlation of daily net (2020-02 on): 0.44 - 0.78 between the main variants - they lose on the same days, so running several does not diversify much. |
| Do the patterns hold in both halves | Found on the whole sample, so this is consistency, not a true out-of-sample test. PF 2020/18 - 2023 → 2024 - 26: **no entry the day before expiry** - option-chart spec 1.11 → 1.17 (as is 1.09 → 1.04), 100 - 250 defaults 1.24 → 1.10 (1.20 → 1.03), Strategy 123 1.24 → 1.29 (1.21 → 1.16), future-read ATM 1.34 → 1.26 (1.33 → 1.17); **no buying the CE** - option-chart spec 1.12 → 1.11, 100 - 250 defaults 1.20 → 1.16, Strategy 123 1.46 → 1.22, future-read ATM 1.44 → 1.34; entries 10:00 - 12:30 only - better in-sample everywhere, mixed after (futures 1.61 → 1.13 against 1.35 → 1.14); no Tuesday - better before, gone after (option-chart spec 1.22 → 1.01): noise. |
| Read | The strategy is a bet that today trends; there is no rule here that recognises a trend day before 10:00 better than the breakout itself, so the losses on non-trend days are the cost of the edge, and quiet years (2023, 2025) are where that cost exceeds the trend days' gain. The two patterns with a mechanism - the day before expiry (theta is fast and the gamma payoff waits for expiry day) and the CE breakout (an option rising with the index's rallies, which have been slower than its falls) - hold in both halves; they are candidates, to be confirmed on new data or a pre-registered split, not adopted. |
| Open (the user's call) | Pre-register "no entry the day before expiry" and "no buying the CE" and test them on data after 2026-10-23 (or a held-out split chosen now); the futures sigma filter applied to the option-chart versions (it would tie them to the future again). |
| Effort | **S** (measured). |
| Priority | _Analysis recorded; nothing adopted._ Related: S120 - S128. |

The futures-read version stays the one worth paper trading. |

### S111. Lab v2 fair-value weekly book: at -6% the shorts are re-sold at once on the next weekly at the same strikes (the longs kept), and the expiry exit moves to the session before the sold expiry. Measured: ₹-51,491 (S110: ₹-82,821); the shorts ₹+204,497, the longs ₹-255,988

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/tools/cagr_book.py` (new flags `--no-stop-resell`, `--two-sessions` give S110; S110's ₹-82,821 reproduces with both), every leg in `results/cagr_book_trades.csv`. 2026-10-25, the user: "after a 6% loss, close the shorts and short again immediately next week's same strikes; and not two sessions, just the previous day of expiry". Same window (entries 2025-05-14 → 2026-10-09). |
| What changed | (1) At -6% the week's shorts are bought back and the same strikes sold at that step on the weekly after the stopped one; that is a new week with a fresh base (margin x 2 plus the longs' value then); the longs are NOT closed (the request names the shorts only; +3% still closes everything). (2) The expiry exit, and the loss roll it carries (S110), now happen on the session before the sold expiry, at its first step (09:30) - the same minute the next week's entry is checked. |
| Impact | **Measured, after costs (0.5 slippage a side, option charges, lot 65): 44 weeks (12 of them re-sold after a stop), 16 weeks with loss rolls (30 rolls); ends 20 targets, 13 stops, 11 expiry exits; the shorts ₹+204,497 (148 legs, 31 winning weeks), the longs ₹-255,988 (33 legs); total ₹-51,491.** Each change alone: re-sell after the stop only (expiry exit two sessions before) 51 weeks, ₹-72,107 (shorts +89,819, longs -161,926); day-before exit only (stop closes everything) 45 weeks, ₹-54,869 (shorts +64,605, longs -119,474). S110 (neither): ₹-82,821. |
| Read | The shorts now earn: re-selling the same strikes after a stop sells a much richer premium (the strike is in the money by then) and half of the 12 re-sold weeks reach the +3% target (W5, W10, W12, W17, W27, W32; the other 6 stop again). But the hedge is held longer and costs ₹-256k, more than the shorts make. Two quirks of "the same strikes": after a rally the re-sold strikes are far out of the money (W15 - W17: the 24800 / 25200 puts sold at 31 / 63, then 7.80 / 15.10, then 4.70 / 6.70) - the week's P&L is then only the hedge's decay and the -6% stop is hit by the hedge alone (W15, W16); and a gap goes straight through the -6% (W25: -18.2% at the 2026-04-08 09:30 step). One 17-month regime. |
| Open (the user's call) | Unquantified: re-sell at the same premium (a new strike) rather than the same strike; the -6% measured on the shorts only; the hedge size. |
| Effort | **S**. |
| Priority | _Open; numbers recorded._ Related: S109, S110. |

### S110. Lab v2 fair-value weekly book, the user's two refinements: +3% / -6% closes the whole book (the longs too), and a week in loss at "the next next day" rolls its shorts to the same strikes on the next weekly. Measured: each refinement on its own and together lose; together ₹-82,821 against S109's ₹+42,706

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/tools/cagr_book.py` (flags `--keep-longs-on-pct`, `--no-loss-roll`; the default is both refinements on), every leg in `results/cagr_book_trades.csv`. 2026-10-25, the user: "in the same strategy -6% or +3% is exit"; then "do roll over to the same strikes in the next available weekly expiry if there is a loss in a week". Same window as S109 (entries 2025-05-14 → 2026-10-09, every strike in the store). |
| What changed | (1) At +3% / -6% the week's shorts AND the 3 monthly longs are closed; the next week buys new longs. (2) Two sessions before the sold expiry, a week with a negative P&L (shorts realized + open, plus the longs' move since the week's entry) buys its shorts back and sells the same strikes on the next weekly at that step's price; the week continues with its P&L carried until +3% / -6%, a profitable "next next day", or a strike with no contract. (3) Also fixed: the steps now run the week's exits, then the long roll, then a new week's entry (the first version of (1) rolled the long before the exit check, so a week could buy a monthly and sell it in the same minute). S109's run reproduces (₹+42,706) with both flags off. |
| Impact | **Measured, after costs (0.5 slippage a side, option charges, lot 65).** S109 (longs kept at ±%, no loss roll): 73 weeks, shorts ₹+159,480, longs ₹-116,774, **₹+42,706**. (1) only: 72 weeks (1 skipped: no monthly 18 days out at the data's end), exits 49 / 17 targets / 6 stops, shorts ₹+86,827, longs ₹-130,881, **₹-44,054**. (2) only: 54 weeks, 14 rolled (24 rolls), exits 13 / 25 targets / 16 stops, shorts ₹+11,372, longs ₹-116,774, **₹-105,401**. **Both (the default now): 42 weeks, 16 rolled (33 rolls), exits 17 two sessions before expiry / 14 targets / 11 stops; shorts ₹-24,783 (150 legs), longs ₹-58,038 (36 legs); total ₹-82,821.** The largest week: W21 (2026-02-09, three rolls) shorts ₹-178,165, longs ₹+196,804, ended at +6.5% on the March fall. |
| Read | Closing the longs at +3% / -6% removes the one event that paid in S109 (the March put sold at 1,800 is now sold at the week's target, 1,219.10, and the following weeks buy new 200-premium hedges), and re-buying a monthly after every ± exit pays the hedge's decay more often. Rolling a losing week turns small weekly losses into longer positions that meet the -6% stop (16 stops with the roll alone, against 11 in S109) - the roll defers the loss, it does not remove it. With both on, the short book itself goes negative (₹-24,783). One 17-month regime, about 50 weeks. |
| Open (the user's call) | Unquantified: a roll only when the loss is small (a cap); the hedge at 1 lot or a cheaper premium; the 2024 - early 2025 weeks when the download completes. |
| Effort | **S**. |
| Priority | _Open; numbers recorded._ Related: S109. |

The 3 monthly longs (nearest 200 premium, at least 18 days out) are NOT closed with the week: under 15 days they are sold and the next monthly bought. Build choices: when the line's side flips at a week's entry the old right's longs are sold and the new right's bought; 0.5 slippage a side, option charges, lot 65. |
| Impact | **Measured, 2025-05-14 → 2026-10-09, after costs: 73 weeks traded, none skipped; exits 38 two sessions before expiry, 24 targets, 11 stops; the shorts ₹+159,480 (146 legs, 45 winning weeks); the longs ₹-116,774 (18 legs: 16 rolls, 1 side flip, 1 data end); total ₹+42,706.** The longs: 14 of the 16 rolls lose ₹-8,989 to ₹-37,257 each (the monthly bought near 200 decays to 3 - 80 by 15 days out), one roll +₹21,437 (August 2025) and one +₹670; **the side flip on 2026-03-09 sold the 2026-03-30 25600 put at 1,800.00 (bought at 200.00 on 2026-02-10; the index fell from 25,365 to 23,736) for ₹+311,244**. Without that one leg the book is about ₹-268,500. Weeks by line side: 42 put-side (2025-05 → 2026-03), 31 call-side. Worst week -14.9% (2026-02-02: the shorts +₹32,995, the long hedge -₹98,924 in a day). |
| Read | The shorts earn about ₹2,200 a week; holding three 200-premium monthlies and rolling them every month costs about ₹30,000 a roll, more than the shorts earn - the S103 hedge cost again, now paid monthly instead of weekly. The one time the hedge paid (the index 6% lower in a week and a half, 2026-02-27 → 03-09) it paid for everything, which is what a hedge is for; but the result is that one event, not the rule. |
| Open (the user's call) | Unquantified: the hedge at 1 lot; a cheaper long (100 premium); the weeks before 2025-05 when the download completes (the 2024 - early 2025 put-side year). |
| Effort | **S**. |
| Priority | _Open; first numbers recorded._ Related: S103 - S108. |

### S107. Lab v2 Strategy 94: Strategy 1's Foundation rules on the day-open ATM option's own 1-minute candles, buys only, each buy tagged by where its entry sits against the same option's previous-session range. Measured: 75% of the buys lose whichever side of the range they start on (inside 1,431 of 1,899; above 414 of 551; below 495 of 663); the whole thing ₹-288,781, PF 0.82

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/foundation_opt.py` (family module; the legs from `pdl_short.py`, the previous-session high added), `st94.py` (`fopt.sides`, `fopt.expiry_min_days`). 2026-10-25, the user: "extend Foundation 1, apply it on options directly; see how many of the buys are losses when taken inside the previous day's range". |
| Build choices | (1) Instrument: each session's day-open ATM CE and PE, nearest weekly expiry at least 1 day out, each leg its own chart (as Strategies 90 / 65). (2) Strategy 1's rules (touch, stop at the previous swing, exit at the stop or the next CHoCH, flat 15:25) on the option's candles warmed up on the two sessions before; only SETUPs whose entry candle is in the session; one trade a leg at a time; the entry at the SETUP candle's close, the exit at the engine's price (the straddle family's leg_structure fills). (3) Buys only (`sides: long`). (4) The tag: the entry premium against the same contract's previous-session high / low - inside, above, below (recorded, never acted on). (5) 0.5 slippage a side, option charges, lot 65. |
| Impact | **Measured, the store's options, 2024-09-20 → 2026-10-09, after costs: 3,113 buys, 2,340 losses (75.2%), ₹-288,781, PF 0.82; halves ₹-183,221 (PF 0.79) / ₹-105,560 (PF 0.86). By the tag: inside the previous-day range 1,899 buys, 1,431 losses (75.4%), ₹-189,170, PF 0.81; above the range 551, 414 losses (75.1%), ₹-26,291, PF 0.91; below 663, 495 losses (74.7%), ₹-73,320, PF 0.76.** Exits: 941 stops, 1,863 next CHoCH, 309 at 15:25. Inside by leg: CE 943 buys ₹-128,336 (PF 0.75), PE 956 ₹-60,834 (PF 0.88). The mean losing trade is about the same on every tag (₹-714 inside, ₹-734 above, ₹-620 below). The halves disagree on the outside tags (above: ₹+15,542 then ₹-41,833; below: ₹-59,269 then ₹-14,050). |
| Read | The answer to the question is three in four, and it is three in four everywhere: the previous-day range does not separate the buys - the loss rate is 75% inside, above and below, and the mean loss is the same size. What the run shows instead is that Strategy 1's rules do not work as a buying rule on an option's own chart at all: a 1-minute option chart makes a bullish SETUP every few minutes (3,113 in two years, six a session), most are bought at a premium that then decays or reverses, and the next CHoCH or the previous swing takes it out small; the 24.8% winners do not pay for them. This is the same lesson as Strategy 65's lineage (S70 - S75): the option's own structure is a management tool, not an entry signal. |
| The sells (measured 2026-10-25, `sides: both`, same window, numbers only - no strategy file) | 3,330 sells (the bearish SETUPs selling the option), 2,366 losses (71.1%), ₹-302,218, PF 0.81; halves ₹-155,714 / ₹-146,504 (PF 0.81 both). By the tag: inside the previous-day range 1,945 sells, 1,383 losses (71.1%), ₹-219,199, PF 0.77; above 391, 293 losses (74.9%), ₹-29,962, PF 0.82; below 994, 690 losses (69.4%), ₹-53,056, PF 0.89. By leg: CE sells ₹-93,601 (PF 0.88; below the range PF 0.97, the least bad cut of all), PE sells ₹-208,616 (PF 0.74). The mean win (about ₹+1,350) is twice the mean loss (about ₹-700) on every tag, which is why 71% losers still lands at PF 0.8 and not worse. The buys in the same run: 3,120, ₹-286,955 - unchanged in character from the buys-only run. Read: the sells lose like the buys, and the range tag does not separate them either; selling the option on its own bearish SETUP is not a premium-decay trade (the exits are the next CHoCH or the previous swing, minutes later), it is a 1-minute mean-reversion bet against the same noise. |
| A 1:1 target (measured 2026-10-25, both sides, the same window, numbers only) | The stop distance mirrored from the entry as the target (the previous swing's stop; a SETUP with no stop below / above the entry is not traded in these variants). Stop-or-target only, else 15:25: buys 1,731 trades, 45.8% winners, ₹-316,169, PF 0.72 (inside the range PF 0.76, above 0.64, below 0.70); sells 1,778, 50.6% winners, ₹-127,782, PF 0.88 (inside 0.86, above 0.86, below 0.92); both sides by half ₹-166,723 / ₹-277,228. Stop, target or the next CHoCH, whichever first: buys ₹-438,230 (PF 0.70), sells ₹-436,392 (PF 0.70). As built for reference: buys ₹-286,955 (PF 0.82), sells ₹-302,218 (PF 0.81). Read: a 1:1 target lifts the win rate to about half, as it must, and the result gets worse, because the trades held longer (a 1:1 target takes longer to reach than the next CHoCH) keep paying the stop at the previous swing, and capping the winners at 1R throws away the few that ran; the sells with stop-or-target only are the least bad variant of the whole study (PF 0.88) and still lose on every tag and in both halves. Nothing here turns the option's own SETUPs into an entry. |
| Open (the user's call) | Unquantified: the position inside the range (thirds) - the rows do not carry prev_high / prev_low (price_trade keeps the fixed columns; the label carries the tag); a filter on the index's structure instead; sells (`sides: both`). |
| Effort | **S** each. |
| Priority | _Closed by measurement for the question asked; nothing adopted._ Related: S70, S102. |

### S103. Lab v2 Strategy 91: weekly 150 / 450-premium options sold on the side of a 10% CAGR fair-value line, three monthly 200-premium longs as the hedge, marked hourly. Measured: ₹-34,577 over 67 positions; the shorts earn ₹+409,987 and the monthly longs lose ₹-444,565; 38 of 105 pre-expiry sessions could not be entered because the store lacks the 450-premium strike

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/cagr_hedge.py` (family module, own runner and chart; option prices from the download store), `st91.py` (`cagr`: base_price, cagr, entry_time, steps, short_premiums, long_premium, long_lots, strike_round, premium_tolerance, monthly_min_days, long_exit_days, target_pct, stop_pct, margin_per_short_lot). 2026-10-24, the user's rule verbatim in the strategy file. |
| Build choices (not in the request) | (1) The P&L base for the +3% / -6%: ₹150,000 margin per short lot (two) plus the long legs' premium paid (about ₹39,000): ±3% ≈ ₹10,000, -6% ≈ ₹-20,000. (2) The fair value compounds daily: 2000 × 1.10^(days / 365.25). (3) "Nearest strike rounded to 100": among the multiples of 100 alive that day, the one whose premium at 09:30 (the candle's open) is nearest the target; none within 75 points of the target: the entry is skipped and listed. (4) Steps at 09:30, 10:30 … 14:30 and 15:25; entry fills at the 09:30 open, exits at the step price. (5) "The next next day is the expiry": the session two sessions ahead is the sold expiry (for a Tuesday expiry: out on the Friday 09:30 step). (6) One position at a time; 0.5 slippage a side per leg, option charges, lot 65. |
| Impact | **Measured, the store's options, 2024-09-20 → 2026-10-09, after costs: 67 positions (201 legs; 45 put-side, 22 call-side), 36 winners, ₹-34,577, PF 0.97; halves ₹-26,887 (32 positions, all put-side) / ₹-7,690 (35; put-side ₹+6,351, call-side ₹-14,041); worst position ₹-21,257 (the one -6% stop), best ₹+10,727.** Exits: 61 on the calendar rule (two sessions before the sold expiry) ₹-53,045 between them, 4 targets ₹+40,374, 1 stop ₹-21,257, 1 on the monthly's 15 days ₹-649. **By leg: the short weeklies ₹+409,987, the three long monthlies ₹-444,565.** 38 pre-expiry sessions skipped: 27 for no strike with a premium within 75 of 450 (the store holds strikes within about ±800 points of the index in many weeks; a 450-premium weekly option is around 400 points in the money), 7 for the 200-premium monthly, 4 for the 150-premium weekly, 1 for no monthly 18 days out at the end of the data. |
| Read | The two shorts do what they are meant to - decay for four sessions - and would have made about ₹4 lakh on their own; the hedge is three lots of a monthly option bought every week and sold four sessions later, and its decay (three lots at about 200 points, 25 - 40 days out) costs more than the two shorts earn. Only 5 positions reached the ±% levels: at this size the P&L rarely moves 3% of the margin inside four sessions, so the calendar rule closes nearly everything, and the line's side was "above" (puts) for the first year and "below" (calls) for most of the second - the fair value (about 21,100 in September 2024, 25,900 in October 2026) crossed the index in late 2025. The missing 450 strikes mean a third of the weeks are not measured. |
| Open (the user's call) | Unquantified: the hedge at one lot, or at a 100-premium strike, or held to the monthly's 15 days instead of re-bought weekly; the base of the ±%; the store's strike range (more strikes to download before this rule can be measured on every week). |

### S102. Lab v2 Strategy 90: the day-open ATM option sold when it closes below its own previous-session low; a bullish CHoCH of the option exits a losing trade, a profitable one trails the stop at the bounce's top. Measured: ₹+74,643, PF 1.10, positive in both halves; the CE side carries it, the PE side loses

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/pdl_short.py` (family module, own runner and chart; option prices from the download store through `store_reader.py`), `st90.py` (`pdl.expiry_min_days`). 2026-10-24, the user: "one more strategy: if an option crosses below the previous day's low, sell; if CHoCH upside, exit if in loss, else trail". |
| Build choices (not in the request; fixed before the run, all in the module text) | (1) Instrument: each session's day-open ATM CE and PE (the index's 09:15 open rounded to the strike), nearest weekly expiry at least 1 day out; each leg its own machine, one position per leg, both may be short at once. (2) Level: the same contract's low of the session before (none: no trade). (3) "Crosses below": a 1-minute CLOSE below the level (not a wick), sold at the next minute's open; after an exit a fresh cross is needed (a close back at or above the level first). (4) Structure: the Foundation engine on the option's own 1-minute candles (touch mode, the lab's default), warmed up on the two sessions before. (5) "Exit if in loss else trail": at each bullish CHoCH of the option, in loss (the CHoCH candle's close above the sale price) buy back at that close; in profit, the stop goes to the top of the bounce so far (the highest high since the option's latest confirmed swing low) and is lowered to each new confirmed swing high; a later bullish CHoCH applies the rule again. No stop before the first CHoCH (the request has none). (6) Flat at 15:15; 0.5 point slippage a side, option charges, 1 lot (65). |
| Impact | **Measured, the store's options, 2024-09-20 → 2026-10-09, after costs: 1,101 trades on 490 days (587 CE, 514 PE), 41.2% winners, +2,217 premium points, ₹+74,643, PF 1.10; halves ₹+38,859 (PF 1.10) / ₹+35,784 (PF 1.11); worst trade ₹-10,023, best ₹+9,670.** By exit: 545 CHoCH-in-loss exits ₹-671,515 between them, 261 trail stops ₹+167,192, 295 held to 15:15 ₹+578,966. By leg: the CE ₹+85,674, the PE ₹-11,031 (second half: CE ₹+50,138, PE ₹-14,353). |
| Read | The edge is in the trades that never see a bullish CHoCH (held to 15:15) and in the trailed ones; the CHoCH-in-loss exit is the stop, and it fires on half the trades (41% winners overall). The CE side pays and the PE side does not: a put that breaks its previous-day low is the market rising, and the bounce (a bullish CHoCH on the put) comes with the index's dips, which are sharper than its rises. Two years of one weekly-option regime; the previous-day low is a level every participant sees. |
| SMA 10 / 20 at the cross (measured 2026-10-24, the option's own 1-minute closes, slope = current value against the previous) | At 862 of the 1,101 crosses both SMAs already slope down and at 933 the close is below both: the cross is late in the option's fall by construction. Slope does not separate the trades: SMA 20 down 892 trades ₹+62,646 PF 1.10, up 207 ₹+12,981 PF 1.13; SMA 10 down 919 ₹+68,333 PF 1.11, up 181 ₹+6,733 PF 1.08. The stack does a little: SMA 10 below SMA 20 781 trades ₹+77,333 PF 1.14; SMA 10 above SMA 20 320 trades ₹-2,690 PF 0.99. The one real split is the leg, not the SMA: CE with both sloping down and the close below both 455 trades ₹+69,390 PF 1.21; the same on the PE 405 trades ₹-15,629 PF 0.95. |
| Where it fails (measured 2026-10-24, in-sample cuts of the 1,101 trades; patterns, not a tuned filter) | (1) The profit is concentrated: 2024-09 → 11 and 2026-03 make ₹+116,996 of the ₹+74,643; 13 of the 26 months lose. (2) The direction of the index day decides, not the option's structure: the CE on index days closing 50+ points down ₹+272,702, on days 50+ up ₹-119,470; the PE the mirror (₹+170,720 / ₹-139,208); both legs lose on the sideways days (close within 50 points of the open: ₹-110,101 over 336 trades). (3) The CHoCH-in-loss exit churns: the 612 trades held under an hour lose ₹-476,338 (PF 0.02 - 0.25), the 489 held longer make ₹+550,981; the median loser lasts 26 minutes, the median winner 149. (4) Cheap premium loses: a cross below 100 points of premium 351 trades ₹-57,630 (PF 0.72); 200+ points 129 trades ₹+71,231 (PF 1.6); 1 - 2 days to expiry 367 trades ₹-25,028. (5) The PE after an index rise: the PE on gap-up days ₹-16,906 (PF 0.87), the PE when the index rose in the 15 minutes before the cross ₹-18,758 (PF 0.89), the PE on gap-down days ₹-8,458 (14 trades). (6) Entries in the 11:xx hour ₹-30,554 (PF 0.67) and 15:xx ₹-2,409; the first cross of a leg each day is flat (617 trades ₹+12,433, PF 1.03) and the re-entries make the money (#2 and #3: ₹+62,300, PF 1.3). (7) By weekday: Tuesday ₹-9,538, Wednesday ₹-22,720, Friday ₹+79,585 (PF 1.76). The 33 trades below ₹-3,000 are 18% of all the losses (28 of them CHoCH exits, 22 entered in the 09:xx hour). |

### S101. Lab v2 Strategy 86 with a ROLLING range (the previous W minutes' high / low in place of the 09:15 candle's), measured to bring in the one-way days. It loses at every window; the loss shrinks as W grows, back towards Strategy 86. No strategy created

|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/orr_opt.py` (`orr_opt.range` = {kind: rolling, minutes: W, only_beyond}; absent in every strategy). 2026-10-24, the user, after S100: "check how we can introduce a rolling window to achieve profits here". Read as: the sweep / reclaim measured against a rolling range so that on a one-way day the pullbacks against the trend give the entries the fixed opening range cannot. Numbers only; Strategies 86, 88 and 89 are unchanged (the base run reproduces 86's 942 trades / ₹+98,310 exactly). |
| What was measured | At each minute the reference low / high is the min / max of the previous W completed candles (the 09:15 candle included at the start); a close below the rolling low is the sweep and its level is latched; a later close above that level is the reclaim; the PE is sold at the day's low (the CE at the day's high on the mirror), the same strike rule, exits and occupancy as Strategy 86. A second form, `only_beyond`: the 09:15 range stays the reference until the index has closed past it on that side, and only then does the rolling range take over (the pullback entries on a day that has already broken out). |
| Impact | **Measured, the store's options, 2024-09-20 → 2026-10-09, after costs; Strategy 86 as is: 942 trades, ₹+98,310, PF 1.11.** Rolling range, reclaim entries: W = 15 min 1,510 trades ₹-112,036 (PF 0.93); 30 min 1,489 ₹-40,810 (0.97); 60 min 1,433 ₹-6,936 (0.99); 120 min 1,367 ₹+15,151 (1.01; halves ₹+3,896 / ₹+11,255). With the three-close breakout as well: 30 min ₹-14,448, 60 min ₹+7,745; the breakout alone on the rolling range 30 min ₹-32,228, 60 min ₹-35,838. `only_beyond`: 15 min 1,154 trades ₹-17,569 (0.98); 30 min 1,145 ₹+23,191 (1.02); 60 min 1,102 ₹+37,811 (1.04; halves ₹-441 / ₹+38,252). |
| Read | A rolling range fires on every session (505 of 508 days) and on most of them it is noise: the shorter the window the more sweeps and the worse the result, and as W grows the rolling range turns back into the day's range and the numbers walk back towards Strategy 86's without reaching them. Confining it to the days that have already broken out (`only_beyond`) halves the damage but still loses to Strategy 86 at every W: the pullback entries on a breakout day sell the option at a day extreme the trend has left far behind (little premium to decay) with the same stop as a reclaim trade's - S100's asymmetry again. The edge in Strategy 86 is the failed break of the first candle's range specifically; a moving reference removes it. |
| Open (the user's call) | The other reading of "rolling" - ROLLING THE STRIKE: on a one-way day, after a Strategy 86 entry, moving the sold option up (down) to the newest swing low (high) to keep collecting premium - is not measured; it changes the exit side, not the entry, and would not add the missed days' entries. S100's (a), (b), (c) remain the untested ways in. |
| Effort | **S** (measured). |
| Priority | _Closed by measurement; nothing adopted._ Related: S98, S100. |

### S100. Lab v2 Strategies 88 and 89: a continuation (breakout) entry for the one-way days Strategy 86 misses - three 1-minute closes beyond the opening range. | | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/orr_opt.py` (`orr_opt.breakout` = {closes: N}, `orr_opt.reclaim`), `st88.py` (reclaim + breakout), `st89.py` (breakout only). 2026-10-24. The user: "Strategy 86 works on the reversal days but misses the one-directional days; how to bring in those trades" → the count of Strategy 86's missed days (below) → "do it with N = 3; one more strategy as well: by default don't wait for the reversal, check for continuation trades and enter". Strategy 86 is kept. |
| The gap measured first | Of the 508 complete sessions in the window Strategy 86 traded 453. 52 of the 55 it missed are one-way days: the index closed outside the opening range on one side and never closed back inside before 15:15, so the reclaim entry never came. They are the big days: mean open → 15:15 move 193 points against 112 on the traded days (2026-10-08 -375, 09-28 -292, 07-17 +201). |
| What was built | A second trigger per side: N = 3 consecutive 1-minute closes ABOVE the opening high is a long signal (the PE sold at the day's low, as a reclaim long would), three below the opening low a short one (the CE at the day's high). The count restarts on a close back inside and after an exit. Strategy 88 keeps the reclaim entries beside it; Strategy 89 drops them (`reclaim: false`). Same strike rule, exits (the index closes back through the strike, else 15:15), both sides, one position per side, every session. Also: a side whose contract has no candles at all in the store on that day is skipped once and left (it used to re-trigger every three closes and log ~100 skips a day on 2026-02-03, 04-07 and 04-13, where the store lacks the strike). |
| Impact | **Measured, the store's options, 2024-09-20 → 2026-10-09, after costs. Strategy 88: 1,239 trades on 505 days, 46.3% winners, ₹+14,870, PF 1.01; halves ₹+13,712 (PF 1.02) / ₹+1,158 (PF 1.00); worst trade ₹-7,807. Split by trigger: the 816 reclaim trades ₹+92,518 (PF 1.12), the 423 breakout trades ₹-77,648 (PF 0.86, 50.1% winners, 229 CE / 194 PE; halves ₹-13,055 / ₹-64,593). Strategy 89 (breakout only): 824 trades on 499 days, 53.4% winners, ₹-24,861, PF 0.97; halves ₹+16,464 (PF 1.04) / ₹-41,326 (PF 0.90); exits 15:15 563, index through the strike 261.** Strategy 86 on the same window: 942 trades, ₹+98,310, PF 1.11. |
| Read | The breakout trades fire on 499 days, not the 52 one-way days: three closes beyond the range happen on most sessions, including the ones that then reverse (and in 88 they occupy the side, costing 126 reclaim trades and ₹5,800). They win half the time but lose more when they lose: after a break the option at the far extreme is cheap, so a 15:15 win earns little decay while a stop (the index all the way back through the day's low / high) costs the same as a reclaim trade's. Strategy 89's second half, the year with the larger one-way moves, is its worst. The continuation idea as built does not pay; the one-way days are real but this trigger does not isolate them. |
| Open (the user's call) | Unquantified, each a new strategy: (a) the breakout only when no reclaim came by a time gate (the one-way days proper); (b) the option sold at the broken boundary (the opening high rounded down for the PE), nearer the money, more premium; (c) a larger N or a minimum distance beyond the range. |

(3) "A CHoCH has just occurred" = it is the latest structure break (a BOS after it ends it), one entry per CHoCH, and the OB was created after the CHoCH or within the 10 bars before it. (4) Expiry: the nearest weekly on or after the day; strike: the index close at the signal rounded to 50; the previous-day low: that contract's own previous session. (5) Fills at the option's 5-minute close; stop before target in a candle; the 30-minute exit at the close of the candle 30 minutes after the entry; flat at 15:00. |
| Impact | **Measured on the lab's data, ATM options, 1 lot, 2025-12-01 → 2026-09-30.** Section 1 reading (PE bought), with the filter: **21 trades (20 refused by the filter), 52% winners, +89.0 premium points gross (PF 1.72), ₹+3,169 after costs (PF 1.34)**; long 8 trades -20.5 points, short 13 trades +109.6 points; exits: time 17, target 4. Halves: Dec - Apr 15 trades +29.3 points, ₹+37; May - Sep 6 trades +59.7 points, ₹+3,132. Without the filter: 41 trades, +195.3 points (PF 1.76), ₹+7,556; halves +187.1 / +8.2 points. Exit-table reading (PE sold), with the filter: 21 trades, -155.4 points (PF 0.40), ₹-12,717; without: 41 trades, -224.5 points, ₹-19,714. All option data (2025-08-01 → 2026-10-09), section 1 reading, with the filter: 25 trades, ₹+1,131. |
| Read | The lab finds fewer signals than the document (44 before the filter against 55) and the same picture in its main lines: a small positive in premium points, nearly every exit on the 30-minute clock, the positive part from the bought PEs. Here the previous-day filter does not help: it halves the trades and the points (195 → 89), and in Dec - Apr it turns ₹+9,132 into ₹+37. 21 trades in ten months (t 0.5): not evidence either way. Selling the PE on a bearish signal loses, as it should (it is a bet the market goes up). |
| Open (the user's call) | Which reading of "short" the document means; whether the filter stays; the document's code, to match its 55 signals. Unquantified: the document's own suggestions (longer holds, an opposite-CHoCH exit, a higher-timeframe bias). |
| Effort | **S** each. |
| Priority | _Open; first numbers recorded._ Related: S93, S69. |
