### S93. Lab v2 Strategy 81: the user's NIFTY regime spec (CE / PE open interest + structure). Measured, futures, August 2025 → October 2026: 1,648 trades, ₹-18,68,938, PF 0.23; both halves, every regime and both sides lose

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/oi_regime.py` (family module in the spec's four separate pieces: `oi_series`, `structure`, `regime`, `signals_of`; `chart_main` for the chart), `st81.py` (`oi_regime`: every number), `ui/js/chart.js` (`drawOI`: the two OI lines; regime labels as markers), `ui/js/util.js` (the rules text). 2026-10-20, the user's pasted build spec "NIFTY regime strategy (CE/PE OI + structure)". |
| Data | The option files' `open_interest` column (5-minute files of each weekly expiry; the lab's option loader did not read it). Share of 5-minute bars with the 3 ATM strikes' CE and PE OI: 2025-01 → 05 none; 2025-06 0.76; 2025-07 0.13; 2025-08 → 2026-08 0.76 - 1.00; 2026-09 0.70; 2026-10 0.50 (weeks not exported yet). Backtest window 2025-08-01 → 2026-10-09; no trade on a bar without OI. |
| Build choices (not in the spec; fixed before any run) | (1) OI = the 3 strikes around the ATM (from the index close of that bar, ± 50), nearest weekly expiry with the expiry day included; dOI per strike against the same strike one bar before, summed (an ATM change does not jump it); a 15-minute bar reads its last 5 minutes. (2) "Short buildup" = dOI > 0 and that side's premium falling over the window; "dominant" = its dOI larger than the other side's. (3) Window n = 6 bars; "tight" = the 6-bar high - low at most 40 points (no ATR). (4) Swings: a pivot 2 bars each side, confirmed 2 bars later; BOS / CHoCH on a close beyond the last swing, each level broken once. (5) Regime precedence: CHoCH, BOS down, BOS up, Sideways, none. (6) Sideways entries: the close in the top / bottom 25% of the 6-bar range, fresh when the bar before was not. (7) "Bounce" = the bar before closed up and this one closes down (sell in BOS down); "dip" the mirror (buy in BOS up). (8) Stop 30 points, target 30 points, at most 12 bars, out on any CHoCH, flat at the square-off (15:14 since 2026-06-01), no entry from 15:10. (9) Traded on the near-month futures; the price candles are the futures by default, the index in a second backtest. |
| Impact | **Measured, Futures type, 1 lot, 5 points slippage a side, after costs, 2025-08-01 → 2026-10-09, 5-minute: 1,648 trades, ₹-18,68,938, PF 0.23, 40% winners, ₹-10.2 points a trade.** First half (to 2026-02-28) 784 trades ₹-9,17,241 (PF 0.20); second half 864 trades ₹-9,51,697 (PF 0.24). By exit: target 643 trades ₹+5,36,311; stop 560 ₹-17,17,483; CHoCH 217 ₹-4,39,346 (3% winners); bar cap 175 ₹-1,97,472; square-off 53 ₹-50,948. By regime at the entry: Sideways 168 trades ₹-1,73,400 (PF 0.21); BOS up 732 ₹-8,99,700 (0.20); BOS down 748 ₹-7,95,837 (0.25). Long 815 ₹-9,92,848; short 833 ₹-8,76,090. Bars by regime: none 8,789, BOS down 5,600, BOS up 5,486, Sideways 1,416, CHoCH 997. 15-minute candles: 657 trades, ₹-7,59,783, PF 0.24. Price read on the index (traded on futures): 1,626 trades, ₹-17,94,541, PF 0.26. Options via futures (ATM; full-chain data only from 2026-06-25, so only the 3-month run): 584 option rows, ₹-89,872, PF 0.65 (futures, same 3 months: 306 trades ₹-3,39,350). |
| Read | Target hit slightly more often than stop (643 against 560) is not enough: a target nets 20 points after slippage, a stop costs 40, and the CHoCH and bar-cap exits are nearly all losers. No regime is better than another, so the OI conditions are not separating good entries from bad ones at these settings. Fourteen months, both halves alike. |
| Open (the user's call) | Unquantified: the spec's numbers that were chosen here (n, tight, edge, pivot, the points, the bar cap) - each needs the user's value or a pre-registered grid with a pick / check split; the options as the traded instrument once more full-chain option months exist; 3 strikes against a wider band. |
| Effort | **S** each (SPEC values). |
| Corrected 2026-10-20 (the user: "CHoCH is shown on both sides of the candle, Sideways is not correct, correct it; check for 1-minute candles as well") | (1) The chart drew each CHoCH twice: the structure event and the regime label; the regime label no longer marks a CHoCH. (2) The regime windows reached into the day before (at 09:15 the 6-bar range was 156 points and the OI sums included yesterday's bars); every window is now the last bars of the same session, and a regime needs a full window with OI on every bar. (3) Sideways also needs no BOS / CHoCH inside the window (price not breaking structure). (4) The regime flipped bar by bar (on 1-minute candles 113 changes on 2026-08-20); it is now a state: it changes only when another regime is seen on 2 bars in a row (`confirm`), a CHoCH clears it, a BOS ends Sideways, each session starts with none. (5) Lengths are in minutes (`window_min` 30, `pivot_min` 10, `max_min` 60: on 5-minute candles the same 6 / 2 / 12 bars as before) so that 1-minute candles read the same span of time; 1-minute bars read the 1-minute option files. On 2026-08-20 the 5-minute and the 1-minute charts now give nearly the same regime sequence (14 and 15 changes). **Before → after, futures, 2025-08-01 → 2026-10-09:** 5-minute 1,648 trades ₹-18,68,938 PF 0.23 → 1,882 trades ₹-21,84,171 PF 0.22 (halves ₹-10,19,201 / ₹-11,64,970); 15-minute 657 ₹-7,59,783 PF 0.24 → 765 ₹-9,29,671 PF 0.20; index-read 1,626 ₹-17,94,541 PF 0.26 → 1,821 ₹-20,34,494 PF 0.26; 1-minute (new): 2,564 trades ₹-29,65,029 PF 0.22. By regime, 5-minute: Sideways 62 trades ₹-77,824 (PF 0.15), BOS up 910 ₹-11,53,778 (0.19), BOS down 910 ₹-9,52,568 (0.25); 1-minute: Sideways 98 ₹-98,934, BOS up 1,183 ₹-15,05,606, BOS down 1,283 ₹-13,60,489. More trades because a persistent regime stays open for entries longer; the result a trade is unchanged. |

|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/sma_cross.py` (`sma_cross.valid_at_entry`), `st73.py` - `st80.py` (the flag set in all eight). 2026-10-14. The user asked whether, after a stop, the strategies stop or keep entering "as long as the moving average is valid, that is down slope and price below it". As built they kept entering every later bearish SETUP of the armed day and never looked at the average again; the user asked for the check ("yes, go on all three", read as all of the strategies offered: 73 - 80). |
| What changed | At each entry candle the SMA of the low that was crossed (the period of the last signal before the entry) must have its last completed 15-minute value below the one before it, and the entry price must be below that value. Otherwise the SETUP is skipped; a later SETUP the same day is taken when the average is valid again. Nothing else changed: re-entry after a stop stays, one position at a time, the stops and targets of S89. Strategies 69 - 72 do not carry the flag (69 and 71 re-run unchanged). |

### S89. Lab v2 Strategies 73 - 80: the SMA cross on 15-minute candles, the structure on 5-minute or 1-minute candles, the stop at the swing high or at the SMA of the highs, the target 1:1 or 1:2. Measured, futures 2018 - 2026: all eight lose; the least bad is 5-minute structure, SMA-high stop, 1:2 (305 trades, ₹-91,283, PF 0.84, 2 positive years of 8)

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/sma_cross.py` (`signals_arm`: `candles_min`, `stop: structure / sma_high`, `target_r`), `st73.py` - `st80.py`. 2026-10-13, the user's instruction: "try the stop as the SMA high or the previous swing high; try 15-minute SMA and 5-minute CHoCH / BOS; try 15-minute SMA and 1-minute CHoCH / BOS; try 1:2 risk reward as well, not just 1:1; create new strategies for all these variations". Strategies 67 - 72 are kept; 69 and 71 re-run unchanged after the code change (2,657 trades ₹-28,57,043; 578 trades ₹-4,99,852). |
| The eight | 73: 5-minute structure, swing-high stop, 1:1. 74: the same, 1:2. 75: 5-minute structure, SMA-high stop, 1:1. 76: the same, 1:2. 77: 1-minute structure, swing-high stop, 1:1. 78: the same, 1:2. 79: 1-minute structure, SMA-high stop, 1:1. 80: the same, 1:2. |
| Build choices (not in the instruction; fixed before the run) | (1) The signal is Strategy 68's on 15-minute candles (built from the strategy's own 1- or 5-minute candles, 09:15-aligned; a value is used only from the candle that completes it): a close down through a falling SMA 20 / 50 / 100 / 200 / 500 of the low arms the rest of the day. (2) The structure rules are Strategy 1's (CHoCH and BOS by touch; on 5-minute candles that is Strategy 2); Strategy 3's rule (CHoCH by close) is measured beside it, not built. Bearish SETUPs only, one position at a time. (3) "SMA high" = the SMA of the candle HIGHS on the same 15-minute candles, with the period of the SMA crossed last before the entry (the fastest when several were crossed on one candle), its value at the entry candle, fixed; not above the entry: no trade. (4) Risk to reward: the target is 1 or 2 times the stop's distance, replacing the 30 points. (5) Square-off; no CHoCH exit; stop before target in one candle. (6) Warm-up 21 sessions (the SMA 500 of 15-minute candles needs 20). |
| Impact, futures | **Measured, Futures type, 1 lot, 5 points slippage a side, after costs, 2018-01-31 → 2026-10-09 (split at 2023-03-09; positive years of 8).** 73: 353 trades, ₹-2,92,422, PF 0.44, 44% winners; target 157, stop 129, close 67; median stop distance 33.0 points; halves ₹-1,30,294 / ₹-1,62,128; 0 years. 74: 345 trades, ₹-2,32,168, PF 0.61; 89 / 148 / 108; ₹-93,403 / ₹-1,38,765; 1 year. 75: 309 trades, ₹-1,30,277, PF 0.75, 46% winners; 101 / 72 / 136; stop distance 60.6; ₹-85,746 / ₹-44,531; 2 years. **76: 305 trades, ₹-91,283, PF 0.84, 42% winners; target 32, stop 81, close 192; stop distance 60.6; ₹-52,593 / ₹-38,691; 2 years.** 77: 1,570 trades, ₹-17,16,247, PF 0.08, 16% winners; 686 / 794 / 90; stop distance 13.0; 0 years. 78: 1,480 trades, ₹-15,51,129, PF 0.21; 415 / 924 / 141; 0 years. 79: 830 trades, ₹-8,97,857, PF 0.39; 298 / 291 / 241; stop distance 42.4; 0 years. 80: 753 trades, ₹-7,67,099, PF 0.48; 135 / 312 / 306; 0 years. With Strategy 3's rule (CHoCH by close) instead, same order 73 → 80: ₹-2,28,374 (246 trades, PF 0.42); ₹-1,75,457 (245, 0.60); ₹-1,73,038 (225, 0.59); ₹-1,30,933 (224, 0.71); ₹-10,90,082 (986, 0.09); ₹-10,10,316 (953, 0.24); ₹-6,53,231 (613, 0.41); ₹-5,82,704 (564, 0.48). |
| Impact, options (stored All data runs, from 2026-06-30: one quarter) | Options via futures W-ATR2 (a put bought) / options on their own chart, weekly scan (the option sold). 73: 20 trades ₹+7,149 / 90 trades ₹-9,181. 74: 20 ₹+6,128 / 80 ₹-8,491. 75: 18 ₹+16,061 / 75 ₹+3,640. 76: 18 ₹+12,043 / 66 ₹+7,302. 77: 112 ₹-18,187 / 273 ₹-30,237. 78: 106 ₹-21,084 / 243 ₹-23,221. 79: 48 ₹-22,123 / 129 ₹+1,027. 80: 46 ₹-18,602 / 107 ₹+9,621. On an option's own chart the 15-minute SMA 200 and 500 are rarely formed (a contract has too few sessions), so the signal there is mostly the SMA 20 / 50 / 100. |
| Read | Three things move the result in one direction on 8 years of futures. (1) The 5-minute structure is better than the 1-minute one in every pair: on 1-minute candles the swing-high stop is 13 points away, so a 1:1 target is 13 points and the 10 points of slippage leave 3 (Strategy 77: targets hit 686 times and it still has 16% net winners). (2) The wider stop (SMA of the highs: 42 - 61 points) is better than the swing high in every pair. (3) 1:2 is better than 1:1 in every pair. All three say the same: the wider the trade, the smaller the share of the costs. None is positive: the best, Strategy 76, is ₹-299 a trade over 305 trades, with 192 of them ending at the square-off, neither target nor stop. ### S82. Lab v2 Strategy 65, two checks before building (2026-10-11): exit the trailed lot on the first big red candle after a rally (helps a little, both halves); buy the other leg at a trail exit (loses under every target and stop tried)

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | A study on Strategy 65's stored trades as of S81 (176 entries, ₹-92,050; `results/ST65/all-data_1m`, Options (via futures) W-ATM, 2026-06-30 → 2026-10-09). Nothing in the lab was changed. The user's request: "if there is a rally then exit on the first big red candle; validate, if we buy the other side on the trail exit, whether that will give 20 points or 10 points". |
| Definitions (fixed before running; none was given) | **Rally**: the option has traded R points above the entry, R = 20 (the trail's own unlock) or 10. **Big red candle**: a 1-minute candle of the option closing below its open with a body of at least K times the mean body of the 14 candles before it, K = 1.5, 2, 3. The trailed lot leaves at that candle's close when it comes before its stored exit. Knock-on effects (the leg free earlier, so later signals taken) are not counted. **Other side**: at each trail-stop exit of the trailed lot (45 cases) buy the other option of the same strike and expiry at that minute's close; costs 1 point slippage and ₹63 charges a lot. |
| Impact A (big red candle) | **Measured, change to the trailed lots' total (stored: 176 lots, ₹-38,035).** Rally +20: K 1.5 fires on 37 lots, ₹+2,613 (halves +3,032 / -419); K 2 on 23 lots, ₹+6,620 (+3,907 / +2,714; better on 17, worse on 6); K 3 on 11 lots, ₹+2,785 (+2,603 / +182). Rally +10: K 1.5 on 88 lots, ₹+14,888 (+8,086 / +6,802; better on 56, worse on 32); K 2 on 70 lots, ₹+14,261 (+6,445 / +7,816); K 3 on 38 lots, ₹+9,701 (+8,379 / +1,323). |
| Impact B (other leg) | **Measured, 45 buys, median premium 92.** Traded +10 at some point before the close: 26 (58%); +20: 11 (24%). Held to the close without target or stop: ₹-17,706 (14 winners). Target 10 with stop 50 / 20 / 10: ₹-10,813 / ₹-10,586 / ₹-5,808 (target reached 25 / 23 / 20 times). Target 20 with stop 50 / 20 / 10: ₹-20,797 / ₹-20,550 / ₹-14,538 (target reached 11 / 10 / 7 times). Every line negative in both halves. |
| Read | A: the rule is positive for every definition tried and in both halves for most; the best (rally +10, K 1.5 or 2) takes back about ₹14,500 of the trailed lot's ₹-38,035. It does not make the strategy positive (₹-92,050 would be about ₹-77,500). Six definitions on 176 lots: the size of the gain is not established, the sign looks stable. B: buying the other leg when the trailed leg stops out does not pay: it gives 10 points more often than not but the rest lose more, and 20 points one time in four. |
| Open (the user's call) | A is not built: which definition (rally 10 or 20, K) to put in Strategy 65 is the user's to choose; rule 0(c): before / after on the same window when it is built. B: not built, no reason to. |
| Effort | **S** to build A. |

### S79. Lab v2 Strategy 65: the first stop changed from the option's latest swing low to its previous CHoCH candle's low less 10%. Before ₹-82,367 (198 entries), after ₹-34,769 (87 entries); 75 signals have no usable stop under the new rule

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/st65.py` (`straddle.stop: {rule: prev_choch, buffer_pct: 10}`), `atm_straddle.py` (`_at_signal`). 2026-10-11, the user's instruction after asking what the stop was (the latest swing low: median 6.65 points below the entry, 61% of entries stopped there): "the stop is the previous CHoCH - 10%". Everything else in Strategy 65 is as in S78 (fourth build). |
| How it was read | The option's own chart: the low of the latest CHoCH candle (either direction) at or before the entry candle, less 10% of that low. Not read as the combined chart's CHoCH: a level of the combined premium is not a price of one leg. When that level is not below the entry price (the CHoCH happened when the premium was more than about 11% higher) there is no stop and the signal is not taken. |
| Impact | **Measured, Options (via futures) W-ATM, after costs, same window (2026-06-30 → 2026-10-09). Before: 198 entries (396 lots), ₹-82,367, PF 0.57; halves ₹-64,464 / ₹-17,904. After: 87 entries (174 lots), ₹-34,769, PF 0.72, t -1.53, 37% winners; halves ₹-31,190 (PF 0.62) / ₹-3,579 (PF 0.91).** 14 of 39 days positive; worst day ₹-7,811, best ₹+8,058. Stop distance: median 34.7 points (quartiles 23.0 and 71.0) against 6.65 before. **Signals not taken: 75 with the CHoCH level not below the entry, 29 with no CHoCH of the option yet in its warm-up window, 37 because the leg was still in a trade** (the wider stop keeps positions open longer). Lot T1: ₹-26,379 = target 30 lots ₹+34,974; next bearish CHoCH 40 ₹-44,582 (none a winner); first stop 7 ₹-4,622; close 10 ₹-12,150. Lot TRAIL: ₹-8,390 = moved stop 30 lots ₹+52,963; next CHoCH 40 ₹-44,582; first stop 7 ₹-4,622; close 10 ₹-12,150. Bought on the straddle's sell signal: 46 lots ₹+5,106 (PF 1.35); on its buy signal: 128 lots ₹-39,875 (PF 0.63). |
| Read | The stop is no longer the problem: 7 of 87 entries reach it. The loss has moved to the option's next bearish CHoCH (40 entries, about ₹1,115 a lot, never a winner) and to positions carried to the close. 30 of 87 entries (34%) reach +20 points, against 19% before. Part of the improvement is fewer trades, and which trades are left is decided by where an old CHoCH happened to be, not by anything about the signal. The sell-signal half is positive on 23 entries: too few to read. One quarter of data. |
| Open (the user's call) | Unquantified: what to do with the 75 signals whose CHoCH level is above the entry (skip as now; or use the latest CHoCH whose low is below the entry; or a plain 10% below the entry); whether the next-CHoCH exit should stay now that the stop is wide. |
| Effort | **S** each. |
| Priority | _Open; before / after recorded._ Related: S78. |

### S78. Lab v2 Strategy 65: buy the winning leg at the straddle chart's signal candle, two lots (one out at +20 points, one trailed with the stop moved only after +20). Four builds in one day, each measured; the current one is the worst: 198 entries, ₹-82,367

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/st65.py` (`straddle.arm: both`, `entry: signal`, `trail: bos_level`, `two_lots`), `atm_straddle.py` (`_at_signal`, `_walk`). 2026-10-11. Four instructions from the user, each changing the build before: (1) naming Strategy 64, "in this, do it for both sell and buy signals"; (2) "no, not that; correct ST65; this is also long only; when you get a sell in the combined chart, enter long in the winning one"; (3) "in Strategy 65 we are entering late; enter at the same time as the derived chart's signal"; (4) "move the stop only after 20 points; enter 2 lots, one trailed, the other out at 20 points; modify the same strategy". Strategy 64 is kept as built. |
| What is built (fourth build) | ANY SETUP on the derived straddle candles, bullish or bearish → at that candle's close buy TWO lots of the winning leg (the CE or the PE with the larger change of its close over the last 5 candles). Nothing is sold. First stop, both lots: the option's own latest confirmed swing low below the entry. Lot T1: out at entry + 20 points; its stop never moves. Lot TRAIL: the stop stays until the option has traded at entry + 20, then goes to the level of the latest bullish BOS of the option since the entry and follows every later one. Both: the option's next bearish CHoCH, the square-off. Within a candle the stop is checked before the target. One entry a leg at a time. |
| Impact | **Measured, Options (via futures) W-ATM, after costs, 2026-06-30 → 2026-10-09: 198 entries (396 lots), ₹-82,367, PF 0.57, t -3.51, 24% winners.** First half (to 2026-08-12) ₹-64,464 (PF 0.49); second half ₹-17,904 (PF 0.74). 17 of 41 days positive; worst day ₹-14,690, best ₹+9,467. **Lot T1: ₹-50,941** = target 37 lots ₹+43,213; first stop 120 ₹-72,457; next CHoCH 28 ₹-19,254; close 13 ₹-2,443. **Lot TRAIL: ₹-31,426** = moved stop 31 lots ₹+54,385 (all winners); first stop 120 ₹-72,457; next CHoCH 32 ₹-14,342; close 15 ₹+988. Bought on the straddle's buy signal ₹-60,515; on its sell signal ₹-21,852. |
| The three earlier builds, same window (not in the lab any more) | (1) The armed option's own bearish SETUPs traded as shorts beside the buys: 96 trades, ₹-5,686, PF 0.80. (2) Long only, both straddle signals arming the winning leg, the buy at the option's own bullish SETUP afterwards: 85 trades, ₹-5,808, PF 0.78. (3) One lot bought at the straddle signal's candle, the stop following every BOS from the entry: 217 trades, ₹-19,744, PF 0.74 (first stop 107 trades ₹-53,906; moved stop 87 trades ₹+30,839). |
| Read | Holding the stop still until +20 points makes it worse, not better: 120 of 198 entries (61%) now end at the first stop with the full loss on both lots, against 107 of 217 when the stop followed every BOS; only 37 entries (19%) reach +20. The early BOS moves were what cut the losers short. One quarter of data, but both halves agree. |
| Open (the user's call) | Unquantified: a wider first stop; moving the stop to the entry price once a smaller profit is reached; the two lots on the second build (entry at the option's own SETUP), which lost least. |
| Effort | **S** each. |
| Priority | _Open; numbers of all four builds recorded._ Related: S77, S76, S75. |

Strategy 63 is kept as built. |
| Build choices (not in the instruction; fixed before the run) | (1) At every bullish BOS of the option's own chart while the long is open, the stop moves to the level that BOS broke (the latest swing high confirmed before it), from the next candle; it never moves back (`trail: bos_level`). (2) Fill at the stop, or at the open when the candle opens beyond it. With BOS by touch the BOS candle can close below the level it broke; the next candle then exits at once. (3) Measured beside it, not built as a strategy: the stop at the latest confirmed swing low at that BOS (`trail: bos_swing`). |
| Impact | **Measured, Options (via futures) W-ATM, 1 lot, after costs, 2026-06-30 → 2026-10-09, 41 trades in each.** No trail (Strategy 63): ₹+5,908, PF 1.30, 22% winners; halves ₹+12,706 / ₹-6,798; without its best day ₹-6,014. **Stop to the BOS level (Strategy 64): ₹+1,261, PF 1.08, 27% winners; halves ₹+9,545 (PF 2.31) / ₹-8,285 (PF 0.04); without its best day ₹-9,311.** By exit: moved stop 23 trades ₹+11,264 (43% winners); first stop 11 trades ₹-8,221; next CHoCH 4 trades ₹-2,634; close 3 trades ₹+852. Stop to the swing low behind the BOS: ₹+5,749, PF 1.34, 29% winners; halves ₹+8,903 / ₹-3,154; without its best day ₹-4,359. The trade of 2026-09-23 14:03: ₹-305 → ₹+224 (out at 181.90 at 14:17) → ₹+272 with the swing-low stop (out at 182.65 at 14:34). The trade of 2026-07-08 13:47: ₹+11,503 (held to the close at 419.80) → ₹+10,582 (out at 405.60 at 14:11). |
| Read | The trail does what it was asked to on the trade that prompted it, and costs about as much elsewhere: 23 of 41 trades now leave at a moved stop, most of them for a few points, and the eight positions that used to run to the close (₹+16,938) become three. Every version still rests on the same single day and loses in the second half. 41 trades in one quarter cannot separate the three. |

In Strategy 59 the short legs held to the close made ₹+73,496; here the legs that would have run are taken out at 20 points and what reaches the close is the remainder (₹-21,179). A fixed target with the structure stop is the wrong way round for a short-premium book: it caps the decay collected and keeps every adverse move. One quarter of data. |

### S70. Lab v2 Strategies 57 and 58: Strategy 3 read on the day-open ATM straddle's candles. Measured: both lose; the money is in the positions held to the close

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/atm_straddle.py` (family module with its own `run`), `st57.py`, `st58.py`. 2026-10-11, the user's request: "apply Strategy 3 on the day-open ATM straddle candles; a variant: when we see a buy trade in this, buy only the one that is going up, and exit that on the sell side". |
| Build choices (not in the request; fixed before any run) | (1) The chart: CE + PE of the strike nearest the index's first candle of the day (nearest weekly expiry at least 1 day out, the same day's when the next is not in the data), within 2 strikes of the ATM or the day is skipped; candles as the explorer's straddle chart (open / close summed, high / low from the summed open and close). (2) One engine run per session on that session's own pair, warmed up on the pair's two sessions before, so the structure does not jump when the strike changes overnight; only SETUPs entered in the session trade. (3) ST57: bullish SETUP = buy both legs, bearish = sell both; ST58: bullish only, one leg. (4) "The leg that is going up" = the larger change of its close over the last 5 candles up to the entry (a tie to the CE). (5) "Exit on the sell side" = the straddle trade's own exit: its stop at the previous swing of the combined premium or the next CHoCH, or the square-off. (6) A leg fills at its own candle close at the straddle's entry and exit candles, also when the exit is the stop (the stop is a level of the combined premium). One straddle trade at a time. |
| Impact | **Measured, Options (via futures) W-ATM, 1 lot a leg, after costs, the full-chain window (first trade 2026-07-01, last 2026-10-05; 41 sessions with a trade), 15:14 close.** **ST57: 456 legs (228 straddle trades), ₹-56,971, PF 0.72, t -1.94; as straddles 18% winners, PF 0.48.** Bought (bullish) 208 legs ₹-31,788, PF 0.59; sold (bearish) 248 legs ₹-25,182, PF 0.80. CE legs ₹-47,203, PE legs ₹-9,768. First half ₹-42,942 (0.65), second ₹-14,029 (0.82). By exit: next CHoCH 222 legs ₹-49,171; stop 156 legs ₹-50,462 (PF 0.23); **held to the close 78 legs ₹+42,662 (PF 2.26)**. Gross before costs ₹-27,944. Median hold 18 minutes. **ST58: 104 trades, ₹-22,496, PF 0.63, t -1.17, 13% winners**; CE 46 trades ₹-21,814, PE 58 trades ₹-682; stop 46 trades ₹-28,179 (2% winners), next CHoCH 52 ₹-23,048, **held to the close 6 trades ₹+28,731**. |
| Read | The same picture as on futures (S64): the positions that survive to the close pay, the ones stopped or reversed inside minutes lose more than that. Buying the leg that has just risen (ST58) wins 13% of the time: it buys after the move. One quarter, 41 sessions: not evidence either way. |

### S69. Lab v2 Strategies 55 and 56: the TradingFinder "Smart Money Liquidity Structure - OB Signal Setups" indicator, ported. The user's manual TradingView backtest was profitable; the lab's is not, alone or with Strategy 3

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/smls.py` (the port), `st55.py` (ST3 + a same-side signal), `st56.py` (the signals themselves). Scratch `smls_study.py`, `smls_check.py`. 2026-10-11: the user pasted the Pine script and said "use this along with Strategy 3 and we are done, it's profitable, I did backtest in TradingView manually for all the trades". |
| What was ported | As written, the script's defaults: 3-candle pivots and its H / L / HH / HL / LH / LL state machine; the One- and Two-liquidity setups (a five- or seven-label pattern, a block at the pattern's origin, kept if one or two of its swing points are still untouched); the Midway setup (9-candle pivots, "Defensive": break 0.25 ATR(50), body 0.6 ATR, reaction 0.5 ATR, 30 candles, all four quality points); blocks end on entry (proximal mitigation), a close through, or 1,000 candles; the signal = the first candle after creation that trades into the block. **Not exact: the script takes each block's edges from an imported library (`TFlab/OrderBlockRefiner_TradingFinder/2`) whose source was not supplied; the port uses the origin candle's full high–low range (the refiner off).** Pivot ties resolved as: above every high of the n candles before, not below any of the n after. A first version of the port added the four Midway quality points as booleans (an OR, not a count), so Midway never fired; fixed before any number below. |
| Impact | **Measured, near-month futures, 1 lot, 2018-01-03 → 2026-10-09; split 2023-03-09.** Signals on 1-minute candles: 861 long, 830 short (0.8 a session); One 318, Two 142, Midway 1,231. **The signals traded on their own (entry at the signal candle's close, stop at the block's far edge, flat 15:25), before any cost: +0.2 / +1.7 points a trade on the two halves, 9% / 11% winners, median stop 6.9 pts.** With a 1R / 2R / 3R target: −0.1 / −0.6 / −0.8 pts (H1). By setup, before costs: One +1.2 pts, Two +0.1, Midway 0.0 (H1). On 3-, 5- and 15-minute candles, before costs: −4.9 / +1.3, −2.8 / −7.4, −2.1 / −10.3 pts. **ST56 (with the lab's one-position lock, 10 pts slippage and charges): 1-minute 1,405 trades, ₹-13,16,043, PF 0.33, t -14.7, 10% winners, 1,212 stopped; every year loses. 5-minute 263 trades, ₹-3,38,577, PF 0.32. 15-minute 56 trades, ₹-76,102, PF 0.33.** **As a filter on ST3** (the ST3 trade, kept if a same-side signal fired within N candles): N = 10: 11 / 10 trades; N = 30: PF 0.32 / 0.16 (76 / 63 trades) against 0.36 / 0.37 without; N = 60: 0.29 / 0.12; an opposite-side signal within 30: 0.43 / 0.39. On ST43's and ST46's filters fewer than 20 trades a half, all losing. **ST55 (ST3 + same-side signal within 30): 139 trades, ₹-1,76,494, PF 0.23, t -6.66; every year loses.** |
| Why the lab and the manual backtest can differ (none of these is established) | (1) The block's edges: the refiner library narrows a block; here it is the whole origin candle, so the entry comes earlier and the stop sits elsewhere. (2) The exit: the indicator gives entries only; a manual backtest chooses the stop and the target by eye; here the stop is the block's far edge (median 6.9 pts on 1-minute candles, inside the noise: S60, S64). (3) Costs: 10 pts of slippage plus about ₹356 of charges a round trip here; a chart-reading backtest usually has none. Before costs the lab's signals are about flat, not profitable. (4) The timeframe and instrument the user tested on are not known here. (5) Hindsight: on a finished chart the blocks and "Liquidity" lines that were later invalidated are gone or faded, and the eye picks the entries that worked. |
| Fix sketch | None proposed. To reconcile: the user names the timeframe, instrument, stop and target used, and a few dated trades from the manual backtest; those are opened on ST56's chart. If the refiner library's source is supplied, the port can use the exact edges. Until then ST55 / ST56 are records, not candidates. |
| Effort | Reconciliation **S** once the trades are supplied; the refiner **S–M**. |
| Priority | _Open; numbers recorded._ Related: S68 (the other SMC port), S64 (stops inside the noise). |

| Definitions (fixed before the run; all read at the SETUP candle on 1-minute futures) | Choppiness Index CHOP(14) = 100 × log10(Σ true range over 14 / (highest high − lowest low of the 14)) / log10(14): "not chop" ≤ 61.8, "trend" < 38.2. ATR(14) above its value 30 candles ago. The 15 candles before the SETUP span at least 20 pts (the note's 20-point box). The previous CHoCH is at least 15 candles earlier (not a rapid alternation). Clock: 09:15–10:30 or 13:30–15:15 good, 11:00–13:00 chop. Not the middle: entry in the outer quarters of the session's range so far, and of the last 60 candles. Not testable here: heavyweight stocks (no data), order-block respect, the 15-minute anchor as drawn by hand. Split 2023-03-09. |
| Impact | **Measured, Futures, 1 lot, after costs; PF H1 / H2, yes vs no.** On ST3 (0.36 / 0.36): **CHOP < 38.2: 0.53 / 0.49 vs 0.31 / 0.33 (488 / 381 trades; held under 30 min 48% / 46% vs 67% / 66%)**; CHOP ≤ 61.8: 0.36 / 0.38 vs 0.43 / 0.16 (it refuses only 4–9%); ATR rising 0.39 / 0.37 vs 0.33 / 0.35; 20-pt box 0.41 / 0.38 vs 0.26 / 0.22; slow CHoCH 0.38 / 0.36 vs 0.31 / 0.37; good clock 0.43 / 0.38 vs 0.28 / 0.33; chop window 0.27 / 0.37 vs 0.40 / 0.36 (H1 only); outer quarters of the session range 0.42 / 0.34 vs 0.31 / 0.39 (reverses); of the last 60 candles 0.37 / 0.36 vs 0.35 / 0.35. The clock and "not the middle" rules look good on the first half and reverse on the second: not rules. The 20-point box and rapid-CHoCH rules are real but small, and the box is already covered by ST43's 20-pt stop. |

### S65. Lab v2 Strategies 44 and 45: the two structural entry ideas (a sweep of a real extreme; a higher-degree pivot as the broken level). Measured before building: both mark the worse trades

|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/liquidity.py` (`extreme_sweep`, `pivot_degree`), `st44.py` (ST43 + extreme sweep), `st45.py` (ST43 + 15-candle pivot). 2026-10-11, at the user's request ("validate, and if profitable or we need a history of it, code it"). |
| Definitions (fixed before the validation run) | **A. Extreme sweep** (bullish; highs for bearish): within the 30 candles up to the CHoCH candle, a candle went below the level and closed back above it; A1 level = the session's low so far, A2 = the previous session's low, A3 = either. **B. Higher-degree pivot**: the swing the CHoCH broke is the highest high / lowest low of the K candles on each side of it, all before the CHoCH candle; B1 K = 15, B2 K = 30. Split 2023-03-09 (H1 2,479 / H2 2,488 ST3 futures trades). |
| Impact | **Measured, Futures, 1 lot, after costs; PF H1 / H2, passes vs fails.** On ST3 (0.36 / 0.36): A1 0.43 / 0.33 vs 0.35 / 0.37 (417 / 470 pass); A2 0.41 / 0.27 vs 0.35 / 0.38; A3 0.43 / 0.32 vs 0.33 / 0.38; B1 0.38 / 0.32 vs 0.36 / 0.36 (92 / 117 pass); B2 0.28 / 0.05 (26 / 30). The sweeps help in H1 and hurt in H2: not stable. **On ST43's filters (0.52 / 0.52): with A1 0.27 / 0.36, without 0.56 / 0.57; with A3 0.38 / 0.49, without 0.56 / 0.53; with B1 0.36 / 0.36, without 0.56 / 0.56; with B2 0.46 / 0.05.** On the best base both ideas select the worse trades in both halves. **Built for the record: ST44 (ST43 + A3) 246 trades, ₹-2,61,038, PF 0.47; ST45 (ST43 + B1) 150 trades, ₹-1,98,706, PF 0.36.** Options via futures W-ATR2, Jun 30 → Oct 9 2026: ST44 28 trades, ₹+7,040, PF 1.97, t 1.17; ST45 20 trades, ₹-4,370, PF 0.56 (too few to read). |
| Read | Neither is profitable and neither is a stable improvement. The opposite reading (ST43 without a 15-candle-pivot break: 248 / 405 trades, PF 0.56 / 0.56; without a session-extreme sweep: 0.56 / 0.57) is consistent on both halves but small, was found on the whole history, and still loses. The entry side of this structure is now tested through slope, trend, swing age, displacement (twice), separation, sweep (any swing, aged, aged + extreme, session / previous-day extreme), equal levels, pivot degree, risk, time of day and CHoCH delay: best stable result PF 0.52–0.56 on futures. |
| Fix sketch | None proposed. Re-run ST43–ST45's option books when the option history is in the store. |

### S64. Lab v2 Strategies 41 and 42: the sweep tightened to a swing at least 40 candles old. Measured: still not a filter; the whole loss sits in trades held under 30 minutes, and no filter so far moves that

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/st41.py` (ST3 + aged sweep), `st42.py` (ST3 + slope size + swing age + aged sweep), `liquidity.py` (`sweep.min_age`, `sweep.extreme`). Built 2026-10-11 at the user's request (a pasted follow-up to S63 naming the definitions and the numbers). |
| Build choices | The user's values, fixed before any run: sweep lookback 30 candles; the swept swing formed at least 40 candles before the sweep candle (and at most 240); displacement off; equal level kept as an off switch. `extreme` (the sweep candle is also the lowest low / highest high of the 30 candles) is built as an off switch (the brief calls it optional) and measured below. ST42 keeps ST38's slope (6 candles, 2.0 pts) and swing-age (10 candles) filters and switches its other three off. |
| Impact | **Measured, Futures, 1 lot, after costs, 2018-01-03 → 2026-10-09.** ST3 4,967 trades, ₹-48,98,447, PF 0.36, avg ₹-986. **ST41 4,529 trades (91% kept), ₹-43,87,908, PF 0.37, avg ₹-969.** Refused by the aged sweep: 438, PF 0.31. With `extreme`: 2,341 kept (47%), PF 0.36 vs 0.36 refused: no separation. Slope + swing age alone: 1,074, ₹-9,31,973, PF 0.48, avg ₹-868. **ST42 1,001 trades, ₹-9,04,679, PF 0.46, win 22.3%, avg ₹-904**; the 73 SETUPs the sweep removes from slope + swing age have PF 0.78 (better than the kept ones). ST42 + `extreme`: 492, PF 0.42. Options via futures W-ATR2 (Jun 30 → Oct 9 2026): ST41 294 trades, ₹-16,669, PF 0.86; ST42 94 trades, ₹+4,225, PF 1.13, t 0.38 (one quarter, not evidence). **By holding time (ST3 / ST41 / ST42): trades held under 30 minutes are 63% / 63% / 55% of the trades and 121% / 122% / 130% of the loss (win rate 0–6%; average ₹-1,700 to ₹-2,300); 30–60 min loses about ₹-950 to ₹-1,240 a trade; 60–120 min wins (47–50%, +₹440 to +₹620); over 120 min wins 79%, +₹3,800 a trade.** Median hold 20 / 21 / 26 min. |
| Read | (1) Aging the swept swing to 40 candles leaves the sweep passing 91%: on 1-minute candles it is still almost always true, and adding the extreme rule halves the trades without changing their quality. The sweep, in every form tried, does not select better SETUPs. (2) Slope size + swing age remain the only filters with an effect (PF 0.36 → 0.48), and that effect is small against the cost of a round trip. (3) The loss is a holding-time fact: the trades stopped out or reversed inside 30 minutes lose more than the whole book; those that survive an hour are profitable. No entry filter tried so far (S62, S63, here) changes the share of quick losers by more than 8 points. |
| Holding-time study, checked in the lab (2026-10-11; the user pasted an outside analysis split at 2023-03-09) | Scratch `hold_study.py` on ST3's 4,967 futures trades, H1 2,479 / H2 2,488. **Reproduced:** median risk (entry to stop) short-hold (<30 min) vs long-hold (>60 min) 15.7 vs 19.4 pts in H1, 22.6 vs 28.7 in H2; median minutes since 09:15 222 vs 122 (H1), 203 vs 131 (H2); LONG share 50–52% in all four. **Not reproduced:** the CHoCH-to-entry delay (the pasted analysis: 8–10 vs 30–39 min): in the lab the median delay is 2 minutes for short and long holds in both halves. **Filters, PF H1 / H2 (share held < 30 min):** ST3 0.36 / 0.36 (63% / 63%); risk ≥ 20: 0.45 / 0.40 (55% / 59%); risk ≥ 25: 0.45 / 0.44 (51% / 56%); entry before 12:15: 0.37 / 0.37; before 11:15: 0.44 / 0.40; delay ≥ 10 min: 0.41 / 0.43 (12% of trades). On the base slope + swing age (0.46 / 0.50; 54% / 54%; 481 / 593 trades): + risk ≥ 20: 0.52 / 0.52 (48% / 52%; 305 / 498); + risk ≥ 25: 0.56 / 0.52 (44% / 49%); + entry before 12:15: 0.53 / 0.48; + risk ≥ 20 + before 12:15: 0.62 / 0.49 (127 / 289); three-way combinations leave 18–60 trades a half. Every slice is net negative on both halves; the average loss per trade in H2 stays ₹-1,000 to ₹-1,240. **Break-even stop after +X pts (stop to the entry price once a candle has been X pts in profit):** worse on ST3 in both halves at every X (X = 15: PF 0.36 → 0.30 / 0.25, it takes out 20% / 31% of the trades at zero before their own exit); on the base it lowers PF in both halves (X = 20: 0.46 → 0.40, 0.50 → 0.43). It removes more winners that revisit the entry than losers. Read: risk ≥ 20 on top of slope + swing age is the only addition that helps in both halves (0.46 / 0.50 → 0.52 / 0.52), and it is small; a break-even overlay is not the missing piece. |
| ST43 built as the reference (2026-10-11, user's go-ahead) | `strategies/st43.py`: ST3 + slope size (6 candles, 2.0 pts) + swing age (10 candles) + stop at least 20 pts from the entry (`SPEC["risk"]`). It reproduces the study's slice exactly. **Futures, 2018-01-03 → 2026-10-09: 803 trades, ₹-7,15,100, PF 0.52, t -6.42; H1 305 trades, ₹-2,04,403, PF 0.52, avg ₹-670; H2 498, ₹-5,10,697, PF 0.52, avg ₹-1,025; every traded year loses** (2018 ₹-16,931 … 2025 ₹-2,51,355, 2026 ₹-96,802). Max profit came before max loss in 452 of 803. Options via futures, Jun 30 → Oct 9 2026 (one quarter): W-ATR2 90 trades, ₹+7,570, PF 1.24, t 0.68; W-ATM 90, ₹+7,927, 1.24; M-ATR2 91, ₹+5,132, 1.15; M-ATM 92, ₹+5,237, 1.15: positive on all four books, none significant. The 20 pts was chosen on the whole history, so the futures figure is in-sample; the option books are the test to repeat when the option history is in the store. Still untested on the entry side: a sweep of a session extreme / previous day's high or low, and a higher-degree pivot as the protected level. |
| Fix sketch | None proposed. The duration result points at the stop and the first 30 minutes, not at the entry filter: what distinguishes, at entry, the 19% that last over an hour from the 63% gone in 30 minutes is unmeasured. A pre-registered study on a first half / second half split, with the user's go-ahead, before any more filters. The higher-degree-pivot / session-extreme sweep of the brief is the remaining liquidity variant. |

| Build choices (the brief gave ideas, no rules or numbers; fixed before any run) | **Sweep** (on): within the 30 candles up to the CHoCH candle, a candle went through a confirmed swing low (bullish; swing high for bearish) and closed back inside; the swing confirmed before that candle and formed at most 240 candles before it. **Equal level** (built, off: the brief says "prefer (or require)"): the swing the CHoCH broke has a twin swing of the same kind within 0.25 × ATR(14), formed up to 240 candles earlier. **Displacement** (on): the SETUP candle's body ≥ 1.0 × ATR(14), or a fair-value gap in the trade's direction (bullish: low[k] > high[k−2]). Not built: order block / last opposing candle (optional in the brief); displacement on the next 1–2 candles (it would move the entry). Futures and options via futures only. |

Each filter alone, kept vs refused (PF): **sweep 4,951 kept vs 16 refused (it passes 99.7%: on 1-minute candles some swing is wicked through and reclaimed within 30 candles almost always)**; equal level 3,514 kept 0.36 vs 0.37; displacement 3,307 kept 0.36 vs 0.37 (average loss ₹-1,013 vs ₹-933: slightly the wrong way, as in S62). Options via futures W-ATR2: ST39 164 trades, ₹-30,624, PF 0.55; ST40 4 trades (the 234 / ₹-24,224 / 0.74 first written here came from a cache bug in `liquidity.py` that reused the futures run's sweep table for the option window; fixed 2026-10-11, futures figures unaffected). |
| Fix sketch | None proposed. The sweep as defined is not a filter. A sweep that means something needs a definition of which liquidity counts, chosen before running, e.g. only the session's high / low or the previous day's, only a swing at least N candles old, or the sweep candle making the lowest low of the lookback. Each is a new pre-registered variant with the user's go-ahead. Displacement has now failed twice (S62, here). |

| Priority | _Open; first numbers recorded._ Related: S62 (ST38). |

Not built: the first brief's distance-from-AVWAP, higher-timeframe AVWAP and volume filters; a protected-level persistence rule inside the engine (it would change which CHoCHs exist; here the filter acts on the SETUP). Futures and options via futures only (thresholds are futures points). Stop / exit = ST3's. Thresholds in points are not scaled to the index level (10,000 in 2018, 25,000 in 2026). |

The average loss per trade barely moves (₹-986 → ₹-868 … ₹-1,177): the filters cut the number of trades, not the loss on each, and a futures round trip costs about ₹1,070 (10 pts slippage + charges). Options via futures W-ATR2: 4 trades. |

Readings to test next, each a new pre-registered variant with the user's go-ahead: drop the trend filter (no effect) and the displacement filter (wrong sign); a separation threshold that scales with the index (10 pts at 25,000 is 0.04%) so it is not a 95% cut; slope + pivot age only (1,074 trades, PF 0.48) as the base. None is near break-even on futures at these costs. |

### S60. Lab v2 Strategy 32: Strategy 1's option legs taken only with the option's own SMA-200 slope. Measured: the kept legs lose less, not significantly, and the design month reverses

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/st32.py` (`leg_filter`; hook `leg_filter` in `core.run_type`). Built 2026-10-03 at the user's request ("buy only when the 200 SMA on options is uptrend, entry Strategy 1 Foundation; long with an uptrend, short with a downtrend"). |
| Build choices (the request did not specify these) | (1) "Uptrend" is the instantaneous slope: SMA[entry candle] > SMA[the candle before] (the user's standing definition of SMA slope). Downtrend is the mirror; a flat SMA refuses both. (2) The SMA is on the option's own candles at the backtest's timeframe, over the contract's whole series, so it is formed from the contract's 200th candle. Before that the leg is refused. (3) Options via futures: each leg is filtered on its own. A bullish SETUP is long CE (needs the CE's SMA rising) plus short PE (needs the PE's SMA falling). Options standalone: the engine's long / short on the option's chart, filtered the same way. A refused leg locks nothing. (4) Futures are refused (the rule is about the options' own trend). Every number is in `SPEC["sma_filter"]`. |
| Impact | **Measured 2026-10-03, options via futures, full-chain window Jun 30 → Sep 25 2026, net after costs per leg.** ST1's legs split by ST32's filter. W-ATR2: kept n 292, mean ₹-42.8 (t -0.60); refused n 274, mean ₹-110.8 (t -2.29); kept − refused +₹68.0 (Welch t +0.79). M-ATR2: kept −55.0 vs refused −124.6, +69.6 (t +0.84). W-ATM: kept −24.3 vs refused −126.1, +101.8 (t +1.15). Books: ST32 W-ATR2 292 trades, net ₹-12,501, PF 0.86; ST1 566 trades, net ₹-42,856, PF 0.76. Standalone W-SCAN: ST32 626 trades, ₹-76,547, PF 0.65; ST1 1,022 trades, ₹-1,15,591, PF 0.67 (the same loss per trade). **ST1's design month (Aug 26 → Sep 25) reverses:** ST32 W-ATR2 48 trades, ₹-11,783, PF 0.27; ST1 92 trades, ₹-4,123, PF 0.86. The direction is consistent across three strike choices but no difference is significant, every kept book still loses, and the window is one quarter. Not evidence of an edge. |
| Change 2026-10-03 (user's request: "Foundation 1 indicators applied directly on the chart of options, not index and futures") | ST32 now runs **Options standalone only** (`TYPES = ("OPT_NATIVE",)`): swings, protected level, AVWAP pair, CHoCH and SETUP are read on each option's own 1-minute chart. Contracts come from `options.native_scan` (every 5 min, ATR2 / ATM / ITM1 / OTM1 per side; one position per side). Options via futures and futures are refused. Before / after on the same window (All data = full-chain Jun 30 → Sep 25 2026): standalone W-SCAN 626 trades, ₹-76,547, PF 0.65 before and after (unchanged, as expected); M-SCAN 646 trades, ₹-1,06,026, PF 0.58. The via-futures book above (W-ATR2 292 trades, ₹-12,501, PF 0.86) is no longer produced by ST32. The SMA drawn on the option panes is now computed over the contract's whole series, the same series the filter reads (before, it was drawn from the backtest window's first candle). |
| With managed exits: ST33 (2026-10-03, user's request "check st32 with managed exits") | New strategy `strategies/st33.py`, not a change to ST32: ST32's entries and SMA filter + ST9's managed exits (3 lots, stop 5% of the entry premium = 1R, lots out at 1R / 2R, trail from 3R 1R behind, no CHoCH exit, flat 15:25). **Measured, Options standalone, Jun 30 → Sep 25 2026, net after costs.** W-SCAN: ST33 641 positions, ₹-2,59,179, ₹-404 per position, **₹-135 per lot**; ST32 626 positions, ₹-76,547, ₹-122 per lot; ST9 standalone (same exits, no SMA filter) 1,033 positions, ₹-3,99,688, ₹-129 per lot. M-SCAN: ST33 ₹-165 per lot, ST32 ₹-164, ST9 ₹-129. Read: (1) the managed exits do not change the loss per lot (−135 vs −122 weekly, −165 vs −164 monthly); with three lots per position the loss per position triples. (2) With managed exits the SMA filter does not help: ST33 loses as much or more per lot than ST9 unfiltered. (3) The 5%-of-premium stop is hit on 1,215 of 1,923 lot exits (63%, ₹-5,90,504); targets and trail earn ₹+3,30,467. (4) Gross is negative before costs (W: ₹-1,42,758), and costs (₹1,16,421) roughly double the loss. One quarter, no significance test across the books (the lots of one position are not independent). Not evidence of an edge. |

A SETUP whose CHoCH-candle level is not beyond the entry is not taken (the engine's rule). "Previous CHoCH high or low" was read as the CHoCH candle's own low / high (Foundation's `choch_candle`); the other Foundation stop (`prev_swing`, the last swing) would be a separate variant. **Measured, same window and books as ST33.** W-SCAN: ST34 596 positions, ₹-2,78,735, ₹-468 per position, **₹-156 per lot**, t -8.91 (ST33 ₹-135, ST32 ₹-122). It is still hit on 64% of lot exits (W: 1,136 of 1,788, ₹-6,14,720). Gross is negative before costs (W ₹-1,69,618). Worse than ST33 on both books; not evidence of an edge. |

The first minute goes against the entry: the SETUP buys / sells the close after a burst. (2) **The stop is inside the noise:** median stop 5.55 pts vs a median 1-minute candle range of 3.05 pts in the 30 min before entry (stop < 1.5 candles in 33% of trades). The stop is touched before 15:25 in 81% of trades; 39% of those then reach +2R the same day; the median best move before 15:25 is 3.19R. (3) **Costs are 35% of 1R:** about 1.94 pts per round trip (slippage both sides + charges) vs the 5.55-pt median stop. Read: no reliable direction edge at entry, a stop that normal candles reach, and costs that need +0.35R just to break even. Untested ideas, each a new pre-registered variant only with the user's go-ahead: enter on a retest of the SETUP level instead of the breakout close; a stop sized to the option's own noise (a multiple of its recent 1-minute range); a longer hold. |

The stop is not the cause; the entry has no direction edge (row above). |

Untested: a weekly shorts-only variant with a 30% stop would be a new strategy with these numbers as its reference, and selling options carries open-ended risk beyond the stop on gaps. |

Read: most of what the shorts earn is the option's time decay, available without any signal; the CHoCH close beats the no-signal control on the check half by about 1 pt (both books) but not on the weekly pick half; the SETUP close does not beat it on weekly. **Filters on shorts (weekly PF pick / check, no filter 1.12 / 1.04):** SMA200 slope (ST32) 1.11 / 0.90; SMA200 slope + price (ST35) 1.17 / 0.90; price vs SMA200 1.14 / 1.03; EMA20 vs EMA50 1.18 / **1.29** (the only one better than no filter on both weekly halves, but 0.78 / 1.15 on monthly); index vs its SMA200 1.34 / 0.86; Supertrend(10,3) 1.07 / 1.16; SMA50 slope 1.01 / 1.21; session VWAP 1.00 / 1.07; RSI, MACD, EMA20 slope, index EMA20 slope ≈ no filter. Monthly: no filter 0.95 / 1.06; price vs SMA200 0.96 / 1.16 (best both halves); SMA200 slope 0.97 / 1.03. **Longs:** every filter loses on both halves in both books; the SMA200 filters are the least bad on the pick half and collapse on the check half. Read: the SMA200 slope does not help shorts (worse than no filter on the weekly check half); no indicator beats no filter consistently across both books and halves; 15 filters tested, so a single good half is expected by chance. |

To read it properly: a longer full-chain window (fill earlier expiries with `tools/breeze_options.py`), and a pre-registered alternative definition (close above / below the SMA instead of its slope, or a slope over N candles) chosen before looking at its numbers. ### S59. Lab v2 Strategy 31 (`htf_v1`): Foundation's 1-minute trades taken only in the 1-hour CHoCH direction. Measured: the filter halves the trades but does not improve them

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab_v2/strategies/st31.py`. Built 2026-10-03 at the user's request ("look at the CHoCH in 1 hour, trade 1-minute Foundation in that direction only"). Look-ahead test: `tests/test_st31_causal.py`. |
| Build choices (the request did not specify these) | (1) The direction is the latest 1-hour CHoCH (`htf.mode: last_choch`); the engine's trend (`trend`, which changes only on flips) is the alternative. On these data the two are identical: every 1-hour CHoCH in the futures file flipped the trend. (2) The 1-hour candles are 09:15-aligned and built from the 1-minute futures. A 1-hour candle is known at start + 60 min (the session's last one at 15:30), and a 1-minute entry uses only the 1-hour candles closed by its own close. (3) The 1-hour engine warms up on 20 sessions before the window. (4) The 1-minute rules are Strategy 1's unchanged (prev-swing stop, exit at the stop or the next 1-minute CHoCH, flat by 15:25). Nothing is taken before the first 1-hour CHoCH. (5) Futures and options via futures only; standalone options and index signals are refused. |
| Impact | **Measured 2026-10-03.** Futures, all data (2021-10 → 2026-09): ST31 2,560 trades, net ₹-26,17,341, PF 0.33. ST1 has 5,242 trades, net ₹-53,98,316, PF 0.33. Same PF: half the trades, the same loss per trade. Before slippage and charges, ST1's trades with the 1-hour direction average +2.66 pts (n 2,589, t +1.66) and those against it +1.57 pts (n 2,643, t +0.97). The difference is +1.09 pts per trade (Welch t +0.48, not significant). It is positive in 5 of 6 years; 2025 reverses (with -1.14, against +3.27). The futures round trip costs about 10 pts of slippage plus charges, so neither group clears costs. Options via futures, Jun 30 → Sep 25 2026 (the full-chain window), W-ATR2: ST31 274 trades, net +₹2,001, PF 1.02, t 0.09; ST1 566 trades, net ₹-42,856, PF 0.76. One short window, t near 0: not evidence. |
| Fix sketch | None proposed. Open options: a stricter 1-hour definition (e.g. only within N 1-hour candles of a flip); the 15-minute or 30-minute candle as the higher timeframe; a pre-registered window before reading any of them, so the choice is not fitted to the numbers above. Each needs the user's go-ahead. |
| Effort | **S** per variant (the parameters are in `SPEC["htf"]`). |
| Priority | _Open; first numbers recorded._ |

### S58. Lab Foundation engine (Strategies 1–4 and every strategy built on their SETUPs) goes silent in a sustained trend: no CHoCH, so no SETUP, while the protected level stays far behind price

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab/engine.py` `run()` (v1) and its port `research/strategy_lab_v2/core.py` `_engine` (v2, trade-for-trade identical). Noticed 2026-10-01 while building lab v2. |
| What | Strategy 1 (1-minute futures) has its last CHoCH on 2026-09-04 15:20 (a bearish flip) and its last trade at 15:22 the same day. The data runs to 2026-09-25: 13 more sessions, with no CHoCH and no trade. Over that stretch NIFTY futures fell from 24,045 to 23,186 (low 23,056). The protected high stayed at 24,058 / 24,070 (later 23,923) and was never broken. BOS events kept printing (366 after 09-04), but entries need a SETUP and a SETUP needs a CHoCH. v1 gives the same result (parity test on 2026-08-26 → 09-25). |
| Why it matters | Foundation only re-enters after a protected-level break. In a one-way trend the protected level is the swing that started the leg, so the strategy stays flat through the trend's continuation, which is the move it is supposed to be aligned with. Whether this is intended (trade only reversals / retests) has not been decided. |
| Impact | **Unquantified.** To measure it: count the sessions with no SETUP per strategy over All data (v2: `python backtest.py ST1 all --type FUT`, then group trades by session), and compare with the sessions' net NIFTY move. Then decide whether a BOS-continuation entry is worth a pre-registered test on a frozen window. |
| Measured on the long history (2026-10-10) | Lab v2 now reads the Breeze store (index and futures Jan 2018 → Oct 9 2026). On one continuous run from 2018, Strategy 1 (1-minute futures) has **no CHoCH from 2020-11-04 13:40 to 2022-03-07 09:23: 16 months, no SETUP, no trade**, while the futures went from 11,820 to above 18,000 and back to 15,840 (the protected low sat at 14,193 in between and was never broken). CHoCHs per year: 2018 1,518; 2019 1,656; 2020 1,300; **2021 0**; 2022 1,394; 2023 1,746; 2024 1,274; 2025 2,354; 2026 1,545. ST1 Futures, all data: 8,336 trades, ₹-81,99,748, PF 0.30; every traded year loses. The engine's state is path-dependent: the old files began on 2021-10-01 with a fresh state and did trade Oct 2021 – Mar 2022, so **a backtest's trades depend on where its history starts** (a window run with a 2-session warm-up is not a slice of the long run for Foundation strategies). Not decided: whether to re-anchor after N sessions without a CHoCH, or to define every backtest as a slice of one run from the first session (as FZ and the learner already do). Either changes trades and needs the user's go-ahead. |
| Fix sketch | None proposed. Any change (for example a SETUP after a BOS in the trend's direction, or re-anchoring the protected level after N BOS) changes what trades get taken. It needs the user's go-ahead and a before / after run on the same window (rule 3). |
| Effort | Measurement **S**; any rule change **M**. |
| Addendum 2026-10-11 (a present instance, found from the user's question about the chart of 2026-09-21) | Strategy 1, All data run: no trade after 2026-09-04 through 2026-10-09 (the last session in the data). On 2026-09-21 the chart draws what the engine holds: trend AVWAP anchored 2026-09-04 15:13, at about 23,500; protected level 23,923.5, set on 2026-09-04; the futures trade 23,360 - 23,475; all 21 events of the day are BOS down; no SETUP. The futures fell from 24,100 to 22,500 over those weeks, every swing high since sits below the old trend AVWAP, so none qualifies as a protected level and no CHoCH can print until price is back above 23,923.5. The same rules started fresh do trade: the 'This month' run (warm-up from 2026-09-29) has 15 trades in October, and Strategy 66 (gap-day reset, S84) trades on every one of those sessions. So Strategy 1's result depends on where its window starts. The chart is right; the rule is the gap. Unquantified beyond S84. |
| Priority | _Open; noticed, not measured._ Related: S57 (a touch that uses up the protected level without a CHoCH under `choch_mode close`). |

### S55. Redirected question from S54: can NIFTY's own directional acceleration be detected early enough to beat the option's theta and expiry-day asymmetry?

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | Follow-up to [`research/strategy_lab/studies/waves/`](../research/strategy_lab/studies/waves/) (S54). Filed 2026-09-30 from the user's review of the S54 report. No code yet. |
| Why | S54 found the option "staircase" is NIFTY × delta (≈ 85 %) + gamma (13–18 %), IV only 3–4 %, and the staircase shape itself is reproduced by shuffled bars. User's reframing: the staircase is the effect, the cause is a NIFTY move; ask whether the NIFTY move is predictable early. |
| What S54 already measured that bounds it | (1) The option wave-2 signal read as a NIFTY signal had no NIFTY follow-through: 30-min NIFTY move in the option's direction +5.8 / +3.9 / −6.5 bp (2024 / 2025 / 2026). (2) The option response is lopsided near expiry, so a NIFTY edge must clear a hit-rate bar. ATM CE, 2025, for a ±0.25 % NIFTY move over 15 min: DTE 3–4 +20.9 / −20.2 %, drift −0.7 %; DTE 0 +48.8 / −70.3 %, drift −6.9 %. A symmetric ±0.25 % call must be right about 50 % of the time at DTE 3–4 but about 59 % on expiry day just to break even before costs (p·48.8 = (1−p)·70.3). (3) NIFTY index candles carry no volume; any volume profile must come from futures or front-weekly option volume. |
| Impact | **Measured 2026-09-30** ([`studies/accel/REPORT.md`](../research/strategy_lab/studies/accel/REPORT.md)): pre-registered verdict **2 — structure, not tradable**. Volume-backed acceleration (PV), 30-min NIFTY futures excess over momentum-matched bars: 2024 +1.3 bp (in-sample), 2025 −0.2 bp [−4.0, +3.8], 2026 +5.0 bp [+0.6, +10.0] (n 67). Price-only acceleration is the same (+4.9 bp in 2026). Volume adds nothing in any year. Compression, opening-range and 1-minute signals: nothing. Real ATM options on PV at 30 min, net: −3.5 % / +2.3 % / +18.4 % [+1.2, +40.0] (n 105 / 52 / 25). |
| Fix sketch | Nothing to build. The clean next checks, both needing the user: (1) run the frozen code once on the unread 2021-10 → 2023-12 futures reserve (H1 on P and PV, same rule; pre-register up / CE and down / PE as separate tests too, the user's 2026-09-30 question); (2) paper-forward from 2026-10. Post-hoc CE / PE split (LEDGER): no consistent asymmetry on NIFTY; options CE −3.0 / +6.7 / +19.1 %, PE −3.8 / −0.7 / +17.2 %, all intervals across zero. |
| Reserve confirmation (2026-10-02, `CONFIRM.md`) | 2021-10 → 2023-12: PV +1.4 bp [−1.3, +4.1] → **inconclusive**; price-only +2.2 [−0.1, +4.6]; **CE vs PE: no difference** (up − down +0.9 [−5.8, +7.1]). Price-acceleration excess is positive in all six periods 2021–2026 but significant only in 2022 and 2026: a weak, uneven ~+2 bp / 30 min continuation, below the expiry-day option hurdle. |
| Effort | **L** (study + confirmation, done). |
| Priority | _Done 2026-10-02. Only paper-forward remains; no option rule follows._ |

|---|---|
| Where | [`research/strategy_lab/studies/waves/`](../research/strategy_lab/studies/waves/) — `PREREG.md` (design frozen before any outcome), the study code, `events.csv`, `REPORT.md`. Filed 2026-09-30. No strategy file, no `TradeConfig`, no bean. |
