### S53. Lab Strategies 29–30 (rainbow ribbon intraday, `rainbow_v1`): what "rainbow" and "best" were taken to mean, the pre-registered grid, and the numbers

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`research/strategy_lab/rainbow.py`](../research/strategy_lab/rainbow.py), `strategies/strategy_29.json` (1-minute) / `strategy_30.json` (5-minute), `studies/rainbow_grid.py` (+ `rainbow_grid.json`), `tests/test_rainbow.py`. Filed 2026-09-29. |
| Why | User (2026-09-29): "create a new strategy of best intraday with rainbow for 1min and 5min". Nothing in the repo or the user's earlier material defines either word, so the build took: **rainbow** = Mel Widner's Rainbow Charts (10 recursive 2-period simple averages of the close and its oscillator) as the default, with an EMA ribbon (5/8/13/21/34/55) as the alternative — both are values of the file's `rainbow.kind`; **best intraday** = the variant a pre-registered grid picks on Jan 5 – Jun 30 2026 (in sample) and that Jul 1 – Sep 25 2026 (out of sample) then reports, all positions flat by 15:25. **Pre-registered before any run (this entry):** grid = kind (widner, ema) × trigger (cross = a fresh close outside the ribbon; pullback = the same after the ribbon's far edge kept its slope over the last 5 candles and the ribbon was touched in them; the trigger was first written as "the fan held 5 candles" and changed to the slope test on the synthetic cases before any data run, because the fastest lines of a 2-period ribbon swap order on every dip) × fan (full = every faster line beyond the next; none) × oscillator minimum (0, 25; lookback 10) × exits (band = the ribbon's far side as the stop and a close back through it as the exit, lab.tranches; s9 = the lab's managed 1R / 2R scale-out + trail 3R−1 at stop 50; trail = managed all-lot trail 3R−2 at stop 50) = 48 variants per timeframe; fixed: 3 lots, one position per contract, entries 09:20–14:30, square-off 15:25, near-month futures on their own candles, lab costs. **Rule:** the highest in-sample net among variants with ≥ 30 in-sample positions and PF > 1; if none qualifies the highest in-sample net is taken and said so. One run per variant over Jan–Sep, read in two parts by entry date. Chosen in the build, not by the user: the grid itself, the 09:20–14:30 window, 3 lots, stop 50 in the managed variants, the EMA periods. **Corrections after the adversarial review of the module (2026-09-29/30, before the corrected grid's numbers were read):** (a) 32 of the 48 variants (not 12) use the two S50 managed-exit blocks, and under those blocks the one-position lock refuses most signals, so the exit block shapes the entry set too — the exits are not a neutral factor; (b) the pullback trigger as first coded (touch of the band within 5 candles) could never fail after a fresh close outside the band, so it was redefined as a re-emergence (a close outside the band on the trade's side within the last 5 candles, then back, with the far edge keeping its slope) — the grid had already run once in full on the pre-review code (old pullback, running-sum SMA, lot-exit count) and both timeframes' tables were read: every one of the 96 variants lost in sample and out of sample, and the picks were 1-minute EMA · cross · fan none · osc ≥ 25 · all-lot trail 3R−2 and 5-minute Widner · cross · fan full · osc ≥ 25 · all-lot trail 3R−2 — so the fixes below were made after seeing numbers; none of them was chosen to move a pick (the qualification change cannot, since no variant qualified), and the published numbers are the re-run on the fixed code; (c) the Widner lines are computed as exact window means (a running sum let floating-point drift decide band and fan ties, so a window's start changed the signal set); (d) the selection rule counts positions (distinct entries), not the lab's lot-exit records, and a PF of None with a positive net counts as > 1, ties by higher PF then grid order; (e) the ribbon is not reset at the session open (the first candles of a session read the previous close), so a 09:20 start does not stop early entries — the share of entries per hour is in the payload; (f) `tests/test_rainbow.py` runs the rule on the Jul–Sep window for its truncation check, so that window's signal count (not its P&L) was seen before the pick; (g) the band variants carry an unused 50-pt position stop (strategy mode uses the ribbon's far side); on the 1-minute candles that band stop is often within the 5-pt slippage. |
| Impact | **Measured 2026-09-30 (studies/rainbow_grid.json, the run after the review fixes; 3 lots, lab costs: 5-pt slippage a side, Zerodha futures charges, lot 65; net ₹ after costs; 'lot exits' are the lab's trade records, up to three per managed position).** **1-minute (ST29)**: pick = EMA 5–55 ribbon, cross, fan none, osc ≥ 25, all-lot trail 3R−2 (NO variant qualified - the highest in-sample net taken); in sample 277 positions, net ₹-395,954, PF 0.83, t -1.22; out of sample 112 positions, net ₹-334,212, PF 0.57, t -2.38; its out-of-sample rank 11 of 48. Across the grid: 0 of 48 variants positive in sample, 0 out of sample; the best out-of-sample variant was Widner 10 × SMA(2), cross, fan none, osc ≥ 25, all-lot trail 3R−2 at ₹-288,563; the worst in sample lost ₹-20,411,088 (5744 positions, band stop + band exit). Band exits (the ribbon's own stop and exit) averaged 1,807 in-sample positions and ₹-6,479,492 per variant against 285 and ₹-754,541 for the managed exits (whose one-position lock refuses most signals): a fast ribbon flips sides every few candles, so the ribbon's own exits trade the costs away. **5-minute (ST30)**: pick = Widner 10 × SMA(2), pullback, fan full, osc ≥ 25, all-lot trail 3R−2 (NO variant qualified - the highest in-sample net taken); in sample 138 positions, net ₹-150,088, PF 0.87, t -0.68; out of sample 81 positions, net ₹-131,634, PF 0.73, t -1.08; its out-of-sample rank 2 of 48. Across the grid: 0 of 48 variants positive in sample, 0 out of sample; the best out-of-sample variant was Widner 10 × SMA(2), cross, fan full, osc ≥ 25, all-lot trail 3R−2 at ₹-123,638; the worst in sample lost ₹-3,937,188 (1114 positions, band stop + band exit). Band exits (the ribbon's own stop and exit) averaged 347 in-sample positions and ₹-1,049,482 per variant against 196 and ₹-495,639 for the managed exits (whose one-position lock refuses most signals): a fast ribbon flips sides every few candles, so the ribbon's own exits trade the costs away. Reading: 48 nested variants picked in sample make the pick's out-of-sample rank a weak test (the maximum of 48 noisy nets is optimistic by construction; a rank near the middle means the pick was noise, a rank of 1 means the in-sample order held); with every variant negative in sample on both timeframes the honest statement is that a fresh close outside a fast moving-average ribbon on NIFTY futures, flat by 15:25, does not pay its costs in 2026 under any of these 48 rule sets - the two files record the least-losing rule, not an edge. The in-sample half has 100 sessions with a hole (no May 2026 contract: Apr 28 -> May 29), the out-of-sample half 62. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing changes without the user (Rule 0(c)); each is a new pre-registered variant with its own in-sample / out-of-sample read, never a retune of the picks. Candidates: the ribbon on the index with trades on futures (`underlying INDEX`, refused in v1); option types (refused in v1); a stop by the ribbon in the managed mode (position.stop is fixed points); a ribbon reset at the session open (today the first candles read the previous close); a pullback that also requires no close through the far side inside the window (the review measured it keeps about half the pullback signals); a fan-collapse exit; slower periods (the 2-period Widner ribbon flips sides every few candles). |
| Effort | **S** per variant (one strategy file; a run is seconds). |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-29 before the grid ran; numbers added 2026-09-30 from the re-run on the reviewed code. Next step is the user's: keep the pair as a measured negative, or pre-register one of the variants under Fix sketch._ |

### S52. Lab Strategies 25–28 (CHoCH to CHoCH put retest, `c2c_v1`): what the build had to decide that the spec does not say, and the first numbers

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`research/strategy_lab/c2c.py`](../research/strategy_lab/c2c.py), `strategies/strategy_25.json`–`strategy_28.json` (`c2c` block), `tests/test_c2c.py`. Source: the user's *Nifty CHoCH to CHoCH in Plain English* PDF (2026-09-29, titled "Strategy 52" on the platform it came from). Filed 2026-09-29. |
| Why | Built as asked; the user chose (2026-09-29) all combinations of: window PCR rules on / off × stops on the candle close / by touch (the four files), and index / futures signals (backtests of each file), puts only. **Decided in the build, not by the spec — each changes trades:** (1) *regime* = the engine's trend flip (a close through the protected level AND the AVWAP); a CHoCH that breaks the protected level without flipping is ignored, for entry and for the bullish exit. The spec's "a regime flips when a candle closes through the level" may mean the protected-level break alone. (2) *anchored VWAP* = the engine's AVWAP from the swing high the flip re-anchors at, equal-weighted on the index (no volume), so the ±50-pt band on index signals is around a TWAP, not a VWAP. (3) the spec lists six entry rules and says "all seven"; the seventh is taken from its summary sentence ("this isn't the breakdown bar itself") = the swing-high confirmation is not on the flip candle. (4) *trail*: 5 pts behind the peak premium once the premium has gained, never lowered; for any premium above ₹100 that is tighter than the 5 % stop from the first uptick, so in practice the stop is ~5 pts (touch books: the entry candle's own high counts). (5) exits checked stop → bullish flip → 15:15 ladder; the flip exits at that candle's close (the spec gives no fill); the ladder runs on the first put candle labelled ≥ 15:15, once per session, and not on an entry day when the entry came after 15:15. (6) one position at a time (the spec's "one put"); positional, no square-off. (7) expiry "nearest, 15–30 days" = nearest monthly ≥ 15 days out. (8) spec guard rails not built: max delta 0.3, gamma 0.1, vega 500, 2 % max-loss cap, 10 % bid-ask tolerance, and the live-balance sizing. Note delta ≤ 0.3 would reject an ITM1 put outright — the spec's own strike rule and its guard rail disagree. **Window PCR** (user's choice over dropping it): Σ PE OI / Σ CE OI within 300 pts of the ATM on the traded expiry, NA below 9 strikes with both rights (NA blocks an entry, never fires an exit). It is not the exchange PCR the thresholds were written for: at the candidates on index signals its median is 1.16 (p25 1.06, p75 1.35, min 0.99), so **the 15:15 "PCR > 0.95" exit is true on nearly every day — every PCR-book position alive at 15:15 is closed then**, and the book is intraday in effect. **Data:** only 2026-06-23 → 07-13 and 07-22 → 09-14 can be priced (full-chain monthly data: Jul-28, Aug-25 Breeze, Sep-29 Kite; Oct-27 missing), 2.5 months. |
| Impact | **Measured 2026-09-29, one window (2026-06-23 → 2026-09-14), 1 lot, ITM1 put, net ₹ after charges and 0.5-pt slippage — too few trades for any verdict (every t below 1.8 in absolute value):** index signals — ST25 (PCR, close stops) 31 trades +10,009 PF 1.70; ST26 (no PCR, close) 44 trades −447 PF 0.98; ST27 (PCR, touch) 33 trades −2,178 PF 0.76; ST28 (no PCR, touch) 47 trades −6,894 PF 0.52. Futures signals — ST25 28 trades +15,248 PF 2.16; ST26 60 +15,276 PF 1.59; ST27 28 +3,985 PF 1.53; ST28 67 +2,917 PF 1.17. Exits are almost all the 5-pt trail (e.g. ST25 index: 23 trail, 6 stop, 2 ladder PCR; ST26 index: 29 trail, 9 stop, 6 bullish flip) — the spec's own note that trades close within minutes shows here too. Close stops beat touch stops in all four pairings in this window; PCR on beat off on index signals only. Entry funnel ST25 index: 304 swing highs → 144 in a bearish regime → 136 not the flip candle → 66 in the ±50 band → 62 above the day floor → 13 PCR NA, 34 PCR < 1.3 → 31 taken, 3 locked. What would measure more: the Oct-27 (and earlier monthly) full chains, `tools/kite_options.py` / `tools/breeze_options.py`, then an All-data run; a full-chain PCR series to test the thresholds as written. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing changes without the user (Rule 0(c)). Open choices, each a `c2c` value or a small switch: regime = protected-level break vs the engine flip (1); trail only after a set gain, or the 5 % stop alone (4); a PCR exit threshold re-based on the window PCR's own distribution, or the exit rule dropped (it currently makes the PCR books intraday); the delta cap vs ITM1 conflict (8). |
| Effort | **S** per variant (one strategy file; a run is under a minute). **M** to fetch the missing monthly chains. |
| Research programme (2026-09-29) | The user set a 16-phase research programme on this strategy (signal edge vs option edge, bull / bear / flat, decay, DTE, strike, exits, walk-forward). Ledger: [`research/strategy_lab/studies/c2c/LEDGER.md`](../research/strategy_lab/studies/c2c/LEDGER.md). **Phase 1 audit (EXP-001) found blocking defects:** (A1) the Breeze index 5-minute file the lab uses for INDEX signals has corrupt candles — 111 bars with a range above 150 pts, 33 of 162 15:20 candles in 2026 above 60 pts where Kite and the long index file are flat — so every INDEX backtest in the lab, Strategy 25–28's index numbers above included, is affected; (A3) the engine's regime is path-dependent: a flip needs a close through the AVWAP anchored at the trend's start, so after a long trend no flip happens — run continuously the regime froze for 330 sessions (2020-11 →) and 300 sessions (2023-11 → 2025-02), 0 flips in 2021 and 2024, while a 5-session re-warm (the lab's per-backtest start) gives 90 flips in 2024-H2. Signals depend on the run's start date. (A4) "next candle open" enters on the 09:15 print after a session's last candle (14 of 1,384 signals). Phase 3 signal study (EXP-002, INCONCLUSIVE because of A3): no directional edge measured on NIFTY for either side 2021–2026 (bearish excess over a same-time-of-day random entry +1.1 pts at 60 min, t 0.62; bullish −2.0, t −1.29). **Decision needed before any further phase:** the regime definition (A3) and the index data source (A1). |
| Research programme, development results (2026-09-30) | User decisions: both regime definitions carried (R-A = 5-session rolling memory, R-B = no AVWAP in the regime; the A3 freeze comes from the AVWAP-qualified protected-level candidates, not the flip line — EXP-003), the long index file for research only (A1 left in the lab), all phases run. Development data only (index 2021–2023; options 2021 and 42 of 52 expiries of 2022 from `tools/breeze_options.py --research`, 2023 not yet fetched); 2024 validation and 2025–2026 blind untouched. **No option edge in any of ~1,000 cells:** all 60 strike × expiry baseline books lose (EXP-008); every exit and holding period loses on the bearish side (EXP-011); the exact baseline (R-A bearish) −80,001 ₹ per lot over 377 trades, PF 0.64, t −3.25 (EXP-005). Measured causes: puts lose ≈ 5–7 pts to the close through an intraday IV decline at every DTE (EXP-004); the 5-pt trail closes 74–89 % of trades inside one candle's noise (EXP-005/011/016); slippage decides the sign of every near-zero cell (EXP-016); movement is not missing — NIFTY moves 33–89 pts favourably before the close after almost every signal — direction is (EXP-006). **One lead:** bearish signals with a narrow retest band (±20 instead of ±50) show a monotone NIFTY-level excess (R-B +7.1 pts to the close, t 3.07) that walk-forward carried to 2022 (+6.8, t 1.52) and 2023 (+6.2, t 1.76) — a signal-level lead, not yet an option edge (as an option trade still −157 ₹ per signal). Next: finish the 2023 fetch, re-run, then test the frozen baseline and the ±20 lead once on 2024. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-29; development phases done 2026-09-30 (ledger EXP-001 … EXP-017); 2024 validation pending the option fetch._ |

### S51. Lab Strategies 19–24 (learner, `rl_v1`): what is out of sample and what is not, and the build choices behind the learner

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`research/strategy_lab/rl.py`](../research/strategy_lab/rl.py), `strategies/strategy_19.json`–`strategy_24.json` (`rl` block), `tests/test_rl.py`, the Journal tab. Filed 2026-09-29. |
| Why | User (2026-09-29): "journal every trade and induce reinforcement learning after each trade to optimize strategy, money management and profit factor", all four decisions (take / skip, exit profile, size, stop), all three reward versions tried separately, learning from the whole data, both 1-minute and 5-minute entries, no shortcuts for hardware. Built as a contextual bandit (linear Thompson sampling, one model per action, full-information updates in exit-time order). Chosen in the build, not by the user: (1) the action set = skip + 3 exit profiles × stops 35 / 50 / 75 × lots 1–3 (27 + skip); the three profiles are the 2026 exit study's candidates (S50), so **a 2026 window is out of sample for the learned weights but not for the action set**; (2) the 21 features (rl.FEATURES), ridge 1.0, exploration 0.3, reward scaling (net ₹ / (lot × 50 pts); R = net points per lot / stop; pf = losses × 1.5), all fixed before any learner result was seen and recorded in each file's `rl.notes`; (3) "learn from the beginning" = the learner starts empty at Oct 2021 and learns through the file's end once per strategy, each backtest a window of that run; (4) an outcome is learnable only when every lot closed for a reason that does not depend on where the data ends (stop / trail / target anywhere; other exits only before the last bar), so truncation cannot change a decision; (5) the yardsticks: the base book = first profile · stop 50 · max lots (Strategy 9's rule) and the random book = a seeded uniformly random action per SETUP (skip included), both run as their own books under the lab's one-position-per-contract lock so they are comparable with the learner's book; the oracle = the best action per SETUP in hindsight, which ignores the lock and is a bound, not a book; (6) sizing reads lots off the max-lots outcome (each lot's exit is independent in `lab.manage`), so 1 lot = the trailing lot, 2 lots = first target + trailing lot; (7) futures only in v1; (8) after the adversarial review (2026-09-29): an intraday profile is not an action at or after its square-off time (for the learner, the base book and the control alike); rewards are per-lot values clipped at ±10 × lots (so 3-lot outcomes are not censored more than 1-lot ones and the R reward, the position's R, can learn sizing); the oracle is the best net among feasible actions; the session-result features are per-lot R multiples whatever the reward. (9) **From the same review (validity and integration lenses, 2026-09-29), built the same day unless marked OPEN:** OPEN — the reward of an action is its own outcome at that SETUP, so the SETUPs a long-held position locks the book out of are in no reward — the learner is biased toward the positional profiles relative to their value to the book (documented in rl.py and on the Journal tab; the first publish shows it: ST19 all-data 2026 locked 372 of 824 SETUPs and took 59; a reward charged with the locked-out SETUPs' base outcome, or a `lock: none` copy of a file, would measure it — a behaviour change for the user to call); the ±10 clip binds on the right tail only (losses are stop-bounded), mostly on the 3-lot trailing arms — the clips per action are in the payload and a tile; exploration is inert under full information (a decision is close to the argmax of 27 posterior means, so `pred` is biased upward against the realised reward) and lots are chosen by expected value with no risk term — said in rl.py and on the tab, with a pred-vs-realised correlation and gap tile (taken trades, and all feasible actions: the measure of whether the features carry any signal); 200 seeded random books with the learner's percentile, and 200 matched permutations (the learner's own window decisions shuffled across the window's SETUPs in time order under the same lock, same action multiset so the same take rate and sizes; the learner's percentile and the share at or above it) — `control_draws` in the `rl` block; the seed spread: the learner run from empty on 4 more seeds (`spread_seeds`, default seed + 1000 k), each window's net per seed with mean and sd; a month straddling the window start is a learning row and a scored row, and a test asserts the scored rows sum to the tiles; the six files' backtest notes corrected ('Unseen test' and 'Design period' are inside S50's 2026 exit-study window, so not unseen by the action set; 'This month' is not intraday — two profiles hold overnight); rl.py is part of a learner strategy's results/history version id (the change list says 'code (rl.py, the learner) changed'); a backtest square-off override on a learner row is refused (each profile carries its own square-off); learner rows close the lots of a contract whose candles end before its expiry date at its last candle ('expiry', MAR22) — the lab's own futures rows keep the calendar rule (cosmetic in 2026 windows; noted here rather than re-hashing every stored result); OPEN — the learner's capital figure assumes 3 lots at every SETUP (position.lots) whatever it took. |
| Impact | **Unquantified until the build publishes** (the 2026-window build on the causality-fixed code, commit 9a69356, runs after this entry). What to read: per learner strategy, learner net vs base vs random control vs oracle, month by month, and the action mix — read in this order: the seed spread (one seed is one path: four features and the lock depend on the learner's own decisions), the matched-permutation percentile (where it traded, at its own take rate and sizes), the random-book percentile (skip is 1 in 28, so random books trade almost every free SETUP: necessary, not sufficient), and only then the gap to the base; the oracle is never below 0 at a SETUP and sums the maximum of 27 noisy outcomes, an upper bound only; the seven windows are nested cuts of one run, not independent checks; every action model gets every SETUP's outcome, so update COUNT is not the issue — the pred-vs-realised correlation over all feasible actions measures whether the 21 SETUP-time features carry any signal; regime drift (2021–2025 weights applied to 2026) is one reading of a weak 2026, not the only one. **First publish (2026-09-29, single seed, one random draw, before item 9 was built)**, 2026 all-data window, learner vs base book vs one random book: ST19 (1m, net) +55,217 vs −674,314 vs −125,434 (824 SETUPs: 59 taken, 393 skipped, 372 locked — the lock bias in numbers); ST20 (5m, net) +60,001 vs −319,821 vs −82,301; ST21 (1m, R) +247,931; ST22 (5m, R) −33,824; ST23 / ST24 (pf) skipped almost every SETUP (0 and 10 taken). Every taken decision was a positional profile at stop 75 or 50; the intraday base profile was never chosen. **Second publish (2026-09-29, item 9 built; 2026 all-data window, 5 seeds, 200 random books, 200 matched permutations):** ST19 (1m, net): seed nets +55,217 / +188,961 / +166,323 / +40,696 / −12,068 (mean +87,826, sd 86,101), 74th percentile of the random books, 52nd of the permutations (p = 0.48) — its net says nothing about where it trades; ST20 (5m, net): mean +21,745, sd 133,801 (3 of 5 positive), 94.5th random, 76.5th permutations (p = 0.235); ST21 (1m, R): +247,931 / +469,288 / +650,087 / +201,882 / +579,856 (mean +429,809, sd 198,510, all positive), 95.5th random, 89.5th permutations (p = 0.105; on the Unseen test 91.5th, p = 0.085) — the only learner with a consistent sign and a placement signal, and still short of p 0.05; ST22 (5m, R): mean −30,826 (1 of 5 positive), 48th of the permutations; ST23 / ST24 (pf): skip nearly everything (ST24: 11th of its permutations). The correlation between `pred` and the realised reward over all feasible actions is 0.04–0.15 in every window: the 21 SETUP-time features carry almost no signal about post-entry outcomes; the learner's gap to the base and random books comes from skipping (take rates 5–35 % against the random books' 20–63 %) and from holding positional profiles at stop 75, not from reading the SETUP. Clips: 2–3 % of action outcomes in the 1-minute windows (473 of 22,158), none in the Unseen test. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing changes without the user (Rule 0(c)). Next candidates, each a strategy-file value: action sets not derived from 2026 (e.g. the pre-study profiles only), a recency weight on updates (forgetting), a 2024–2025 scored window as a second check, option types as a v2. |
| Effort | **S** per variant (one strategy file; the learning run is minutes on 5-minute, about an hour on 1-minute). |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-29; first publish read the same day; item (9) built except the lock-cost reward and the capital figure, which need the user._ |

### S48. Lab Strategies 11–12 (stop and reverse on Strategies 9–10): the readings, and what a reversal costs in a chop

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab/strategies/strategy_11.json` / `strategy_12.json` (`position.reverse`), [`lab.py`](../research/strategy_lab/lab.py) `reversal_of()`, `flip()`, the three managed-exit call sites. Filed 2026-09-28. |
| Why | User's spec (2026-09-28): duplicate Strategies 9 and 10, "SL will have stop and reverse". Readings confirmed with the user: only the **initial stop** reverses (a trail stop after 3R is a profit exit); **once per signal** (`max: 1`); the reversed position is a **full new position** (3 lots, stop 1R on the other side, 1R / 2R targets, trail from 3R); option types reverse into the **opposite signal's legs**. Chosen in the build, not by the user: (1) futures and standalone options reverse at the stop fill on the same contract; Options (via futures) reverse at the new option's close of the stop candle (a different contract has no fill at the stop price); (2) no reversal at or after the square-off time; (3) the reversed position is subject to the strike lock like any position (on futures the original's lock covers it; a new option contract is checked at its own entry); (4) a reversal's own stop only reverses again if `max` > 1. |
| Impact | **First trials measured** (current rules, intraday 15:25): futures 29 Jun – 2 Jul, Strategy 11 +₹8,631 = Strategy 9 +₹8,631 — on 29 Jun the reversal short at 09:27 (stopped 10:25, −₹13,077) replaced Strategy 9's 09:31 short (stopped 10:24, −₹13,077); options via futures W-ATR2 1 Jul −₹7,637 vs −₹3,841 (both reversed legs stopped again within 5–15 minutes); standalone W-SCAN 1 Jul −₹22,382 vs −₹23,555 (30 of 72 lot exits are reversal lots). **Unquantified** over the backtest windows — read `results/history` and the dashboard once the full build has run; compare Strategy 11 vs 9 and 12 vs 10 on the same windows. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing changes without the user (Rule 0(c)). Candidates, each a strategy-file value: `max` > 1 (true stop-and-reverse chain); reverse fewer lots; reverse only futures. |
| Effort | **S** per variant. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-28._ |

### S45. Lab signal source and futures contract: index signals, near-month futures, and the unadjusted roll

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`research/strategy_lab/lab.py`](../research/strategy_lab/lab.py) (`run_variant` futures leg, `fut_contracts`, `main` run keys), `underlying` in the strategy files and backtest entries, [`tools/breeze_history.py`](../research/strategy_lab/tools/breeze_history.py). Filed 2026-09-27. |
| Why | User decisions 2026-09-27: every strategy can run its signals on the near-month futures or on the NIFTY index (`underlying`); on the index the AVWAP is **equal-weighted** (the index has no volume); futures trade the **near-month contract only**, from the day after the previous monthly expiry to its own expiry, closed at the expiry close, never the upcoming contract. Chosen in the build, not by the user: (1) with index signals, a stop crossed on the index fills at the futures price of that candle shifted by the stop's distance (index stop + that candle's basis); entries and other exits at the futures close of the candle; (2) the futures signal series is the stitched near-month series **without back-adjustment**, so each monthly roll is a price step (the old-to-new contract spread) that the swing / protected-level logic reads as a move; (3) the Kite July–September files hold the September contract throughout (next month until 26 Aug), so every futures result so far was on the upcoming contract in July and August; the near-month build changes that. (4) FZ reads `front_month` from the 1-minute file: on the near-month files it is 1 on every session, so FZ's volume-NA share (S37) changes. |
| Impact | **Unquantified** until the near-month history is built (user's Breeze login). First trial on the current files (Strategy 2, Design window, index signals, futures traded): 18 trades, +₹4,330 vs 26 trades, −₹15,560 on futures signals — one window, before the near-month files, not a verdict. To measure: after `tools/breeze_history.py --build`, `results/history` records every strategy's before / after (Kite September contract → near-month); run each strategy with `--underlying INDEX` on the same windows; for (2), a back-adjusted signal series as a strategy-file option run beside the unadjusted one. |
| Fix sketch | (2) is the open design question: unadjusted (as built), difference back-adjusted, or signals on the index only when a roll is inside the window. Nothing changes without the user (Rule 0(c)). |
| Effort | **S** per measured run; **M** for a back-adjusted series option. |
| Measured | **2026-09-28, first near-month / 5-year build** (Breeze 1 Oct 2021 – 25 Sep 2026, 1,188 sessions; the April 2025 and May 2026 contracts still missing — their monthly dates were first computed wrong, the corrected fetch is 32 requests). Same windows, September contract → near-month (`results/history`): Strategy 1 Design 55 / −₹16,985 → 48 / −₹11,191, Unseen 146 / −₹1,83,830 → 229 / −₹2,91,845; Strategy 2 Design 26 / −₹15,560 → 24 / −₹14,303, Unseen 47 / −₹56,783 → 35 / −₹59,728; Strategy 4 Design 10 / +₹16,742 → 10 / +₹15,800. Five years, futures signals vs index signals (futures traded, All data): Strategy 1 −₹49.7L vs −₹37.4L; Strategy 2 −₹5.05L (PF 0.81) vs −₹1.72L (0.92); Strategy 3 −₹27.2L vs −₹23.3L; **Strategy 4 −₹1.00L (0.95) vs +₹4.30L (1.28, 512 trades, t 1.18, max DD −₹2.41L; by year −3.8k / +26k / −29k / +190k / +83k / +164k)**; Strategy 9 −₹31.7L vs −₹23.7L; Strategy 10 −₹14.3L vs −₹4.15L. Index signals beat futures signals on all six; whether that is (2) (the unadjusted roll steps) or the equal-weight AVWAP is **Unquantified** — measure with a back-adjusted futures series. Nothing is significant at t ≥ 2. |

### S46. Lab Options (standalone) rescan: first-fired strike across ATR2 / ATM / ITM1 / OTM1, one position per side — and a quiet contract can hold its side

Chosen in the build: (1) the scan uses the 5-minute index candle that has just completed and ATR(14) on 5-minute candles (earlier: ATR on the strategy timeframe, strike fixed once a day); (2) a SETUP counts for a watched contract when its entry candle closes inside the watch (from that scan's time to the next); (3) ties at the same minute go to the scan-list order; (4) the position exits on the engine's own exit on that option's chart (next CHoCH on the option) even after the contract stops being watched. |

To measure: count side-days blocked by one position per strategy / window; rerun with the managed exit (Strategies 9–10) or a time stop as a strategy-file value. |
| Fix sketch | The user decides whether a standalone position needs an exit that does not depend on the option's own CHoCH (a time stop, the managed exit, or an exit on the index's CHoCH). Nothing changes without the user (Rule 0(c)). |

### S43. Lab Strategies 9–10 (managed exits on Strategy 1 / 2 entries): the rule readings, and what the lock does to a 3-lot book

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | `research/strategy_lab/strategies/strategy_9.json` / `strategy_10.json` (`position` block), [`lab.py`](../research/strategy_lab/lab.py) `manage()`. Filed 2026-09-27. |
| Why | The user's spec: 3 lots, stop 50 pts (futures) / 5% of the premium (options), lot 1 out at 1:1, lot 2 at 1:2, at 1:3 the last lot trails; mirror for long. Readings confirmed with the user (2026-09-27): the trail is a **ladder** (at 3R the stop moves to 2R, then 1R behind each further full R reached); **no CHoCH exit** (lots stay open until stop / target / trail, across sessions, or the option's expiry; the backtest end values what is left, marked *); the stop for lots 2–3 stays at the initial stop until 3R. Chosen in the build, not by the user: (1) R for options = 5% of the **entry fill premium**; (2) on one candle the stop is checked before targets, and a trail move made on a candle applies from the next candle; (3) no fill on a session's opening print: a stop crossed there fills at that candle's close, a target only if the close is still beyond it; (4) `rules.sl_rule = none`, because with Strategy 1's previous-swing rule the engine refuses SETUPs whose swing is on the wrong side, and the position's own stop replaces it; (5) the strike lock applies to the futures contract too: while any lot of a futures position is open, new futures signals, including opposite ones, are not taken. |
| Impact | **First trial measured (Design window 26 Aug – 25 Sep, Strategy 10 = Strategy 2's entries, before the full-chain option data):** futures 45 lot exits, −₹83,316, 32 at the stop, 11 signals not taken (strike lock); options (via futures) W-ATR2 156 lot exits, −₹45,561, 105 at the stop. The 5% option stop is about 3.7 premium points on a ₹74 option; with 0.5-point slippage each side, a 1R lot nets well under 1R. **Unquantified:** each of the build choices (1)–(5) against its alternative, and how many signals the futures lock drops across windows. To measure: the same windows with `lock: "none"` (a copy of the file under another code), and with the stop % varied, read from `results/history`. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing changes without the user (Rule 0(c)). Candidates, each a strategy-file value: option stop as points or ATR instead of a flat 5%; stop to breakeven after 1R; `lock: "none"` for futures, or a lock per direction. |
| Effort | **S** per variant (a strategy file copy + one run). |
| Full results | **Measured 2026-09-27** (first full build, full-chain option data). Strategy 9 (1m) futures: All data 105 lot exits −₹20,574 PF 0.93, Design 42 / −₹77,966, Unseen 66 / −₹10,927; **172 signals not taken by the strike lock** (All data; exits: stop 58, 1R 21, 2R 14, trail 12). Options W-ATR2 All data: via futures 1,038 / −₹1,51,595, standalone 1,956 / −₹3,08,424. Strategy 10 (5m) futures: All data 114 / −₹2,01,847 PF 0.49, Design 45 / −₹83,316, Unseen 69 / −₹1,20,721; 35 signals not taken (exits: stop 79, 1R 14, 2R 11, trail 10). Via futures M-ATR2: All data 420 / −₹21,574 PF 0.92, Unseen 267 / +₹6,634 PF 1.05 (the only green headline). The lock (5) removes most 1m futures signals, so Strategy 9 futures is a different entry set from Strategy 1's, not the same entries with other exits. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-27; decide (5) (futures lock per contract vs per direction vs none) before reading Strategy 9 against Strategy 1._ |

### S44. FZ keeps one HUNT and one REJECT per band: the fade block and the defend branch miss an overwritten event; and a refused converted REENTER still counts as a REENTER

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`research/strategy_lab/fz.py`](../research/strategy_lab/fz.py): the edge-event block of the bar loop (`hunt_at` / `hunt_dir`, `reject_at` / `reject_dir` on the zone), `fade()`, `defend()`, and `gate()` where a branch-1 TAKE on the watched band becomes that watch's REENTER (`open_reenter(k, k, …)`). Filed 2026-09-27 by the independent FZ correctness review. |
| Why | **(a)** A band stores only its latest HUNT and its latest REJECT; a later event of the same kind overwrites the earlier one whatever its direction. `hunt_fade` asks whether the latest HUNT of the ref / left band is in the SETUP's direction within `fade_block_bars`; the defend branch asks whether the latest HUNT or the latest REJECT fell in this visit within `defend_window_bars` at the far edge. The rule text in the docstrings ("a HUNT in S's direction completed within fade_block_bars", "a HUNT or REJECT in this visit within defend_window_bars") reads as *any* such event. `FZ.md` §6 / §8 now describe the one-slot behaviour as built; which reading is the rule is the user's call. **(b)** When a branch-1 TAKE is converted into the watch's REENTER and the position callback refuses it (`wrong_side_stop`, `sl_dead_at_fill`), the row keeps `gate = REENTER` with `refused` set, no position opens, there is no fallback to Foundation's TAKE, and `outcome_gate` counts the row as a REENTER. |
| Impact | **Counts measured, trade impact Unquantified.** (a) A scratch copy of `fz.py` that also kept every event and compared both readings at each SETUP, decisions unchanged (All data, seeded files): ST5 1 SETUP where the any-event defend holds and the latest-slot defend does not (2026-09-03 14:43 down, ACCEPTED on `B2026-07-23 14:52`, gated WATCH `accepted_no_defend`; Foundation +7.1 pts on it), 0 fade rows; ST6 0 / 0; ST7 0 defend, 4 SETUPs an overwritten same-direction HUNT would have BLOCKed `hunt_fade` (07-09 13:38 WATCH, 07-17 14:00 WATCH, 08-14 10:39 WATCH, 08-14 10:59 BLOCK `new`); ST8 0 / 0. Each changed row also moves the positions and watches after it, which only a paired run measures. (b) 0 occurrences: no ledger row of ST5–ST8 on any window has `gate = REENTER` with `refused` set. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing changes without the user (Rule 0(c)). (a) Keep the one-slot reading (docs already match), or keep a per-band event list behind a strategy-file key (e.g. `edge_memory`: `latest` \| `window`) and run ST5–ST8 paired before / after on the same windows; ST5 / ST6 are the base, so a change there is a new code or an explicit base update. (b) Decide what a refused conversion becomes (refused and *not* counted as REENTER in `outcome_gate`; Foundation's TAKE; or WATCH), then fix `gate()` and re-run the parity and truncation tests. |
| Effort | **S** for (b); **M** for (a) (key, tests, paired runs). |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-27. Small on this tape (5 SETUPs over four strategies; (b) latent)._ |

### S32. Look-ahead audit of the Strategy 8 replay: clean — but the fill model is stale-by-one-tick and stops fill with zero slippage

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`MarketDataService.dropIncompleteBars`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/market/service/MarketDataService.java), [`PositionService.monitor`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/position/service/PositionService.java), [`BacktestingOrderPlacementService`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/backtesting/BacktestingOrderPlacementService.java). Filed 2026-09-06 after auditing the [S31](#s31-first-engine-replay-of-strategy8-2024-depth-2-ladder-2715-pts--770-trades--but-the-leg-set-is-not-the-replays) ledger for look-ahead. |
| Why | Asked directly: is Strategy 8 reading candles it could not have seen? Audited the whole path. **No look-ahead found**, on four guards: the fetch upper bound is the tick time (`AnalysisScheduler` line 286 passes `analysisDateTime`); `dropIncompleteBars` keeps a bar only when `ts + width <= asOf`, so a forming bar never reaches a rule; the provider aggregates *after* slicing, which its own javadoc says is what stops `interval - 5` minutes of leakage; and `PositionService` skips any quote not strictly after `entry_time`, so the entry bar cannot close its own trade. Intra-bar is adverse-first, and a rung armed by a bar's own excursion cannot fire on that bar. |
| Impact | **Measured on the 770-trade ledger.** 770/770 entry fills equal the close of the *completed* 15-minute bar (the T+10 five-minute close); 2 coincidentally equalled the next bar's close, 3 the bar-start close. Minimum hold is exactly 15 minutes (one bar), zero exits at or before entry, entries bounded 09:15-14:45 and every `FORCE_CLOSE` at 15:20. The one timing artefact runs **against** the strategy: the replay tick grid is 09:20 + 15k while 15-minute bars complete at 09:30 / 09:45 / ..., so each decision is taken at T+20 but filled at the bar close (price at T+15) — worth **-0.93 pts/trade, -713.4 pts over 2024** versus filling at the decision tick's price. The engine therefore understates this configuration by roughly that much. |
| Open question | **Unquantified: stops and trails fill exactly at the floor with no slippage.** 487 of 770 exits (63%) are `STOP_LOSS` or `TRAIL_SL` fills priced at the floor itself under the S4 resting-order model. That is the one convention in the run that flatters rather than penalises, and it is unmeasured. The measurement: re-price every floor exit at the adverse extreme of the bar that triggered it (or at a fixed points/bps haircut) and re-total — no engine change needed, it is a query over the existing ledger plus the candle rows. |
| Caveat | `entry_time` / `exit_time` are stamped with the **bar's** timestamp, not the decision tick, so a row reading 09:30 was actually decided at 09:50. This does not affect P&L but it does mean the ledger's own clock is 20 minutes ahead of when the trade could have been placed — relevant to anyone diffing this ledger against a replica keyed on time. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing to change without the user. If the slippage measurement proves material, the candidate is a config-driven slippage field applied at floor exits (CLAUDE.md #9 — it is a number that changes P&L, so it belongs in `TradeConfig`, not a constant). |
| Effort | **S** for the slippage re-pricing query. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-06._ |

---

### S31. First engine replay of `Strategy8` (2024, depth-2 ladder): +2,715 pts / 770 trades — but the leg set is not the replay's

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`Strategy8`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/Strategy8.java). Filed 2026-09-06 from the first full backtest run of the bean. Partially answers [S29](#s29-strategy8-is-the-intraday-form-of-the-20sma-15min-candle-rule--the-replays-edge-was-in-carrying-to-expiry)'s measurement request. |
| Why | S29 and S30 both asked for an engine replay of Strategy 8 to diff against the Python replica. One now exists. Run: 2024-01-01 -> 2024-12-31, `strategyIds=8`, `HISTORICAL_ICICI`, 498 hand-made configs (249 sessions x CE/PE), `transaction_type = SELL`, `itm_depth = otm_depth = 2`, one 15-minute SMA-20 `sma_timeframe` row, band 80-250, `sl_pct 0.30`, `max_sl_points 60`, `strategy_defaults` 8 (`target_mode NONE`, `trail_atr_multiple 2.00`). 6,225 ticks, 0 failures, 297s. Ledger cleared before the run. |
| Impact | **Measured, this configuration only.** 770 trades, **+2,715.1 pts**, +3.53/trade, 42.9% win rate, profit factor 1.202, max drawdown **-818** on the trade-sequenced equity curve. By exit: `FORCE_CLOSE` 283 @ +47.52 (+13,447), `TRAIL_SL` 315 @ -10.95 (-3,448), `STOP_LOSS` 172 @ -42.35 (-7,284) — **every point of the edge is the 15:15 close-signal exit; both stop paths are net negative.** By side: CE 407 (+1,915), PE 363 (+800). Monthly range -471 (Jul) to +633 (Nov), two losing months (Feb -24, Jul -471). Not comparable to S29's replica line (1,780 trades / +2.0 / +3,532 over **two** years on a **single ATM leg**): this scans a 4-leg ladder per side, so trade count per session and leg mix both differ. **2025 (Jan 1 → Mar 27, 60 of 61 sessions, 122 configs, same recipe):** 201 trades, +1,425.6 pts, +7.09/trade, 49.3% win, PF 1.491, max DD −199 — `FORCE_CLOSE` 87 @ +38.20 (+3,324), `TRAIL_SL` 78 @ −6.59, `STOP_LOSS` 36 @ −38.45. Twice 2024's per-trade figure, same anatomy: the close exit carries everything and both stop paths lose. Data caveats (missing 03-20 series, thin March book) are in S30. |
| Caveat | Depth 2 was chosen so [S30](#s30-indicator--price-action--volume-study-on-strategy-8-three-gates-transferred-the-rest-did-not--and-the-volume-gate-depends-on-leg-coverage)'s volume gate stays measurable on the same config set — S30's own caveat says a depth-0 config does not run the gate the study measured. That makes this run a valid A/B base for Strategy 9 but **not** the faithful reproduction of the replica [STRATEGIES.md](STRATEGIES.md) describes ("a hand-made config with a 15-minute row and depth 0"). Two further deviations: `instrument.strike_points` is 100 while the option data is on a 50-point grid, so the ladder lands on 100-multiples and ATM rounds down to the nearest 100, not the nearest 50; and the replica priced 1 pt/round-trip costs, which this ledger does not deduct. |
| Open question | **Unquantified: does the depth-2 ladder change the rule's edge, or only its trade count?** The measurement is the same window re-run on depth-0 configs (one ATM leg per side) with a cleared ledger, comparing pts/trade and PF rather than totals. Until that exists, +3.53/trade cannot be read as either agreeing or disagreeing with the replica's +2.0. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing to change. Two follow-ups, neither started: (a) the depth-0 arm above; (b) ~~the Strategy 9 arm on this identical config set~~ — run the same day once the JVM was restarted; numbers recorded in [S30](#s30-indicator--price-action--volume-study-on-strategy-8-three-gates-transferred-the-rest-did-not--and-the-volume-gate-depends-on-leg-coverage). |
| Effort | **S** for each follow-up run — the config set already exists. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-06._ |

|---|---|

### S30. Indicator / price-action / volume study on Strategy 8: three gates transferred, the rest did not — and the volume gate depends on leg coverage

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`Strategy9`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/Strategy9.java) (changeset 049). Filed 2026-09-06 with the strategy. |
| Why | 74 features on every 15-minute bar (RSI 7/14, ADX/DI 15-min and daily, Supertrend, EMA 9/21, SMA slopes, 60-min slope, Choppiness, efficiency ratio, Bollinger width/%B, day range/ATR, prev-day range/body, consecutive closes, candle close position, opening range, position in the day range, gap, TWAP, near-ATM option volume vs trailing median, put/call volume and OI ratios, OI build by side, ATM straddle %, straddle-implied vol weekly and ~30-day, hour, DTE, prev-day ADX/DI regime) were conditioned on Strategy 8's replay trades; ~420 allow-rules per base were scored, the best per feature re-run in the Python engine, plus all pairs of the top-10 and triples of the top-6 (~200 engine runs) and a walk-forward (rule chosen on 2024 tested on 2025 and the reverse). **Measured (hold-to-expiry base +6.0/trade, +6,366):** near-ATM volume spike > 2× → +0.5/trade on a third of entries, gate +458 pts, DD −423 → −336, both years; candle closing on its low → +2.9 vs +9.7–19, gate +236 pts; DTE 0–1 → +0.5 vs +10–14, gate −276 pts but PF 1.43 → 1.60. Regime (fade the prev-day trend +12.3, follow it +2.9) did not survive the engine — skipping with-trend days lost 826 pts because the freed slots refill. RSI, Supertrend, EMA, ADX, 60-min slope, Bollinger width, Choppiness, OI build: no net gain. India VIX was not used (nseindia.com and niftyindices.com refuse the sandbox); a straddle-implied ~30-day vol stood in and showed no usable gate either. |
| Caveat | The gate the bean implements sums volume over the **cached legs** within ±200 of the session-open ATM. The replica summed every 50-point strike in that window; the leg-level version (the traded option alone against its own history) was tried and **hurt** (hold +3,676 vs +6,366) because a strike's own history is dominated by the days it was not near ATM. A depth-0 config therefore does not run the gate the study measured. Walk-forward: rules chosen on one year improved the other year's average in 5 of 6 tests but lowered total points in 5 of 6 — the gates are quality filters. |
| Impact | **Measured 2026-09-06 — engine cross-run over 2024 on the [S31](#s31-first-engine-replay-of-strategy8-2024-depth-2-ladder-2715-pts--770-trades--but-the-leg-set-is-not-the-replays) config set** (`strategyIds=9&configStrategyId=8`, depth 2, seeds 2.00 / 0.250 / 2, 6,225 ticks, 0 failures, 191 s). Strategy 9: **298 trades, +1,821.4 pts, +6.11/trade, 52.7% win, PF 1.468, max DD −238**, against Strategy 8 on the identical configs: 770 / +2,715.1 / +3.53 / 42.9% / 1.202 / −818. Same shape as the replica (602 vs 1,780 trades, +4.8 vs +2.0, PF 1.38 vs 1.16, DD −265 vs −727): the gates give up a third of the total points for per-trade quality and cut the drawdown by two thirds. Exits: `FORCE_CLOSE` 162 @ +30.22, `TRAIL_SL` 98 @ −13.60, `STOP_LOSS` 38 @ −45.82. **Not a filtered subset of the Strategy 8 ledger:** only 119 of the 298 are exact twins (same config, leg, entry bar) of a Strategy 8 trade; the other 179 are entries Strategy 8 never took because its one slot was already occupied — the gates free slots as much as they filter, so read the two ledgers as different trade sequences on the same signals. On the 119 shared entries Strategy 8 averaged +8.81, against +2.56 on the 651 it took that Strategy 9 refused. Gate check from the ledger: all 298 entries have DTE ≥ 2 (min 2) and signal-candle close position ≥ 0.25 (min 0.251), where Strategy 8's 770 fail 318 and 411 of the same tests — those two gates held on every entry; the volume gate cannot be re-derived from the ledger and is verified only by the `[tick]` DEBUG `legs=` line. **2025 (Jan 1 → Mar 27, 60 of 61 sessions, 122 configs on the same recipe):** Strategy 9 **70 trades, +837.1 pts, +11.96/trade, 58.6% win, PF 2.080, max DD −157** against Strategy 8 on the same configs: 201 / +1,425.6 / +7.09 / 49.3% / 1.491 / −199. Same direction as 2024 with a wider per-trade gap; exits `FORCE_CLOSE` 50 @ +27.02, `TRAIL_SL` 13 @ −12.25, `STOP_LOSS` 7 @ −50.63. Again not nested: 20 of the 70 share an entry with Strategy 8, 50 are slot-freed. Gates held on all 70 (0 DTE / 0 candle violations; Strategy 8's 201 fail 85 / 117). **Two data caveats on 2025:** the import ends 2025-03-27 and its option universe thins from 6 expiries in late Feb to **one** from 03-21 (only contracts expiring ≤ 03-27 were imported), so the last week trades a thin book; and **2025-03-20 could not replay** — the run aborted on `HIST:NIFTY:NFO:2025-03-20:23300:CE`, a series with no rows in the import, so that session was skipped and 03-21 → 03-27 run separately. Still open: the trade-by-trade diff against the replica's LG sheets. |
| Fix sketch | If the engine ledger reproduces the replica, consider the V2 (spot-signal) form — `day move already in favour` was the one gate that added points there (+856) — as its own strategy on the underlying series; not built. |
| Effort | **M** for the measurement. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-06._ |

### S29. `Strategy8` is the intraday form of the "20SMA 15min candle" rule — the replay's edge was in carrying to expiry

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`Strategy8`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/Strategy8.java), [`RuleEngine.decideWithoutCrossGate`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/rules/RuleEngine.java), the chandelier trail in [`PositionService`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/position/service/PositionService.java) (changeset 048). Filed 2026-09-06 with the strategy. |
| Why | The rule (15-min SMA-20 slope down + close below previous close, sell the ATM option, chandelier 2 × ATR-14 stop, no target) was replayed on the dbeaver export Jan-2024 → Dec-2025 in Python before it was coded here. **Measured:** intraday (square-off 15:15) 1,780 trades, +2.0 pts/trade, +3,532 pts, PF 1.16, 20/24 months positive; held to the weekly expiry 1,060 trades, +6.0/trade, +6,366 pts, PF 1.43, max DD −423, 22/24 months positive. The always-in baseline with the same exit and no signal made +5,518 pts held to expiry (PF 1.19, DD −1,008): the entry rule adds ~850 pts and halves the trade count and drawdown; the profit is theta. The bean implements the intraday form because every ledger invariant (15:15 close signal, 15:20 force-close, per-day caps and loss cap, backtest day loop) assumes flat overnight. |
| Differences from the replica | Ledger fills a trailed stop at the floor even when the bar gapped through it (replica: at the open); `max_sl_points = 60` on the standing rule caps the first stop below 30% on premiums above 200 (replica: pure 30%); the replica traded the ATM strike on every bar while a tagged `AUTO_DOWNTREND` config offers the detector's legs and only when it wrote a 15-minute row; ATR is a simple mean, not Wilder, in both. Costs: replica 1 pt/round trip; here `charge_rate`. |
| Impact | **Partially measured 2026-09-06 — see [S31](#s31-first-engine-replay-of-strategy8-2024-depth-2-ladder-2715-pts--770-trades--but-the-leg-set-is-not-the-replays)** (2024, depth-2 ladder: 770 trades, +3.53/trade, +2,715 pts, PF 1.202, DD -818; the whole edge is the 15:15 close exit). That run is not the depth-0 single-ATM-leg setup this entry asks for, so the trade-by-trade diff below is still open. The Python numbers are not an engine replay. Measure by running a `8`-tagged month (or a hand-made 15-minute depth-0 config) through the backtest and diffing the ledger against the replica's `LG V1 chand 2xATR intraday` sheet trade by trade, as was done for strategies 6/7. |
| Fix sketch | Hold-to-expiry needs: (a) `Strategy8.buyRulesFor` to fire the close-time BUY only on the leg's expiry day; (b) `OrderService.forceCloseOpenPositions` to skip strategy-8 orders on non-expiry days; (c) the backtest day loop and `BacktestingPositionMonitorService` cache to keep quoting an order opened on an earlier day; (d) per-day caps to count carried positions as open; (e) overnight margin. Behind a `strategy_defaults.carry_to_expiry` flag so nothing changes for 1–7. Do not build it without the paired before/after replay (Rule 0(c)). |
| Effort | **M** for the measurement. **L** for the carry-over. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-06. Measure first: if the engine's intraday ledger does not reproduce the replica's +2.0/trade, the carry-over is moot._ |

### S26. Pressure's trigger fires close to, but not identically with, the reference implementation

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`PressureScore`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/pressure/PressureScore.java) + [`SpotFeatures`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/indicator/series/SpotFeatures.java). Filed 2026-09-05 from a user cross-check of a local run against the reference ("dbeaver Pressure") over **1–23 Jan 2024**. |
| Why | The user compared spot entries one for one. **Of 78 local spot entries, 65 had a reference entry within 15 minutes and 63 of those matched direction** — the user's own reading was "the same strategy with a slightly different trigger". The clearest divergence is the very first ticket of the window: the local build **SELL at 09:35 on 1 Jan** (a −50 loser) against the reference **BUY at 09:40**, an opposite-direction call on the first bars of the run. From 2 Jan 09:55 onward the two mostly lock, including an identical 21636.80 SELL. That pattern — disagreement concentrated at the very start, agreement thereafter — points at **indicator warm-up** rather than at the scoring rules: the first replayed day has the least history behind RSI, ATR/Supertrend and ADX, and `Supertrend.direction` additionally seeds `UP` at its first computable bar, so an early bar can be scored off a direction that is a seed rather than a measurement. [S22](#s22-pressures-session-vwap-is-weighted-by-option-volume-because-nifty-spot-has-none) (the option-volume VWAP proxy) is the other candidate and the two are not separable without measurement. |
| Caveat on the source data | **The compared run came from a stale JVM** carrying the strike-collapse defect (three books trading one leg) and no per-day VWAP weights cache. That defect is in leg *selection*, not in scoring, so the **spot** entry timestamps the user matched on should be unaffected — but this entry should be re-derived against a clean run before any conclusion is drawn from it. The 78-vs-87 spot ticket count is itself part of what needs re-measuring. |
| Impact | **Unquantified.** 63 of 78 directionally matched inside 15 minutes is agreement, not equivalence, and the residual is where the P&L difference lives (user's slice: local spot +6.5 mean pts / 65.4% WR against reference +8.5 / 71.3%). The measurement: on a clean run over the same window, join local spot entries to reference entries on nearest timestamp, and bucket the unmatched ones by which score term differed. That names the cause instead of guessing between warm-up and VWAP. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing to change yet, and **nothing to change without the user** — this is the entry trigger. If warm-up proves to be the cause the candidate is to require a minimum number of warm bars before Strategy 5 will score at all (today it scores as soon as each indicator is individually non-NA, and `Supertrend` reports a seeded direction rather than `UNKNOWN` on its first computable bar). That is a real behaviour change: it suppresses early-session entries, so it needs a paired before/after on the same window per working rule 3. Widening the warm-up window is the cheaper half and changes no rule. |
| Effort | **S** to re-measure on a clean run. **M** if a warm-bar minimum is introduced, since that is a new `TradeConfig` field under Rule 9 plus a paired run. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-05. Worth re-deriving as soon as a clean full-year run exists; the January slice is the natural window because a reference comparison already exists for it._ |

### S21. `Strategy6`'s three gates are replay-selected — the constants are strategy identity, not config

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`Strategy6`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/Strategy6.java) (built 2026-09-05 on the user's request: *"Build this as strategy 6"*), [`CommonRules.higherTimeframeSmaDownTrending`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/rules/CommonRules.java) / `isAtOrBeforeEntryCutoff`, the stop-loss lock gate 6 in [`OrderService.handleSignal`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/order/service/OrderService.java), and the confirmation-timeframe fetch in [`AnalysisScheduler`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/scheduler/AnalysisScheduler.java). |
| Why | The gates come from a Python replica of this pipeline (detector + strategies 1/2 + `OrderService` caps + the current `PositionService` bracket, entries verified bar-for-bar against `analysis/s6-measurement/run-B-ledger.csv`) replayed over the dbviewer NIFTY 5-minute series, Dec-2023 → Dec-2025, `max_deviation = 5`, the standing AUTO_DOWNTREND numbers, lot 75, changeset-029 charge rates. Baseline: strategy 1 1,581 trades, +695 pts/share, PF 1.03, ≈ ₹+5k after charges; strategy 2 963 trades, +102 pts, PF 1.01, ≈ ₹−22k. What lost: re-entries after a stop in the same book (343 trades, −583 pts), entries after 15:00 (86 trades, −304 pts, 29% win), and signals while the leg's 15-minute SMA-50 was not in a whole-day down-trend (725 trades, −925 pts vs +1,620 when it was). Replayed end-to-end (slot and cap effects included), strategy 2 + the three gates with the 15-minute rule reading *unknown ⇒ allow*: **561 trades, +2,021 pts, PF 1.28, 67% win, ≈ ₹+134k**, 2024 +907 / 2025 +949, positive in every half-year, max drawdown ≈ ₹28k. Strategy 1 + the same gates: 769 trades, +1,826 pts, PF 1.17. Gating the 15-minute rule as *unknown ⇒ block* (which removes the 09:15–09:25 entries, the replay's best slice at +1,143 pts over 199 trades) cost ≈ 200 pts. The 20-SMA slope filter alone is noise — strategy 1's slope-up entries (840) and slope-not-up entries (741) average the same — but on top of the other three it lifted PF 1.17 → 1.28 and smoothed the curve, which is why 6 extends 2 rather than 1. Full variant grid and ledgers are in the user's `money_maker_backtest_analysis.xlsx` (2026-09-05). |
| Impact | **Measured in replay only, and in-sample:** roughly thirty variants were tried on the same two years and these are the survivors, so the live numbers will be smaller. The rank-on-2024 / read-2025 check held for the three gates (2024 +1,067 → 2025 +693 for strategy 1 + gates) and failed for the runner-ups (no-re-entry alone +1,461 → +119; skip-Fridays +1,050 → −38). 2025H2 was the weakest half for every variant. Two further caveats on the money figures: the charge rates are the unverified changeset-029 seeds, and lot 75 is applied throughout although NIFTY traded 25-lots for most of 2024. |
| Open questions | **(a) Constants vs columns.** The 15-minute width, SMA-50 and the 30-minute cut-off are constants on the bean, and the stop-loss lock is a bean declaration, not `TradeConfig` columns — CLAUDE.md #9 says tunables belong in config, and #9 also says to ask before hardcoding, which this build did by being asked to build exactly this. Promote them the day a second strategy wants different values (three columns + `TradeConfigFormDTO` / `TradeConfigViewDTO` / both directions of `TradeConfigAdminService` / the combined query, per the four-places note in ORDERS_AND_POSITIONS.md). **(b) The replica's 15-minute series was always present; here it is fetched from the 15-minute underlying bar's ATM window,** so at a tick where the 15-minute and 5-minute ATM differ a leg can lack its confirmation series and read *unknown*. Rare (the two closes differ by less than a strike almost always) and it errs toward allowing — measure with a replay whose `[tick]` log counts `htf15Sma50DownOrUnknown` unknowns versus verdicts. **(c) Live parity of the confirmation fetch:** under `BROKER` the extra 15-minute request per leg per tick is one more rate-limited call; `BacktestMarketDataCache` makes it free in replay. Watch `kiteHistorical` throttling on the first live day with a 6-tagged config. **(d) The 09:15-bar edge** (+1,143 pts over 199 trades in the baseline) is the largest single slice in the replay and is not a gate here — it rides on *unknown ⇒ allow*. Whether it deserves its own strategy (opening-bar entries only: 220 trades, PF 1.37) is unmeasured out of sample. |
| Measured after the build (2026-09-05, same replica) | **Config knobs on top of 6 all lose:** `no_of_trades = 2` → +1,882 pts (from +2,021), 5-minute rules only → +1,071, SMA-200 dropped → +1,889, `min_option_price = 100` → +1,567, all four → +740. The gates already remove what those knobs remove. **Bracket:** `target_pct`/`sl_pct` 0.25/0.25 → +1,856; `max_sl_points` 45 → +1,584. **Close signal 15:00** → +2,069 (a wash). **Detector `max_deviation`** 0 / 2 / 5 → PF 1.29 / 1.30 / 1.28 — robust to the config-generation setting. Two small gates helped and were **not** adopted: blocking CE sells while spot RSI-14 < 30 (+2,207, PF 1.31, but it is 12 trades over two years) and skipping Fridays (+1,786 with a smaller drawdown but 2025 halves). Strategy 1 with the same three gates and no slope filter: +1,826 / PF 1.17, and +1,756 / PF 1.18 with `no_of_trades = 2` — a slope-less variant is not worth a class. Changeset 046 therefore seeds strategy 6's `strategy_defaults` as a copy of strategy 1's block so nothing is left to be typed differently. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing to fix in the build. Per working rule 3, before changing any of the three gates: tag one window's configs `2,6`, replay, and diff the two ledgers row by row — same detection by construction, so every difference is one of the gates, and the `[tick]` log names which. |
| Effort | **S** for the paired replay; **M** to promote the constants to columns (four places plus the query contract test). |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-05 at build time. Not a defect; a record of what was measured and what was not._ |

### S22. `Strategy7`'s first-hour gate is the one regime signal that transferred — gap rule and regime model did not

| | |
|---|---|
| Where | [`Strategy7`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/Strategy7.java) (built 2026-09-05 on the user's request: *"Create this as strategy 7"*), [`CommonRules.firstHourMoveInFavourAtr`](../src/main/java/com/moneymaker/strategy/rules/CommonRules.java) / `sessionAtr`, changeset 047. |
| Why | The user's regime thread ("Nifty trading indicator analysis") built a 3-class sideways/rally/decline model (AUC 0.65 at 10:15, 0.58 at 09:20), found the gap rule (\|open − prev close\| ≤ 0.5 ATR) to be its one robust sideways identifier, and used the 09:15 ATM straddle as the expected move. Each was tested here against the Strategy 1 and Strategy 6 ledgers, then the promising ones as real gates. **Diagnosis first:** by realised day type, Strategy 6 makes +1,866 pts on sideways days (367 trades, PF 1.45), +2,036 on trends in the leg's favour (110, PF 3.93) and loses −1,881 on trends against (84, PF 0.23) — every loss is a trend-against day, so the only useful regime signal is one that flags those in time. **First-hour direction** (entries after 10:15; skip when the first hour moved against the leg by > 0.2 × expected move): Strategy 6 561 → 518 trades, +2,021 → +2,225 pts, PF 1.28 → 1.34, all half-years positive; the 43 removed trades were trend-against days 35% vs 14% base. Robust: thresholds −0.1/−0.2/−0.3 → +2,186/+2,225/+2,046; ATR-normalised −0.1/−0.2/−0.3 → +2,239/+2,125/+2,114. Built as ATR-based −0.2 (no straddle fetch needed): **525 trades, +2,125, PF 1.31, ≈ ₹143k**, 2024 +814 / 2025 +1,204, halves +545/+269/+815/+389, max DD ≈ ₹29.5k. Strategy 1 with the gate alone: +695 → +1,401. **Gap rule** (skip \|gap\| > 0.5 ATR days): +2,086 but 2025H2 flat — half of this strategy's trend days are the favourable, gap-and-go kind, so the rule throws away 09:15 winners with the losers. **Yesterday's type** (close within the day's range, body ≥ 0.3): PF 1.28 → 1.37 but 1,664 pts on 374 trades — a variance trade-off, not adopted. **Expected-move / IV level, range-so-far, position in the day's range:** no monotonic signal (IV 1.2–1.5× ATR loses in both ledgers but the buckets either side win). **The regime model itself:** not ported — the source thread found it weak for premium selling (its "sideways" is really "low IV"), and this strategy's edge is the option's own premium trend, which the model does not see. |
| Impact | In-sample on 2024–25 like S21; the gate's contribution is +5–10% and the threshold plateau is the evidence it is not a fit. Definition parity with the replay: move = close of the last bar before 10:15 − open of the 09:15 bar; ATR-14 = mean true range of the 14 completed sessions before the day, rolled up from the cached intraday series. The replay's ATR came from daily bars including the 15:30 print, which the intraday roll-up may lack under `BROKER` if the provider drops it — a few points of range, immaterial at a 0.2-ATR threshold. |
| Open questions | **(a)** The checkpoint (10:15) and threshold (0.20 ATR) are strategy constants — S21 (a) applies verbatim. **(b)** Under `BROKER`, does the cached underlying series carry enough sessions for the ATR (the 35-day lookback gives ~24; the rule needs 5) on the first live day after a restart? Unknown ⇒ allow covers the failure mode, but count `firstHourNotAgainstOrUnknown` unknowns in the `[tick]` log on day one. **(c)** The gate reads the underlying at the *signal's* interval; for a 15-minute signal the 09:15 bucket's open is the session open, as required — verify once against the 5-minute series in a replay diff of a `6,7`-tagged window. |
| Fix sketch | Nothing to fix. Per working rule 3: tag one window's configs `6,7`, replay, diff the two ledgers — same detection and same five other rules by construction, so every difference is this gate. |
| Effort | **S**. |
| Priority | _Open — filed 2026-09-05 at build time._ |
